#CH 202
#TITLE: A Smooth Property Transfer
After Qianli Xun finished serving tea to the elders and withdrew, the older generation chatted a while longer before going their separate ways.
Before long, Butler Wang came to report to Chen Jianglin. “Master, a tricky matter has come up, and I need instructions from you and Madam.”
Chen Jianglin had just set out the Go stones, not yet starting his match against Yao Qingyan. “What is it?”
Butler Wang gave it to him straight. “Young Madam wants to dismiss every single maid in Second Young Master’s courtyard and replace them with her own people. Yesterday I couldn’t well turn her down to her face—she only just married into the family. Thinking it over afterward, I realized I had to ask Master and Madam first.”
Yao Qingyan plucked a stone and set it at random on the board. “Those maids in Haoyu’s courtyard have been an eyesore to me for ages—who even knows where he dug them up? I’ve wanted him to send them packing for a long time. Better to take this chance and clear them all out.”
Chen Jianglin placed a stone of his own. “My wife is right. Since Shuzhen wants it done, do as she says.”
Yao Qingyan nodded and asked the butler, “How did Haoyu take it?”
“Second Young Master was none too pleased, of course,” Butler Wang replied, “yet he didn’t seem able to do a thing about Young Madam, so he agreed in the end.”
Chen Jianglin laughed. “Looks like we picked the right daughter-in-law after all. Just follow Shuzhen’s orders, Butler Wang.”
Butler Wang assented, then hesitated. “Master, Madam, there is one more awkward problem to report.”
Chen Jianglin spoke without lifting his eyes from the board. “Go on.”
“Half the maids from Second Young Master’s quarters refuse to leave Chen Manor and will have to be reassigned to other courtyards. I wasn’t sure where to put them. On my way over I ran into Second Master and Third Master and mentioned it in passing, and both were eager to take them. Then I ran into Second Madam and Third Madam, who flat-out refused, saying the maids from Second Young Master’s courtyard look far too seductive and can’t be kept.”
Yao Qingyan paused with a stone between her fingers, then snapped it onto the board. “If you ask me, dismiss the whole lot of them before they stir up trouble in the other courtyards.”
Chen Jianglin agreed without hesitation. “Dismiss them all.”
“Yes, Master.” Butler Wang bowed and withdrew.
Once the butler left, the couple went back to their game. Winning or losing mattered little; what mattered was each other’s company.
Noticing Chen Jianglin letting her have the upper hand again, Yao Qingyan put on a pout. “You let me win every time we play. How boring.”
“Not at all,” he said. “I find watching my wife play delightful—most of all when you win and your whole face lights up.”
Yao Qingyan’s thoughts turned back to their son. “Earlier, we thought about dismissing Haoyu’s maids ourselves, only to drop the idea for fear the Old Madam would step in to spoil her grandson. If the Old Madam finds out Shuzhen cleared out every last maid in his courtyard, do you think she’ll make a fuss?”
“Wait and see,” Chen Jianglin said. “We’ll take it one step at a time.”
They played on until Yao Qingyan took the match, then strolled out into the garden.
Chen Manor’s garden boasted rows of prized flowers and shrubs, bursting with fresh spring vigor. Once their feet grew tired, the two rested inside a pavilion.
They had barely sat down when Steward Fang, who kept the accounts for the Chen businesses, hurried up to report. “Master, first thing this morning, Young Madam’s Matron Wei came over demanding that every property under Second Young Master’s name be transferred into Young Madam’s name. This is too big for me to decide on my own, so I told Matron Wei I had to ask Master before making any move.”
Transferring Chen family assets was a far cry from dismissing a handful of maids. Unable to decide on the spot, Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan knew they needed the full story before weighing their answer. They told Steward Fang to hold off for now.
Their mood for strolling gone, Yao Qingyan sent her personal maid Yingxue to summon Matron Wei to her quarters for a talk.
The couple had no sooner returned to their room and taken their seats than Yingxue stepped in to report. “Master, Madam, Matron Wei has arrived and is waiting outside.”
“Invite Matron Wei in at once,” Yao Qingyan said.
Yingxue stepped back out to bring her in. The moment Matron Wei crossed the threshold, Yao Qingyan rose to greet her and took her by the hand. “Now that you’re living in Chen Manor, Matron, you must drop by my quarters often.”
Even with her hand clasped, Matron Wei did not skip proper etiquette. “My greetings to Master and Madam.”
“No need for ceremony, Matron Wei,” Chen Jianglin said. “Please take the upper seat.”
Matron Wei declined with polite poise. “I am a servant—how could I ever take a seat of honor?”
“Don’t talk like that,” Yao Qingyan insisted. “We’ve heard all about your standing in the Zhu household. What noble young lady in Lin’an doesn’t envy Miss Zhu for having someone as capable as you by her side?”
Yao Qingyan tried to draw her toward the upper seat, yet Matron Wei stood firm and chose a lower chair instead. Yao Qingyan stopped pressing.
Once seated, Matron Wei spoke with modesty. “Everyone flatters me with an empty reputation. All I ask is to stay by my young lady’s side and handle the mundane chores so she has time to compose poems and ci lyrics without wasting the talent Heaven gave her.”
Chen Jianglin smiled. “All of Jiangnan knows Youqi Jushi as a renowned poetess, yet few realize half that fame belongs to our daughter-in-law’s gift and the other half to Matron Wei.”
Matron Wei chuckled. “Now that is something I love to hear, Master. I’m a plain woman who can’t write a line of verse, yet I love watching my miss write. Every time people praise her poetry, I feel as proud as if I had written it myself.”
The three shared a warm laugh, the air in the room light and cordial.
Matron Wei guessed her hosts had called her in over Young Master’s properties. Since they felt awkward broaching it first, she cut straight to the point. “Master and Madam must have something important to discuss with me. If I’m not mistaken, it concerns transferring Young Master’s assets to my young lady?”
“A sharp woman indeed,” Chen Jianglin praised. “Yes, that’s it—not that it’s any great crisis. It just came out of the blue, and Qingyan and I don’t know the full background, so we hoped to hear the ins and outs from you.”
Matron Wei pulled a folded sheet of paper from her sleeve and opened it. Yingxue took it and passed it to Madam.
Yao Qingyan read it through, let out a sigh, and handed it to Chen Jianglin.
Chen Jianglin read every word, his brows knitting tight, and sighed too. “So Miss Zhu only agreed to marry into the Chen family because of what’s written on this sheet.”
“Don’t feel disheartened, Master and Madam,” Matron Wei said. “Marriages are arranged by Heaven. Whatever the reason, my miss has married into the Chen household, and that makes her one of the Chens.”
Yao Qingyan nodded and asked, “Did the two of them consummate the marriage last night?”
“Both my miss and Young Master opposed this match at first,” Matron Wei answered, “yet Heaven brought them together anyway, and here they are, wed. Don’t rush them, Master and Madam. Give the two of them time. We can’t foresee everything that lies ahead, yet I’m certain it will all turn out for the good.”
Her answer made it clear they had not consummated the marriage.
Even so, Matron Wei’s words smoothed the crease between Chen Jianglin’s brows. “Now that Shuzhen has married in, consummation is only a matter of time—no sense rushing it. As for this contract, Haoyu drew it up with our daughter-in-law before the wedding; as elders, we can’t help him break his own word. Besides, whether Haoyu’s assets sit under his name or hers, they remain Chen family property. The fertile water isn’t flowing into an outsider’s field.”
Glancing down at the contract once more, Chen Jianglin broke into a grin. “There’s one clause here I like very much: our daughter-in-law only agreed to give Haoyu twenty taels of silver a month for spending money. That Haoyu was willing to sign his name and press his thumbprint for a mere twenty taels—now that is rare.”
He handed the paper back to Yingxue, who returned it to Matron Wei to tuck away.
“Well said, Master—no fertile water flows to outsiders,” Matron Wei replied. “Once my miss married in, she became a Chen, so transferring Young Master’s assets to her keeps them right inside the family. Besides, my miss isn’t greedy for wealth. She only used those properties as a pretext to stop fighting fate and step into this marriage with an easier mind. Master hit the bull’s-eye just now: my miss is Young Master’s nemesis in the best way. Whatever brought her into this house, she will only bring the Chens good fortune.”
Her words struck straight to the couple’s hearts.
“How sharp you are, Matron—you saw right through the reason Jianglin and I agreed to this match,” Yao Qingyan said, smiling at last. She turned to her husband. “In that case, there’s nothing left to hesitate over.”
Chen Jianglin nodded. “Go straight to Xinglong Pavilion and have Steward Fang handle the transfer, Matron. I’ll send someone over to pass the word along.”
Xinglong Pavilion was the very hall where the wedding had taken place, now back to business as usual.
Built right inside Chen Manor to let the family manage its ventures and confer over major deals, the pavilion housed a combined staff of more than two hundred stewards and clerks. All two hundred lived, ate, and worked along the west wing of the manor, while the Chen family mostly lived on the north and east sides.
The manor’s south-facing main gate served the Chen family, whereas a separate west gate served the stewards and clerks. Even living inside the same compound, the two groups crossed paths very seldom.
The ground floor of Xinglong Pavilion was cavernous—the wedding banquet had filled it the day before. Though Matron Wei had already visited that morning, stepping back inside still made her look all around.
Gone was the sea of festive red from the wedding; in its place hung an atmosphere of brisk, solemn industry. The overhead lanterns stayed lit even in broad daylight because the hall was too vast for sunlight to reach every corner.
Matron Wei walked down the central aisle flanked by busy desks divided into neat sections. In one area clerks bent over sheets of paper writing; in another clerks hurried back and forth delivering ledgers; in a third they sorted and bound stacks of documents into volumes.
She headed straight for the innermost section, where the crisp clack of abacuses grew louder with every step. Several clerks sat behind abacuses there, checking account books while nimble fingers flicked the wooden beads. Steward Fang sat at the main desk—no abacus in front of him, just a ledger he was studying with focus.
Matron Wei stepped up with a greeting. “Steward Fang, I’m back again.”
Steward Fang set down the ledger and rose with a jovial smile. “Master just sent word over. I’ll take care of it for you right away.”
Matron Wei put on a look of mock astonishment. “I came straight here from Master and Madam’s courtyard without stopping once and never saw a messenger. Don’t tell me you and Master can send messages through thin air?”
“Nothing magical about it,” he laughed. “You must have missed him as he passed. Master’s runners are fleet-footed—he walked out the moment before you stepped in.” Steward Fang pulled up a chair himself. “Please, sit.”
Matron Wei took the seat without standing on ceremony. “Now that Master and Madam have approved it, get it done quick, Steward Fang. I’ve got plenty of other things waiting on me.”
Steward Fang sat down and brought out a volume labeled “Property Register,” though instead of opening it right away, he struck up a chat. “Young Madam is truly blessed to marry Second Young Master. You may not know this, Matron, but Song law requires a family’s scattered properties to be registered under a single household head, so all Chen assets stand under Eldest Master’s name. Only because the Old Madam dotes on Second Young Master did she pull strings with the Prefect to split several properties off into his name. Who would’ve thought Second Young Master dotes on Young Madam so much he’d hand every last one over? Remarkable.”
Matron Wei knew about the single-household registration rule, though she had not thought that far into it. Hearing Steward Fang explain it made her think even higher of the Chens—no wonder their business had grown so massive. “Tell me, Steward Fang, is it much trouble to transfer Young Master’s properties into my miss’s name?”
Steward Fang opened the register. “No trouble at all. First I log it here, stamp it with the main seal of the Chen firm plus the personal seals of Eldest Master and Second Young Master, and then take it to the local yamen to register.”
“Much obliged, Steward Fang.”
He dipped his brush, wrote across the ledger for a while, and set the brush down. “All logged. In a moment I’ll head up to the second floor to the seal-keeper and get the three seals stamped—that finishes the first step.”
Only then did Matron Wei realize Xinglong Pavilion had more than one story. Glancing around, she spotted a wooden staircase whose steps were too numerous to count at a glance.
“One more thing I need to clarify with you, Matron.”
She turned back. “Ask away, Steward Fang.”
“All the properties under Second Young Master’s name have been run by people Eldest Master appointed. Now that they belong to Young Madam, will you put your own people in charge, or keep the current managers? Don’t take offense—I have to ask so I can report back to Master.”
Getting Chen family assets signed over to her miss was already a windfall, Matron Wei reflected; why quibble over who managed them? Leaving Master’s people in place would reassure him the wealth stayed inside the family and spare her miss any future friction. Besides, Matron Wei already had her hands full managing her miss’s own dowry and estates.
“Keep the same managers,” she said. “I’ll drop by on my miss’s behalf when I have time, and skip it when I don’t.”
Steward Fang beamed. “That works out best of all.”
#CH 273
#TITLE: Outwitting Granduncle Lu at the Gate
Chen Lu refused to believe a word of it. “Haoyu, I know you dote on your wife, yet you needn’t take the blame for everything onto yourself. Never mind last year’s mess—just yesterday people were handing out free pamphlets on the street! My servant brought one home, and every page was packed with abuse aimed at her. Of all things a woman could do, why must she sit around writing poetry and ci lyrics, putting on pretentious airs and stirring up scandals? How indecent!”
Chen Haodong knew full well Haoyu had started the old rumors himself, yet he held his tongue and stood back to enjoy the show.
When Granduncle Lu mentioned the pamphlets, Haodong guessed Princess Consort Yi’s people were behind it. A woman ruthless enough to want Zhu Shuzhen dead would stop at nothing. Delight bubbled inside him, and the warning from his passing acquaintance Brother Yan flew straight out of his mind.
Chen Haoyu bristled. “What pamphlets? Why are they slandering my wife?”
Unwilling to draw any more eyes to the pamphlets, Qianli Xun cut in. “Ignore them. Just someone with an ax to grind trying to smear me.”
“No way! I won’t let anyone trash my wife’s reputation again—I’m going straight to report this to the authorities!” Turning on his heel, Chen Haoyu made to head for the Judicial Office.
His sister Chen Wenxin’s voice rang out. “No need to report it! Someone has already stepped in and handled it!”
Zhu Xiyan had caught wind of the pamphlets and sent Chen Wenxin over to warn his daughter to quit writing poetry. Arriving right in the middle of the standoff, she had watched from the sidelines for a moment before speaking up.
Relief lit Chen Haoyu’s face. “Sister, thank goodness you’re here! Hurry and talk Granduncle Lu down before he gives Shuzhen any more grief.”
Qianli Xun stepped forward with a curtsy. “I’m sorry you had to witness this spectacle, Sister-in-law.”
Chen Wenxin caught her elbow. “Really, why bother locking horns with an old man?” Letting go of Shuzhen, she walked over to Chen Lu, curtsied, and offered her arm. “Granduncle Lu, don’t hold a grudge against Shuzhen. Let me help you inside.”
To Chen Lu, a married-out daughter was spilled water, not worth his regard. He brushed her off. “Second Young Madam shows me zero respect, so of course I’m taking issue with her. Step aside and keep your nose out of Chen family affairs.”
Fury flared in Chen Wenxin’s chest. She opened her mouth to snap back, only for Qianli Xun to stop her.
“Leave this to me, Sister-in-law.”
Chen Wenxin stepped back—better to let Shuzhen give the old coot a proper tongue-lashing on her behalf.
Qianli Xun stepped up to face him. “Suits me fine—I’ve got a bone to pick with Granduncle Lu too, so let’s lay it all out. Claiming I ruined Chen Haoyu’s chances of inheriting the Chen businesses is pure nonsense! Even setting aside the fact that Chen Haoyu has zero interest in it, with his old track record the elders wouldn’t have looked twice at him anyway. Back then the Zhus and Chens weren’t even betrothed—what did any of that have to do with me? Later, Old Madam and my parents-in-law agreed to this match because they’re sensible people who knew the gossip about me was groundless. Is Granduncle Lu saying he doubts Old Madam and my in-laws too? I married into the Chen house to live a quiet life. Whether Chen Haoyu inherits the family business or not couldn’t matter less to me.”
Chen Haoyu chimed in at once. “Same here—I couldn’t care less either.”
Qianli Xun shot him a pleased smile. “Since neither of us cares, where does this talk about his marrying me ruining his inheritance come from? Please take that notion back and quit bringing it up, Granduncle Lu. Keep harping on it, and folks might get the wrong idea that Chen Haoyu is eyeing the family business and grow resentful.”
Chen Haodong caught the jab aimed straight at him and scoffed under his breath. Still, his sister-in-law’s words pleased him—good, let them stay clear of the inheritance, because fighting him for it wouldn’t end well. Then again, who knew if she meant a word she said? Better keep listening to Granduncle Lu put her in her place.
Chen Lu shot a sideways glance at Chen Haodong before answering. “Good that the two of you know your place. If Old Madam doesn’t look down on you, why should I meddle in Jianglin’s branch? I just feel it’s a pity for Haoyu.”
Chen Haoyu jumped right in. “Save your pity, Granduncle Lu. I’m thrilled to be married to Shuzhen.”
“Since you’re determined to shield your wife and don’t think she’s dragging you down, I’ve nothing more to say—we’ll drop it.” Chen Lu’s eyes swept over the kneeling maids. “Still, as Haoyu’s wife, how do you explain standing back and enjoying the show while ignoring these girls?”
What a busybody, Qianli Xun thought. Time to knock him down a peg and teach him not to poke his nose where it didn’t belong. “This is my household business, Granduncle Lu. Best stay out of it.”
Right on cue, Chen Lu bristled. “I am a clan elder! I have every right to meddle in a junior’s household affairs!”
Walked straight into the trap. Qianli Xun smiled. “Since Granduncle Lu insists on stepping in, why not offer something practical?”
Chen Lu blinked. “What do you mean?”
Instead of answering him right away, Qianli Xun walked up to the kneeling maids and looked each one over. “Chen Haoyu acted like a fool in the past, and wronging you was his fault. If it were up to me, he’d deserve death by a thousand cuts...”
Hearing that, Chen Haoyu stuck his tongue out in fright and kept his mouth shut to hear what came next.
“I’d even let him take you as concubines,” she went on, “except he refuses, and I’m not about to force him. Tell me: aside from becoming his concubines, what other path would you choose? Anything within my power, I’ll do my best to arrange.”
The maids exchanged glances. By now their crying had tapered off, their cheeks were dry, and their heads were clear. Becoming Young Master Chen’s concubines was off the table; might as well angle for the next best thing.
One maid spoke up for the group. “What worries us most is never finding husbands. If Young Madam can arrange good marriages for us, we swear we’ll never come back to make trouble.”
Qianli Xun asked, “What do you look for in a future husband? Age, family background, that sort of thing?”
Another maid spoke up. “If his family has silver, nothing else matters. If his family is ordinary, he should be close to our age and strong.”
Every other maid nodded in agreement.
“Splendid!” Qianli Xun pivoted toward Chen Lu. “Heard that, Granduncle Lu? I’m right in the middle of solving my husband’s headache. Since you’re so fond of meddling in your juniors’ affairs, why not lend a hand?”
Chen Lu stayed baffled. “What are you getting at?”
Qianli Xun pointed and counted them off one by one. “One, two, three, four, five, six—six girls in all. Take your pick, Granduncle Lu! Settle a few of them yourself, whether as concubines or proper wives. You’re never short on silver, so they won’t mind your age. An elder helping a junior out of a jam is only right—and that way all your meddling won’t go to waste!”
Watching his wife tease Granduncle Lu made Chen Haoyu fight to keep a straight face out of respect for his elder.
Chen Wenxin let out an amused snort of pure satisfaction. Even Chen Haodong bit back a chuckle, turning his head away to keep from laughing at the elder he himself had invited.
Chen Lu spluttered, purple in the face. “You—you—hmph!”
Without waiting for Chen Haodong’s arm, he spun around and stalked into Chen Manor. Chen Haodong suppressed his grin and hurried after him.
It was Chen Haodong who had slipped those maids silver and goaded them into making a scene today. They had done their job well enough: at the very least, his sister-in-law had alienated Granduncle Lu, and without Granduncle Lu’s backing, plenty of other elders would drop Haoyu no matter how much he reformed. The only snag was that Haoyu’s wife already looked close to sorting out the mess. No matter—he had another move waiting up his sleeve.
Chen Wenxin gave her a thumbs-up. “Brilliant! Hats off to you!”
Qianli Xun could not summon a smile. “Your brother’s the brilliant one—stirring up a mess this big and leaving me to clean it up.”
Chen Wenxin reached out and poked Chen Haoyu’s forehead. “You—never did a shred of good before, and now Shuzhen has to wipe your backside for you.”
Regret gnawed at Chen Haoyu’s guts, yet no words could soothe his wife right now. He kept his mouth shut.
Qianli Xun turned to the six maids. “I’ve given my word to arrange marriages for you. No more coming here to make scenes, understood?”
“Understood!” they chorused.
“Get up, then. Keep kneeling and people will think I’m bullying the help.”
The maids thanked her and rose to leave.
Qianli Xun tugged her sister-in-law’s sleeve. “Come sit in my room and chat with me, Sister-in-law.”
“Hold on—let me have a word with them first.” Chen Wenxin walked over to Butler Wang and the gatekeepers. “Do Grandmother and the Masters know about today’s scene?”
“No, Miss,” Butler Wang answered.
“Keep it that way. Not a loose word inside the manor, hear me?”
“Rest assured, Eldest Miss. We know our place.”
Reassured, Chen Wenxin took Shuzhen’s hand and walked inside.
Left trailing behind, Chen Haoyu felt his spirits plummet. All his hard work over the past weeks had gone up in smoke; winning a place in his wife’s heart would be ten times harder now. He slapped his own cheek—what good did regret do? Better trail after her and look for a chance to get back in her good graces.
Inside Moxiang Courtyard, he made to follow them into Suxin Thatched Hall, only for the door to slam right in his face. He took a step back and trudged off toward the rear courtyard, resigned to apologizing once his sister left.
Still simmering over the maids, Qianli Xun had shut him out on instinct.
Chen Wenxin sat down without waiting to be asked and laughed. “My, you’ve come a long way.”
“Not the time for jokes, Sister-in-law—I’m fuming!” Not seeing her maid, Qianli Xun called out, “Kuqiao! Are you there?”
Busy tidying the inner room, Kuqiao stepped out at her call. “Back so soon, Miss? Young Madam is here too—I’ll whisk some tea right away.”
That was all Qianli Xun wanted; since Kuqiao read her mind, there was nothing more to order. Still uneasy over the pamphlets, she walked over to her sister-in-law. “Outside the gate, you said someone already dealt with the pamphlets. Who was kind enough to step in?”
“Sit down and I’ll tell you the whole thing.”
Qianli Xun sat and watched her in anticipation.
“Father-in-law told me Prince Yi sent Commander Lin and men from the Judicial Office to round up most of the pamphlets and burn the lot. They arrested the bookshop manager and the men handing them out too, though since it wasn’t a major crime, they released them soon after.”
Prince Yi again. Gratitude mixed with dread in Qianli Xun’s chest—if Mengrong learned her own brother had wrecked her scheme, she would hate her even more.
“What’s wrong?” Surprised that Shuzhen did not look cheered at all, Chen Wenxin added, “Father-in-law also told me to tell you to stop writing poetry and ci lyrics from now on.”
Qianli Xun’s shoulders slumped. “Even if I quit writing, anyone out to make my life miserable will find another way. There’s no stopping them.”
“Sounds like you know who’s behind those pamphlets.”
Qianli Xun covered up at once. “How could I know? If I knew, would I just sit back and let them run wild?”
“True—with those fists of yours, you’d have beaten them silly.” Chen Wenxin paused, a thought crossing her mind. “Wait. Prince Yi questioned the bookshop manager, so he must know who ordered them printed. Why didn’t he arrest whoever it was?”
Qianli Xun asked in alarm, “Does Father know who did it?”
“No, and he couldn’t well ask Prince Yi straight out.”
“Lin Jue made the arrests himself, so he must know something. Didn’t he tell Father?”
“Father-in-law asked Commander Lin, and he claimed he knew nothing.”
Qianli Xun breathed out in relief. She did not want her family finding out that Mengrong despised her over Prince Yi—enough to wish her dead. Troubles of her own making were not for her family to shoulder.
“Why do I get the feeling you’re terrified Father-in-law might find out who it is?”
“Not at all! Stop imagining things, Sister-in-law.”
Chen Wenxin switched tack out of the blue. “Ever since you drifted apart from Princess Consort Yi, Miao Zhiyu and Bai Meiruo have grown thick with her. Makes sense, I suppose—the Qiaos are imperial kin, so it’d be stranger if they didn’t socialize. And Commander Lin clearly wants to use his wife to get closer to the royals and climb higher.”
Qianli Xun’s heart skipped a beat. Did her sister-in-law know something? “Why bring that up all of a sudden?”
“Just passing chatter.”
Qianli Xun exhaled.
“To think how close you and the Princess Consort were last year when she was still Princess Jiahe—everyone envied you two! Who knew things could sour overnight, all because of...” Chen Wenxin swallowed the last two words; complaining about royals behind their backs was asking for trouble.
“Drop it, Sister-in-law. What’s past is past—we have to look forward.” Saying it comforted herself as much as her sister-in-law.
Chen Wenxin chuckled. “Look forward, yes! Still, seeing you lose your temper with Haoyu today took me by surprise.”
“I get mad at him all the time. What’s surprising about that?”
“It’s different! When did you ever get mad at him over other women before? Right after the wedding he spent all day in brothels and you didn’t bat an eye. Now a handful of maids has you slamming the door in his face—that means you’re starting to care about him.”
Her sister-in-law’s words drove the truth home: she really was starting to care about Chen Haoyu.
“Don’t dwell on Haoyu’s past, Shuzhen. Look at who he is now. Changing for your sake proves he’s sincere—and shows he’s not bad at heart.”
Unable to fault the logic, Qianli Xun nodded. The six maids still needed husbands, so she might as well ask her sister-in-law for help. “Sister-in-law, I’ve got a way to settle those maids’ marriages. Could you do me a favor?”
“No need to stand on ceremony with me. Name it.”
“Lin Jue commands plenty of soldiers, and lots of them must still be unmarried. Some of them won’t mind those maids’ pasts, so I want to ask him to help match them up. Given my history with him, I can’t go see him myself—and if Bai Meiruo caught wind of it, she’d raise a storm in his house. Could you ask my brother to step in and put in a word with Lin Jue?”
“Why not send Haoyu?”
“He has no pull with Lin Jue—Lin Jue wouldn’t give him the time of day. If my brother goes instead, Lin Jue will at least respect Father’s position as Prefect and won’t turn him down flat.”
“Clever little scheme—using the Zhu family’s connections to clean up your husband’s mess.”
“Chen Haoyu is your younger brother too! Don’t you want things to go well for him?”
Chen Wenxin laughed in delight. “Seeing how hard you’re looking out for Haoyu, I’d hold a knife to your brother’s throat if I had to make him pull this off!”
#CH 274
#TITLE: One Headache Settled
After posting guards at Lazybones Village and guiding the County Magistrate of Linshui to survey the surrounding soil, Lin Jue rode back into Lin’an after dusk, planning to report to the Prefect and Prince Yi the next day.
As he reined in outside Lin Manor, a familiar voice hailed him. “Commander Lin, back at last! You kept me waiting!”
It was Zhu Jingyuan. Lin Jue dismounted, handed his reins to a servant, and looked toward a parked Zhu family carriage where Zhu Jingyuan was poking his head out.
Through the Prefect’s pull, Zhu Jingyuan held a low-ranking clerical post at the prefectural yamen—no official grade, just an errand-runner—yet everyone showed him face out of respect for his father, and Lin Jue was no exception.
He stepped over. “Has Brother Zhu been waiting out here long?” Having no official title, Jingyuan could not be called “Lord,” and since he had once been his brother-in-law, “Brother Zhu” came out easy enough.
“Not too long—about half an hour. You’re a busy man these days handling big projects for Prince Yi and the Prefect, impossible to track down, so I figured I’d stake out your front gate. Worked like a charm!”
“Since you’re here, Brother Zhu, come inside for a cup of tea.”
“I won’t impose. Why not hop into the carriage and chat a minute?”
Wondering what warranted ambushing him at his gate, Lin Jue climbed aboard and sat across from him. “Must be something urgent, Brother Zhu. Speak straight.”
“Nothing to do with the yamen—a personal favor, and only Commander Lin can pull it off.”
His curiosity piqued, Lin Jue said, “I’m listening.”
“As Commander, you oversee Lin’an’s garrison. Your word carries far more weight now than back when you were a drill officer. Besides, it’s a good thing—your soldiers won’t say no.”
“Come out with it, Brother Zhu.”
“Nothing huge. Six maids around my sister are worried they’ll never get married and begged her to find them good matches. After thinking it over, she figured the soldiers under your command are the best fit. As a married woman, she couldn’t come calling in person, so she had her sister-in-law pass the request to me. You know how I dote on my sister, so I came straight to you.”
Lin Jue caught on in a heartbeat. Zhu Shuzhen wanted husbands for maids—and which maids would she be in such a hurry to marry off? A second’s thought gave him the answer.
“Chen Haoyu’s maids, I bet? He had his way with them and tossed them aside, leaving Zhu Shuzhen to clean up his mess—and she thought of me?”
Guessed dead-on, Zhu Jingyuan dropped the tact. “Sharp as ever, Commander Lin. Haoyu did plenty of foolish things in the past, and now Shuzhen has to sort them out. Wenxin tells me all six girls are fresh and pretty—your men won’t be disappointed. Plus my sister will put together dowries for them, so your soldiers come out ahead.”
Lin Jue sneered inside. *Zhu Shuzhen, oh Zhu Shuzhen! You looked down on me and bent over backward for a mutual divorce, and for what? Marrying Chen Haoyu only to wipe up his messes—life isn’t so sweet now, is it? Starting to see how much better your ex-husband was? Fine, I’ll help you—just to show you I’m ten times the man Chen Haoyu is!*
“Tell Shuzhen to get their dowries ready,” he said.
Delighted by his prompt agreement, Zhu Jingyuan beamed. “Thank you on my sister’s behalf, Commander Lin! Tomorrow I’ll host a banquet at Juxiang Tower to repay you.”
“No need for ceremony, Brother Zhu—tomorrow I’m swamped and in no mood for banquets. Put it on a tab, and we’ll drink our fill once things quiet down.”
“Deal!”
The next morning, after briefing Zhu Xiyan at the prefectural office on Lazybones Village, Lin Jue accompanied him to the Judicial Office.
Prince Yi received them in the main hall. With Zhu Xiyan present, Lin Jue stayed silent behind him after bowing.
Zhu Xiyan spoke first. “Your Highness, yesterday Commander Lin posted guards at the entrance to Lazybones Village so no one slips out. The County Magistrate of Linshui also inspected the soil in detail and confirmed it can only grow weeds and wild vegetables, not grain.”
Shen Jianchen thought it over. “Then work with nature instead of against it. Have the villagers clear the weeds, till the plots, and cultivate wild vegetables on a large scale—and I’ll trouble the Prefect to help secure buyers.”
Reluctant as he was, Zhu Xiyan had to agree. “Understood. As for building earthen cottages and cave dwellings in the village, I’ve already put Commander Lin in charge. Construction should take about half a year.”
“Thank you both for your trouble,” Shen Jianchen said. “Pull this off and it will benefit both court and commoner—worthy of your offices. The people of Lin’an might even sing the yamen’s praises.”
Zhu Xiyan cared nothing for popular praise; he just wanted a quiet life. When Prince Yi first arrived and ignored everything, Zhu Xiyan had been ecstatic. Yet since returning from his wedding, the prince had turned dead serious—piling work on the yamen, burning manpower and silver, with no telling what else he would cook up next.
“I dare not claim credit,” Zhu Xiyan said. “All credit belongs to Your Highness.”
“Both of you have worked hard. Once this succeeds, I will petition His Majesty to commend you.”
“Serving the court is merely our duty.”
“Good. Go see to your duties.”
Outside the Judicial Office, the two parted ways. Pretending to ride off to Lazybones Village, Lin Jue waited until the Prefect was well down the road, then wheeled his horse back to the Judicial Office, tied it up, and walked straight back into the hall.
“Your Highness, I have one more matter to report.”
Shen Jianchen set down his dossier, intrigued. Whatever Lin Jue had to say couldn’t be spoken in front of Zhu Xiyan. “Speak freely, Commander Lin.”
“It concerns Zhu Shuzhen.”
Lin Jue could have handled Zhu Jingyuan’s request on his own, yet seeing Prince Yi today had given him an idea. Nobody knew better than Lin Jue that Prince Yi still could not let go of Zhu Shuzhen. Bringing him word of her let the prince keep tabs on her life—and showed that Lin Jue looked out for the woman in his heart, earning his trust as an insider.
Sure enough, Prince Yi leaned forward. “What happened?”
“Nothing major. Chen Haoyu used to abduct unwed girls and stash them as maids in outside Chen residences, and some lost their virtue to him. Now Zhu Shuzhen wants to marry those girls off, settled on Lin’an’s garrison soldiers, and came to me for help. I wasn’t sure whether to agree and wanted to hear Your Highness’s thoughts.”
Qianli Xun was cleaning up Chen Haoyu’s messes—which meant she had accepted him. Shen Jianchen sighed inside for both of them. All he could do now was help her from the shadows.
“That’s easy enough for you, Commander Lin. Agree to it.”
“Yes, Your Highness.”
“And put your full effort into Lazybones Village.”
“I will spare no effort on any task Your Highness assigns.”
Lin Jue bowed out with an easy mind, heading first to the Military Training Office to pick six soldiers to send to Chen Manor before riding on to Lazybones Village. Getting saddled with the village project was a nuisance, sure, yet locking in favor with two powerful men more than made up for it.
Qianli Xun skipped the congee shed that morning, chatted with the elders a while, and worked on her huaben tale until noon when Kuqiao set out lunch.
Right after lunch, just as she planned to nap, a servant came to report. “A drill officer is waiting outside the south gate with six soldiers, saying Commander Lin sent them to see Second Young Madam.”
Her brother had sent word that morning that Lin Jue had agreed on the spot, yet she hadn’t expected them this fast. Sleepiness vanished. Smoothing her clothes, she walked out to the front gate with Kuqiao.
The drill officer saluted. “Are you Second Young Madam?”
“I am.”
“Per Commander Lin’s orders, I’ve brought the men. Take a look—do they meet your approval?”
Qianli Xun sized up the six men: plain-featured, sturdy builds—well matched for the six maids. The only question was whether they minded that the girls weren’t virgins. “Step aside with me for a word, officer.”
Once out of earshot, the officer asked, “Are you satisfied, Second Young Madam?”
“Very. Do they mind that the six maids...?”
The officer caught her meaning at once. “Rest easy, Second Young Madam. Commander Lin laid out the maids’ circumstances upfront. None of them mind—they just want pretty wives. And with you providing dowries too, why wouldn’t they jump at the chance?”
“Good. I’ll send for the maids right now. If they hit it off, they can leave together on the spot.”
“That’s what Commander Lin had in mind.”
Qianli Xun nodded to Kuqiao, who spoke to a gatekeeper; the servant hitched a carriage and drove off. Kuqiao brought out a chair so her miss could wait in comfort.
Less than three quarters of an hour later, the carriage rolled back up. The moment the six maids stepped down, the soldiers’ eyes glued onto them as though they had never seen pretty women before.
Relieved, Qianli Xun rose and walked over to the maids. “See those six? Happy with them?”
All six nodded in shy approval.
“Then take your pick. Once you choose, you’re going home with him.”
The maids nodded again. “Go ahead, choose.”
Before the maids could move, the soldiers surged forward, each taking a girl by the hand. “I pick you!”
The maids hung their heads, blushing crimson.
Thrilled it went off without a hitch, Qianli Xun said, “Since you’re all paired up, head home and arrange the weddings with your families. Tomorrow I’ll have the dowries delivered to the Military Training Office.”
“Well? Thank Second Young Madam!” the drill officer barked.
“Thank you, Second Young Madam!” they all chorused.
Once they left, Qianli Xun blew out a long breath. “Peace and quiet at last!” Then a fresh worry hit her. “Kuqiao, are there any more maids like that stashed in other Chen residences?”
Kuqiao smiled. “None at all, Miss.”
“How do you know?”
“I already asked Butler Wang last night. He runs the manor—nobody knows the outside properties better than him.”
“Good thinking.”
With the weight off her shoulders, post-lunch drowsiness crept back in. Yawning, Qianli Xun turned to go rest when the rattle of carriage wheels caught her ear. Looking back on reflex, she saw a flashy carriage decked out far too garishly for any official family.
“What’s a carriage from Chunxiang Brothel doing at Chen Manor?” Kuqiao wondered aloud.
“Chunxiang Brothel? How can you tell?”
“I saw Chun Jinqiao ride in one back at Lin Manor.”
A bad feeling prickled Qianli Xun’s spine. One mess cleared, and another rolling up to the door?
Two gaudily dressed women stepped down—one of them unmistakable: Chun Jinmo.
Qianli Xun cursed in her head. *Chen Haoyu, you bastard! I married you for a quiet life, and look at the land mines you left behind! And you? Sneaking off to the silk shop first thing in the morning to dodge it all while I stay home mopping up your garbage!*
Ignoring the pair approaching her, she turned back toward the manor gate.
Chun Jinmo’s voice rang out behind her. “Why walk away without a word when my sister and I came all this way, Young Madam?”
Qianli Xun kept walking.
“Hmph! Don’t tell me Young Madam is scared of us? Where did all that swagger go from when you stormed into Chunxiang Brothel to haul Young Master Chen off? A few days as his wife, and has he already tamed you?”
With her taunting like that, walking away wasn’t an option. Qianli Xun spun around. “What do you want? You’re not welcome at Chen Manor. Be smart and get lost.”
“Whether Chen Manor welcomes me or not doesn’t matter,” Chun Jinmo said. “I came to discuss business with Second Young Madam.”
“If it’s about becoming Chen Haoyu’s concubine,” Qianli Xun snapped, “save your breath. Not that I’d stop him—he’s the one who refuses to take concubines.”
“Sharp as ever, seeing straight through my visit. Only it’s not me who wants to be his concubine. After you hauled Young Master Chen out of Chunxiang Brothel, I came to my senses. Even if he bought my freedom, I’d only ever be a lowly concubine in Chen Manor. With Master Jiang it’s different—he redeemed my contract and made me his primary wife.”
“Smart choice. Should I call you Madam Chun now, then?”
“My surname isn’t Chun—the brothel pinned that on me. My family name is Jiang. Call me Madam Jiang.”
“Hmph! Why isn’t Madam Jiang staying put at the Qiantang Gang instead of rolling up in a Chunxiang Brothel carriage? What are you after?”
“You haven’t met this sister beside me, Young Madam. I brought her today hoping you’d take a page from Commander Lin’s wife, show some generosity, buy out her contract, and install her as Young Master Chen’s concubine.”
Qianli Xun looked at the woman beside Chun Jinmo—just as striking, dressed head to toe like a courtesan, a complete stranger, yet raw hatred burned in her eyes.
*Chen Haoyu, you absolute scoundrel! How many women did you string along in the brothels? Wait till you get home—I’m settling every last score!*
“Dragging some random woman up to my door and expecting me to imitate Bai Meiruo? Laughable! I haven’t got time to waste on you. Excuse me.”
She turned to leave—and a gleaming dagger flashed in the stranger’s hand, lunging straight at her.
#CH 275
#TITLE: Enter Chun Jinlan
The glinting blade made Kuqiao shriek. “Miss! Watch out!”
The split second before the dagger reached her, Qianli Xun shifted sideways, slipping clear of the lethal thrust. She could have disarmed her on the spot, yet wanting to test the woman’s skill, she held back and dodged.
Missing her mark, the woman switched angles and thrust again, aiming straight for the heart.
*That’s all you’ve got, and you dare come at me?*
Unruffled, Qianli Xun leaned clear and snapped a punch into the woman’s wrist. With a gasp of pain, the woman dropped the dagger onto the flagstones. Refusing to quit, she dove to snatch it back.
Qianli Xun gave her no second chance. A crisp kick sent the woman sprawling flat on the ground, unable to get up.
Qianli Xun bent and picked up the blade. “Nice dagger. Couldn’t have been cheap.”
Biting back pain, the woman glared up in silent fury.
Chun Jinmo hurried over to help her up. “I told you she’s tough, and you wouldn’t listen! Insisted on taking her on—and look, you got yourself hurt!”
“Even if it kills me,” the woman spat, “I’m getting my revenge!”
Qianli Xun tossed the dagger to Kuqiao. “Delivered right to our door—waste not, want not.”
Kuqiao caught it. “Count on me, Miss, I’ll put it away safe. Let every blind fool know that crossing my miss ends one way—you don’t walk back out!”
Pleased by Kuqiao’s grit, Qianli Xun frowned in puzzlement. “Hey! Who even are you? First time I’ve laid eyes on you—I never wronged you, did I?”
“You didn’t!” the woman hissed. “Chen Haoyu did!”
*Chen Haoyu, wait till you get home—I’m making you kneel on a washboard!*
Fuming though she was, the mess in front of her came first. “If he wronged you, take it up with him! Why come after me?”
“Blame yourself for being his wife! Because of you, he tossed aside every girl he ruined! You’re the root of it all—kill you, and he’ll go back to his old ways so I can have him!”
What a brainless idiot, Qianli Xun thought. Not worth arguing with a fool—she was about to toss out a soothing word when a group of servants came through the main gate supporting Old Madam Xia.
With nobody gagging the gatekeepers this time, word of the commotion had flown straight to the Old Madam while the family sat listening to a storyteller. Having the maid serve the storyteller tea and fruit while he paused, Old Madam Xia had marched out to see for herself—she wasn’t about to leave Shuzhen facing every storm alone.
Seasoned as she was, Old Madam Xia saw at one glance that handling a scandal like this out on the street would make the Chens a laughingstock.
“Causing a scene at Chen Manor’s gate and attacking my granddaughter-in-law—what kind of behavior is this? Whatever you have to say, say it inside!”
Having elders back her up felt wonderful. Qianli Xun curtsied. “Forgive me for alarming you, Grandmother.”
Yao Qingyan caught her arm and spoke for the Old Madam. “How is this your fault? It’s all Haoyu’s doing.”
Warmed by her mother-in-law’s words, Qianli Xun stepped in to help support Old Madam Xia through the gate. Yao Qingyan called over her shoulder to the two women, “Come along inside, both of you.”
Chun Jinmo whispered in her companion’s ear, “The Chen elders are stepping in—you’ve got a real shot now. I’ll go in with you.”
A faint smile touched the woman’s lips at last. “I hope you’re right, Sister.”
Receiving two brothel women in the main hall was out of the question; servants had already arranged chairs in the courtyard closest to the gate. The Chens, Qianli Xun included, sat facing the entrance as the pair walked in.
“They didn’t even set out seats for us, Sister,” the girl muttered. “Clearly they look down on us.”
“Swallow it for now,” Chun Jinmo murmured back. “We come from a brothel—respectable families are bound to look down their noses.”
Before they could bow, Old Madam Xia cut them off. “Save it—I can’t stomach your courtesies. Give your names and state your business.”
Chun Jinmo spoke first. “My family name is Jiang, given name Liluo. I am married to Master Jiang of the Qiantang Gang, so you may call me Madam Jiang.”
Madam Bao spoke up with ill-concealed glee. “How odd! If you’re already Madam Jiang, what are you doing here making a scene?”
“Madam may not know,” Chun Jinmo went on, “I came from Chunxiang Brothel, where Young Master Chen was my sole patron. I hoped he would buy my freedom and take me as a concubine, yet after Young Madam hauled him out of the brothel, he forgot I existed. I cried my eyes out before coming to my senses. Master Jiang treated me right, bought my freedom, and married me as his primary wife, so I went with him.”
Madam Cui chimed in. “Sounds like you landed well enough. Still doesn’t explain why you’re here causing trouble. Aren’t you afraid of angering Master Jiang?”
“Don’t rush, Madams—hear me out. The day before my wedding, this younger sister came at me with a dagger. When I asked why, I learned she’s just as pitiable as me. Back in the countryside, Young Master Chen saw her beauty, forced himself on her, and instead of marrying her, sold her to a Yangzhou brothel! She wept day and night until they forced her to take customers. The Yangzhou madam was brutal, so she fled back to Lin’an. Hearing Young Master Chen spent all his time with me at Chunxiang Brothel, she blamed me and came after me with a knife...”
The girl cut her off. “Let me tell it, Sister! Once I found out Young Master Chen had dumped Sister too, I stopped blaming her. She talked the Chunxiang Brothel madam into taking me in and gave me the name Chun Jinlan. I hate Chen Haoyu to the bone and want him dead—unless... unless he takes me by his side! Hearing from Sister that he married a wife, dotes on her alone, and acts as if all his past sins never happened only made me hate him ten times more!”
Now everyone had the whole story.
Madam Bao marveled inside at her son’s resourcefulness—this second bomb would wreck Haoyu for sure! No matter how sharp his wife was, she’d never talk her way out of this; Haoyu would end up saddled with a brothel concubine and kiss the inheritance goodbye.
Madam Cui thanked her lucky stars for her quiet, bookish son who never dragged home messes like this.
Yao Qingyan knew her son had been wild, yet abducting a girl and selling her into a brothel? Mortification robbed her of speech in front of her sisters-in-law.
Heart heavy, Qianli Xun thought a washboard was far too kind for a husband like this. Remembering his tender words at Juxiang Tower, she tried telling herself maybe he wasn’t that rotten.
“We can’t believe one side of the story alone,” she said. “We need to hear what Chen Haoyu has to say.”
Nursing a splitting headache over her grandson, Old Madam Xia agreed at once. “Shuzhen is right. Someone!”
A servant stepped up. “Your orders, Old Madam?”
“Go to the silk shop and fetch Second Young Master back.”
“Yes, Old Madam!”
Once the servant left, Old Madam Xia told the pair, “It’ll take half a shichen for Haoyu to get back. Feel free to stroll around the garden; I’ll send someone for you once he arrives.”
Thrilled to tour Chen Manor, the two curtsied. “Thank you, Old Madam.”
Helping Old Madam Xia up, Qianli Xun, her mother-in-law, and the two aunts headed back toward the inner quarters to wait for Chen Haoyu’s interrogation.
Along the path they ran into Ye Anya walking out with her maid Xiaocui.
Madam Bao’s face darkened. “Where do you think you’re going?”
Ye Anya curtsied with caution. “Feeling stifled in my room, so I stepped out for air.”
Madam Bao jabbed a finger at her. “No manners at all! Shouldn’t you ask your mother-in-law’s leave before stepping out? If I hadn’t run into you, I wouldn’t even know where you’d wandered off to!”
“Your daughter-in-law would like to go out for a walk. Please grant permission, Mother.”
“Denied!” Madam Bao snapped. “Stay home today and keep the elders company.”
Old Madam Xia stepped in. “Let Anya go if she wants. Women in the Chen family aren’t bound by petty rules.”
Shamed by the Old Madam’s kindness, Ye Anya bowed. “I won’t go out, Grandmother. I’ll stay and keep you company.”
Knowing full well what Ye Anya was up to, Qianli Xun stepped over and took her hand. “Come with me to Grandmother’s courtyard and chat with her, Eldest Sister-in-law.”
“All right,” Ye Anya murmured.
As the group walked on, Qianli Xun caught Ye Anya flashing a signal to Xiaocui. Xiaocui nodded, lagged behind until everyone passed, then slipped back toward the front gate.
Qianli Xun said nothing, yet worry tugged at her. Where would Ye Anya’s gamble lead? Part of her even envied her sister-in-law’s reckless nerve, while she herself fretted over every step until she wound up cleaning up a husband’s messes.
Had she eloped with Wenyuan, or gone with Prince Yi when he raided the wedding, life would look unrecognizable now. Blame Master Jingguang’s “Right Thought and Right Mindfulness”—every time she hit a crossroads, those words steered her into sacrificing herself to spare everyone else.
*Could I chase my own happiness without a backward glance like Eldest Sister-in-law?*
The answer was still no. She had walked too far down the road of self-restraint to turn back now. Every person’s fate really was sealed the instant they chose their road. Master Jingguang’s other warning echoed clear in her ears: *Fortune and calamity have no gate; people summon them themselves.* Glancing at Ye Anya, she wondered what fate waited at the end of her sister-in-law’s road—and her own.
With trouble at the gate, nobody had the heart for storytelling, so they sent the storyteller home. Sitting with Old Madam Xia, the women drifted between silence and small talk, each turning the crisis over in her head. Ye Anya lamented her ruined rendezvous with Young Master Cao, while Madam Bao hoped the scandal blew up big enough to force Haoyu into taking a brothel girl as concubine.
Meanwhile, Chun Jinmo and Chun Jinlan strolled the manor grounds, and the more Chun Jinmo took in, the heavier her gloom grew.
“He’s out of my reach now,” she sighed. “All my hopes ride on you, Sister.”
Hardness glinted in Chun Jinlan’s eyes. “If he won’t take me, I’ll kill him—and if I can’t kill him, I’ll die right in front of him.”
“What fire—I admire that,” Chun Jinmo said. “One thing puzzles me, though. How did you get hooked up with Chen Manor’s Eldest Young Master?”
“I didn’t know him at all. Somehow he tracked me down, gave me silver, and fed me this whole plan. He’s the one who told me about Young Master Chen and you so I’d come find you.”
“So that’s it.” Chun Jinmo leaned in and whispered something in Chun Jinlan’s ear.
Chun Jinlan gasped. “No way! Really?”
“Keep your voice down—swear you won’t breathe a word! She’s in deep trouble, and her life hangs by a thread. If the Chens take you in today, you’ll live every day in a palace like this—enough to turn me green with envy. And once she’s dead, who knows, you might even be promoted to primary wife! Don’t forget me when that day comes.”
“If it works out, I’ll never forget you, Sister.”
Chun Jinmo let out a wistful sigh. “Truth be told, I still carry a torch for Young Master Chen—he was my first man. All that poetess’s fault! If not for her, I’d never have been forced to marry Master Jiang.”
“You hate her too, Sister?”
“Of course I do.”
“Then talk Master Jiang into taking the job.”
“When Eldest Young Master Chen came to the Qiantang Gang yesterday, my husband actually hemmed and hawed! Turns out the Qiantang Gang looks scary on the outside, yet my husband’s a coward who kept saying the poetess is too tough to mess with. Lucky for us he listens to me—took me two minutes to talk him round. Just watch: a good show is coming soon.”
Chun Jinlan glanced around in nerves. “Lower your voice, Sister! What if someone overhears?”
“Relax, I checked. Nobody’s around.”
They wandered on, floored by the manor’s sheer scale—Chun Jinlan thought it bigger than her whole home village. Reaching the west wing, they gaped at Xinglong Pavilion and the steady stream of clerks bustling in and out.
“Wait—all this is inside Chen Manor, Sister?”
“Where else do you think we are?”
“Why did the Chens stick a shop inside their house?”
Chun Jinmo laughed. “That’s not a shop—see the plaque? ‘Xinglong Pavilion.’ All of the Chen family’s business runs out of there; the actual shops are out on the streets. And those buildings we just passed? If I’m right, that’s housing for the clerks.”
“Even the clerks live inside Chen Manor?”
“Everyone walking around over there was a clerk. The Chens put them up right next to Xinglong Pavilion for convenience.”
“Treating even their clerks this well—no wonder the Chens got so rich.”
“Just remember: after today, you might become one of the mistresses of this place.”
Chun Jinlan smiled, picturing that glittering future—right as a Chen servant approached. “Ladies, Second Young Master has returned. Old Madam sent me to escort you to the eastern pavilion.”
#CH 276
#TITLE: Jinlan Strikes at Young Madam
Footsore from walking, both women grimaced at the trek from the west wing all the way east—until servants trotted up bearing two sedan chairs and set them down beside them. Smiles bloomed on their faces at once.
“Are those sedan chairs for us?” Chun Jinmo asked.
“Yes,” the servant replied. “Second Young Madam worried your feet might tire, so she sent bearers to carry you.”
Exchanging smug smiles, they climbed aboard. The bearers moved briskly and soon set them down near the eastern garden. “We’re here, ladies.”
Stepping out, they looked around and saw no pavilion in sight.
Chun Jinlan frowned. “Where’s the pavilion? Are you playing tricks on us?”
“Follow this footpath straight ahead and take the bend,” the servant said. “You’ll see it right away.”
Walking shoulder to shoulder down the narrow path, Chun Jinmo sighed. “Today we’ve finally seen what real wealth looks like.”
“Even the Qiantang Gang doesn’t measure up?” Chun Jinlan asked.
“The Qiantang Gang is an underworld outfit—money doesn’t buy class. The Chens have both wealth and prestige, and the gang doesn’t have half their money anyway.”
“Do you regret it, Sister?”
“Not regret—just resentment. I gave him my heart, so why did he dump me and marry somebody else?”
“I resent it too.”
“Today’s all on you, Sister.”
The pavilion came into view, cutting their chatter short. The same family members sat around the stone table, only now Chen Haoyu stood in their midst—and Ye Anya was missing, having slipped away on a flimsy excuse to salvage her rendezvous with Cao Ban. Eager for the drama ahead, Madam Bao hadn’t bothered stopping her.
Seeing Young Master Chen again stirred a storm in Chun Jinmo’s chest. She stepped forward. “Young Master Chen, we meet at last.”
Catching his wife turning her head away in displeasure, Chen Haoyu took an instinctive step back. “I hear you married Master Jiang. Better not talk to me like that.”
Dropping the sentiment, Chun Jinmo pulled Chun Jinlan forward. “Do you still remember her, Young Master Chen?”
Recognition flickered across his face. “I remember you, just forgot your name. My fault for acting wild back then and snatching you from the countryside. All I recall is locking you in our country residence—next thing I knew you vanished, so I figured you escaped and didn’t bother chasing you. What are you doing turning up now?”
Fury exploded in Chun Jinlan. She lunged at him, fistfuls of his robe in her grip, tears spilling over. “You ruined my life and dare play dumb?! You defiled me and sold me to a Yangzhou brothel! I was a decent girl, and you made my life a living hell! Today either you take me as your concubine, or you die—or your wife dies—or I die right in front of you!”
With her skills, Qianli Xun could have stepped in and bailed him out in a blink, yet she didn’t lift a finger. You reap what you sow—nobody else could pay his debts for him.
Yao Qingyan started up to scold the girl, only for Old Madam Xia to hold her back. Much as she doted on her grandson, this mess chilled her heart; letting him take a few knocks would do him good.
Overjoyed inside, Madam Bao put on a show of outrage without budging an inch from her stone stool. “This is Chen Manor! How dare you act so wild!”
Seeing the Old Madam restrain her eldest sister-in-law, Madam Cui stayed put, gladder than ever for her own sensible son.
Chun Jinmo stood off to the side watching like a spectator at a play—the wilder Chun Jinlan got, the happier she was, since Jinlan was unleashing everything she herself never dared.
Compared to the disappointment on his wife’s face, Chun Jinlan’s tirade meant nothing to Chen Haoyu. He insisted, “You’re talking nonsense! I never laid a finger on you, and I never sold you to any brothel!”
Screaming like a madwoman, Chun Jinlan wrapped both hands around his throat. “Still denying it?! I’ll strangle you right in front of everyone!”
He endured it a few seconds until his airway tightened, then kicked her away and rubbed his neck. “You crazy shrew! If I did something, I own up to it!”
Sprawled on the paving stones, Chun Jinlan wailed, “Waaah! Chen Haoyu, you’re an animal! You stole my virtue, stuffed me in a sack, and sold me to a Yangzhou brothel! I still remember the man who sold me—big scar across his face, and you called him Scarface!”
Chun Jinmo jumped in. “Scarface always ran with you, Young Master Chen. If he hauled Jinlan to Yangzhou, you had to be behind it. Better own up and take Jinlan as a concubine—it’s best for everyone.”
His throat easing, Chen Haoyu dropped his hand, walked over, and studied Chun Jinlan’s face. “I remember every woman I’ve ever touched, and I have zero memory of touching you. Take Chun Jinmo—when I went to Chunxiang Brothel, I only saw her. I’m not afraid to admit it, not even in front of my wife!”
Qianli Xun rolled her eyes inside. *First time I’ve seen a guy brag about owning up to his sleazy track record like it’s a badge of honor!*
Chun Jinlan sobbed louder. “If you won’t admit it, I don’t want to live!” Scrambling up, she charged headfirst at a pavilion pillar.
Gasps erupted; Chen Haoyu froze.
In a split second, Qianli Xun shot forward, caught Chun Jinlan’s arm, yanked her back, and pinned her upper body against her. “Think you can die in Chen Manor that easy? Not on my watch!”
Chun Jinmo flashed a look. Taking advantage of Qianli Xun’s focus, Chun Jinlan plucked a hairpin from her bun and jabbed straight at the arm pinning her.
Chen Haoyu spotted it first. Terrified his wife would get stabbed, he threw himself forward and thrust his own arm in the way. The hairpin sank straight into his flesh, blood welling at once.
Yao Qingyan rushed to her son’s side in tears. “Haoyu! Bring medicine—hurry!” Both aunts crowded in asking if it hurt.
Hobbling over on a maid’s arm, Old Madam Xia saw the pin jutting from her grandson’s arm and roared in heartbreak, “Enough!”
If not for Chen Haoyu, that pin would have buried itself in her own arm. Hard as it was to call it gratitude, a tremor stirred in Qianli Xun’s chest. Unable to let go of Chun Jinlan lest she lash out again, she kept her eyes locked on his bleeding arm.
Gritting his teeth, Chen Haoyu yanked the hairpin out. Blood spurted, and Yao Qingyan helped him aside.
Watching him bleed stung Chun Jinmo a little, yet envy stung sharper—clearly Young Master Chen cared for the poetess with his whole heart. If someone stabbed at her, he wouldn’t have budged.
Old Madam Xia barked at a maid, “Fetch rope!” Turning to Chun Jinlan, she said, “I’ve watched you long enough to know you won’t quit until somebody ends up dead.”
“I won’t kill anyone,” Chun Jinlan shot back, “so long as you make me Young Master Chen’s concubine!”
“Bah!” Old Madam Xia spat. “Even if my grandson did wrong you, I’d sooner kick him out of Chen Manor than put someone like you beside him!”
“Then wait and see! Either I die, or Chen Haoyu dies, or the woman behind me dies—any one of us will satisfy my grudge!”
When the maid ran back with rope, Old Madam Xia ordered, “Tie her up, Shuzhen.”
Taking the rope one-handed, Qianli Xun trussed Chun Jinlan up in seconds with Kuqiao’s help.
Chun Jinlan yelled, “Rich bullies throwing your weight around! Watch me sue you at the yamen and have the magistrate lock you all up!”
Rubbing her palms, Qianli Xun scoffed, “Talk sense! Carrying on like a lunatic and almost killing someone—tying you up is letting you off light! If the magistrate shows up, you’re the one getting hauled in!”
Growing nervous now that Chun Jinlan was bound helpless, Chun Jinmo felt compelled to chime in. “What right does Old Madam have to tie up my sister? Is there no law in the land?”
Old Madam Xia snapped, “Calling her ‘sister’ every other word—what kind of sister stands by watching while she raises hell? Ten to one you put her up to this!”
“You wrong me, Old Madam—I only came out of pity for her.”
“Pity? Sounds noble enough! From what I’ve gathered, your sister accuses Haoyu of ruining her, and Haoyu denies it. Our family can’t settle this—better report it to the yamen and let the officials investigate. If Haoyu is guilty, the Chens will accept whatever punishment the law hands down. If he didn’t do it, stay far away from Chen Manor!”
Bound tight, Chun Jinlan kept her chin up. “Call the officials, then! Think I’m scared?”
Supported back onto her stone stool, Old Madam Xia gave orders. “Tell Butler Wang to fetch all three Masters home, and tell Eldest Young Master to go to the Judicial Office—say Chen Manor has a case that needs the Judicial Investigator to come in person.”
Medicine arrived, and Yao Qingyan bent over her son’s arm, too distraught to pay attention to anything else.
Hearing the Old Madam summon the Judicial Investigator, Madam Cui warned in worry, “Mother, the Judicial Investigator right now is Prince Yi! How dare we summon a royal to handle a family case?”
“No harm done,” Old Madam Xia replied. “Since Prince Yi took the post of his own accord, he won’t be petty about visiting a commoner’s house—besides, it’s not his first visit here. And we can’t very well haul a tied-up woman to the Judicial Office when she hasn’t been charged.”
“She stabbed Haoyu!” Madam Cui pointed out.
Old Madam Xia sighed. “If Haoyu hadn’t pulled such foolish stunts back then, none of this would’ve happened.”
“Now there’s a sensible word!” Chun Jinlan sneered. “If Chen Haoyu hadn’t abducted me, none of the rest would’ve followed. He’s the culprit!”
His arm salved and bandaged, Chen Haoyu endured the throbbing pain in silence. Yesterday and today, every ugly seed from his past was bearing bitter fruit—right when he had fallen head over heels for his wife. Doing wrong really did catch up with you in the end.
He stole a glance at his wife. Standing off to the side with eyes lowered and brows drawn, ignoring him altogether, she looked deep in thought. Was it because Prince Yi was coming?
It had to be. He had never seen Prince Yi and Zhu Shuzhen act intimate with his own eyes, yet something in his gut told him the man in her heart was Prince Yi, not Qiao Wenxian. Dread tightened his chest: after these two days, would she want out? She had walked away from one marriage via mutual divorce already—would she find a way to leave him too? He couldn’t muster the nerve to say a word to her.
Seeing the servants hurry off to fetch the Chen Masters and the authorities, Chun Jinmo decided it was time to bail before the scandal dragged her under. She told Chun Jinlan, “Sister, I’m a married woman now, not free like before. This is as far as I can stay—I’ve got things waiting at home.” Leaning close to her ear, she murmured, “Whatever you do, I back you. Even if you can’t touch her today, someone else will finish her off soon enough.”
“Thanks, Sister—hurry back. I can handle this alone.”
Chun Jinmo curtsied to the Chens. “Old Madam, Madams, I must excuse myself. If anything happens to my sister here, my husband Master Jiang will stand up and demand justice for her.”
Yao Qingyan snapped, “So long as your sister stops raising hell, everyone stays safe. Don’t stand on ceremony—leave.”
Stung by the cold shoulder, Chun Jinmo hunted for a target to vent on and locked eyes with the poetess, sneering, “So, Youqi Jushi—happy married to Young Master Chen? Don’t think a couple good days means he’ll last a lifetime! I spent enough time in Chunxiang Brothel to know men—hardly a faithful one in the bunch. He’s just hooked on the novelty right now. Give it time, he’ll get bored and go sniffing around outside again, leaving you rotting in an empty bed!”
Chen Haoyu exploded. “Shut your mouth, Chun Jinmo! My heart for my wife is true and will never change!”
“Heh—that’s Madam Jiang to you.” A flash of bitterness crossed Chun Jinmo’s face as she swayed out of the pavilion.
Qianli Xun’s voice rang out sharp behind her. “Courtesy demands give and take! I haven’t had my turn to speak yet—walking off like that is pretty rude, Madam Jiang!”
#CH 277
#TITLE: Prince Yi Takes the Case
Heart sore and furious with Chen Haoyu, Qianli Xun had meant to hold her tongue—yet Chun Jinmo had pushed too far. Not only had she dragged a troublemaker in to wreck the Chens’ peace, she had honeyed her up as “Sister” only to dump her the second the law was called. Qianli Xun despised snakes like that. Had Chun Jinmo slunk off quiet, she would have let her go; since she chose to run her mouth, Qianli Xun wasn’t letting her walk away clean.
Chun Jinmo kept strolling ahead with her maid as if deaf. Qianli Xun strode ahead and planted herself right in her path. “Want out? You go through me first.”
Forced to halt, Chun Jinmo sneered, “What, going to hit me with those fists?”
“For your kind, I don’t need fists. My tongue is plenty.”
“The great Poetess of Jiangnan stooping to curse in public? People will laugh.” Trying to sidestep past her before getting shredded in front of everyone, Chun Jinmo bumped right into Qianli Xun’s outstretched arm.
“Madam Jiang is short on common sense, so let me teach you a little before you go.”
Cornered, Chun Jinmo raised her chin. “What grand lesson haven’t I heard that I need you preaching to me?”
“Not enough, clearly—or you’d know nothing in this world is ever perfect.” Shaking off her gloom, Qianli Xun spoke clear and steady. “Take yourself, Madam Jiang: pretty face, graceful figure, trapped in a brothel—yet still luckier than most courtesans. You caught Chen Haoyu’s eye, and then Master Jiang married you as his primary wife.”
Hearing his name stung Chen Haoyu; back in Chunxiang Brothel he had only chased Chun Jinmo’s looks and maidenhood. How foolish he had been—if regret medicine existed, he’d swallow a whole bottle.
Her words gave Chun Jinmo a flicker of pride; she was luckier than most, except she hadn’t ended up with Young Master Chen. She glanced at him—his eyes were glued to his wife alone.
“I married into the Chen family,” Qianli Xun went on. “Sure, my husband wasn’t much to brag about and stirred up piles of trouble, yet I’ve got a wonderful grandmother, kind parents-in-law, and elders who care for me—more than enough for one lifetime. As for Chen Haoyu, whatever he does is his business. Even if his change of heart only lasts a spell, so what? Right here and now, he’s sincere with me. Who can predict the future? Not even Madam Jiang can guarantee what tomorrow brings.”
Pain pulsed in his arm, yet Chen Haoyu spoke loud before everyone. “Rest easy, Wife—my heart for you will never change.”
A public vow couldn’t lift Qianli Xun’s mood. With a wry smile, she pressed on. “So every word you spat just now means nothing—and my household affairs aren’t for outsiders to fret over. If you love meddling so much, why bolt instead of staying to defend your ‘sister’ when the officials arrive? Scared the trouble will splash onto you, aren’t you? Makes that sweet ‘Sister’ sound mighty fake.”
Already bruised by Chen Haoyu’s vow and now stripped bare in front of everyone, Chun Jinmo flushed purple and barked, “Lies!”
Her point made, Qianli Xun stepped aside and returned to the pavilion.
Yao Qingyan snapped, “Get out already—stick around and the Judicial Investigator might haul you in for questioning too!”
Outnumbered on Chen turf, Chun Jinmo swallowed her rage and turned to Chun Jinlan. “Don’t fall for her tricks, Sister—she’s trying to drive a wedge between us. You know what I’ve got lined up. If the yamen sides with Chen Haoyu, I’ll spend every last copper appealing all the way to the throne for you!”
Touched, Chun Jinlan said, “I know your heart, Sister. Go on—no sense staying here to be insulted.”
*Gullible fool bought every fake word—no wonder Chen Haoyu’s crowd sold her off and Chen Haodong played her like a fiddle.*
“Good—then I’ll leave with an easy mind.” Chun Jinmo hurried out with her maid without looking back.
Meanwhile, at the Judicial Office, Shen Jianchen was poring over dossiers when Kujue arrived from Zhuli Village. Having picked out the site and hired builders for the bamboo cottage, he had come into town first thing to report.
He described the plot on the north edge of the bamboo grove—few villagers around, secluded and quiet, with a waterfall right in view once the cottage doors swung open.
Pleased, Shen Jianchen told Bai Feng to take silver and accompany Kujue back to Zhuli Village to oversee construction, freeing him from constant attendance.
Thrilled at the chance to meet Kuqiao’s parents and build ties with them, Bai Feng missed Kuqiao fiercely after days apart and seized his chance. “Your Highness, Kujue made it all the way into town—at least let me take him to see Kuqiao once!”
Shen Jianchen was about to nod when Han Gu stepped in. “Your Highness, Eldest Young Master Chen is outside reporting a case at Chen Manor that requires the Judicial Office in person.”
“Where is he?”
“Waiting outside.”
“Show him in.”
Chen Haodong had been at the tea shop when the manor’s runner fetched him. Delighted as he was, dragging the Judicial Office in risked exposing himself—until he worked out a foolproof angle on the ride over.
Stepping into the hall, Chen Haodong bowed low. “This commoner pays respects to Your Highness, Prince Yi.”
Fearing it touched Qianli Xun, Shen Jianchen asked at once, “What happened at Chen Manor?”
“It concerns my second brother, Your Highness. He got entangled with a girl in the past who insists he defiled her and sold her to a brothel. Second Brother denies it flat-out. Unable to settle it, my family sent me to request Your Highness come to the manor.”
Sure enough, it touched her. Shen Jianchen rose at once. “Clerk Han, fetch Chief Constable Wen and a squad of constables—we’re riding to Chen Manor.”
Bai Feng panicked. “What about Kujue, Your Highness?”
“Bring him along—we’re headed to the same place!” Striding out, Shen Jianchen led the way while Bai Feng grabbed Kujue and trotted happily behind.
At Chen Manor, all three Chen Masters had been summoned home, gathered with the family in the reception hall. Everyone rose to bow as Prince Yi entered, even Old Madam Xia leaning on a maid.
Chen Jianglin spoke first. “An ugly scandal broke out at home, and hauling the girl to the Judicial Office seemed unseemly, so we troubled Your Highness to make the trip.”
Shen Jianchen’s gaze swept the room and found Qianli Xun in an instant. One glance told him she hurt more than anyone there.
*Hurting over Chen Haoyu—has she fallen for him?* He had no right to ache over that now. For her sake, he would handle this case with utmost care. Warning himself not to linger, he pulled his gaze away before anyone noticed.
“No trouble, Master Chen. As Judicial Investigator, handling cases on-site is routine. Please sit—let me hear the facts before making any ruling.”
He took the main seat, Han Gu sat at a desk to transcribe, Wen Xin stood at his elbow, and the constables guarded the doors.
The instant the prince sat, Bai Feng slipped beside Kuqiao and murmured, “Someone’s outside looking for you.”
Hesitating, Kuqiao whispered to her miss, “Bai Feng says someone’s outside for me. May I step out a moment?”
Kuqiao wasn’t needed here, and she hadn’t seen Bai Feng in days. Qianli Xun nodded and whispered back, “Constables are at the front—slip out the back door.” Kuqiao agreed and slipped out with Bai Feng.
Once the prince settled, Wen Xin barked, “Principals of this case, step forward and kneel to state the facts!”
Chen Haoyu rose first and knelt; maids guided the bound Chun Jinlan forward to kneel beside him.
“Why is she bound?” Shen Jianchen asked.
“After she stabbed Haoyu,” Chen Jianglin replied, “my mother and the women feared she’d hurt someone else and tied her up.”
“Leave her bound, then.” Glancing at Chen Haoyu’s arm—blood had soaked through the bandage, no light wound—Shen Jianchen got down to specifics. “Chen Haoyu, tell me in full how you know this woman.”
Chen Haoyu answered straight. “Last spring, I went out to the countryside for fun and saw her washing clothes by a creek. Struck by her looks, I had my men abduct her and lock her in our country residence, meaning to go back that night... except a few friends sent word inviting me back into town to drink. So I headed back to the city. When I returned to the country house the next day, she had already escaped. I never touched a hair on her head.”
Chun Jinlan cried out, “Don’t believe his one-sided lies, Your Lordship! He ruined me and sold me to a Yangzhou brothel—he destroyed my honor and drove me from home!”
“What is your name?” Shen Jianchen asked.
“This humble woman is called Chun Jinlan now.”
“Tell me everything that happened after you were locked up.”
“I was washing clothes by the creek when someone threw a sack over my head. Strong men hauled me into a carriage and tied me up. No idea how far we rode before they pulled the sack off inside a fine room. Young Master Chen walked in, told me to stay put till he came back that night, and locked the door on his way out. Tied hand and foot, terrified, I curled up on the bed. After dark, in pitch blackness where I couldn’t see a thing, the lock clicked open, someone came in, and forced himself on me—it had to be Chen Haoyu!”
Shen Jianchen frowned. “You just said it was pitch black and you couldn’t see a thing. How can you be sure it was Chen Haoyu?”
“Who else could it be? Why else did he abduct me? Worst of all, afterward he knocked me out cold—when I woke up, I was stuffed inside a sack in a moving carriage, and when they let me out I was in a Yangzhou brothel!”
“Liar!” Chen Haoyu snapped. “You escaped and now you’re framing me!”
Furious, Chun Jinlan scrambled up despite her ropes and lunged to ram into him. Wen Xin flashed forward, blocking her with his scabbard. “Stay on your knees! If you’ve been wronged, His Lordship will see justice done.”
Cowardly at the steel, she sank back down. “All I beg is for Your Lordship to make Chen Haoyu take me as his concubine—otherwise I’ll never give him peace!”
Shen Jianchen glanced again at Qianli Xun. Eyes cast down, face composed, yet the misery rolling off her stabbed right through him. His tone hardened despite himself. “Chen Haoyu!”
Everyone jumped, thinking the prince was about to erupt. Startled, Chen Haoyu answered quick, “Present, Your Lordship.”
Softening his edge, Shen Jianchen asked, “You say you never touched her or sold her. Do you have witnesses?”
“Yes! Scarface and the rest of my crowd can vouch for me, and so can the young masters who invited me back to town that night!”
Sensing something murky underneath, Shen Jianchen turned to Chen Jianglin. “Master Chen, we’ll stop here for today. I’m taking both of them back to the Judicial Office and summoning the witnesses tomorrow for a full hearing.”
“Whatever Your Highness decides.”
Constables filed in. “On your feet,” Wen Xin ordered. “You’re staying at the Judicial Office a few days.”
Chen Haoyu and Chun Jinlan rose and walked out. Passing Chen Haodong, Chun Jinlan shot him a fleeting glance.
Chen Haodong met her eyes without flinching. *Picked the right girl to make a scene—let’s see how Second Brother wriggles out of this! Still, to keep my own neck clean, I’d better go see Scarface tonight.*
That brief exchange of looks did not escape Shen Jianchen’s sharp eyes. Suspicion sparked at once: was Eldest Young Master Chen mixed up in this? Suspicions needed proof, though.
Rising, Shen Jianchen led Han Gu and Wen Xin out as the Chens stood to bow. “Safe journey, Your Highness!”
Passing right by Qianli Xun, Shen Jianchen longed to stop and offer a word of comfort, yet walked straight past. Singling her out would only bring her grief; the best thing he could do was clean up this mess for her.
#CH 278
#TITLE: The Chens Disperse
The second Prince Yi stepped out, Yao Qingyan grabbed her husband’s arm. “What do we do, Jianglin? Won’t Haoyu get locked in a holding cell at the Judicial Office? I hear those cells are awful—how will he stand it?”
“Don’t fret,” Chen Jianglin consoled her. “Listening to the questioning just now, I’m almost certain Haoyu didn’t do it. He’ll be back soon.”
Trusting her husband’s judgment, Yao Qingyan nodded.
