Chen Haodong stepped up wearing a worried face. “Uncle, Aunt, I’ll head over to the Judicial Office in a bit with food and bedding for Haoyu, and slip the jailers some silver so they treat him right.”
“How thoughtful of you, Haodong,” Yao Qingyan said in gratitude. “Take some good wound medicine for his arm too.”
“We’re family, Aunt—no need for thanks. I’ve got fine salve in my room and I’ll pack the lot. Let me go get it ready right now.” Bowing to the elders, he hurried off playing the dutiful cousin.
Blaming herself for spoiling Haoyu rotten, Old Madam Xia hardened her heart and held back every word of worry. “He’s never tasted real hardship in his life—high time he gets a bite of it! Disperse, everyone, back to your own rooms.”
Yao Qingyan and her two sisters-in-law escorted the Old Madam back to her quarters.
Left in the hall with the three Masters, Qianli Xun curtsied. “Father-in-law, Second Uncle, Third Uncle, I’ll excuse myself.”
As she turned, Chen Jianglin called out, “Shuzhen.”
She stopped. “Yes, Father-in-law?”
He wanted to plead on his son’s behalf, yet the words stuck in his throat. What respectable lady could stomach Haoyu’s past—let alone the famous Poetess of Jiangnan who had been reluctant to marry him in the first place? “Never mind. Go rest.”
Qianli Xun turned and left.
Chen Jianglin told his brothers, “Let’s call it a day too.”
Chen Jiangmu stayed put, brow creased. “Hear me out, Eldest Brother. Handing a family scandal to the yamen was a mistake—now the whole city will know.”
“Mother made the call,” Chen Jianglin sighed. “All I can hope is Haoyu really is innocent.”
“I wouldn’t bet on it,” Chen Jiangmu said. “Second Brother and I heard talk outside ages ago—Haoyu used to snatch unwed girls, stashing some in outside residences as maids and selling others to brothels.”
Chen Jianglin whipped around. “Why didn’t you tell me sooner?”
Chen Jiangshu chimed in, “With Mother and Sister-in-law shielding him, and Haoyu so slick with his tongue, telling you would only have put you in a bind.”
Chen Jianglin’s knees buckled; Chen Jiangmu caught him and eased him into a chair.
Chen Jianglin let out a heavy sigh. “Then he likely really did ruin that girl and sell her off. What on earth do we do?”
“Plan for the worst, Eldest Brother,” Chen Jiangmu urged.
“Best fix is simple—let Haoyu take her as a concubine and end the whole thing!” Chen Jiangshu was thrilled to slip that in: between a wife with a battered reputation and a brothel concubine, Haoyu’s standing would crater, knocking him out of the inheritance race for good.
Chen Jianglin rejected it flat-out. “Never! No Chen man has ever taken a concubine—much less a brothel woman!”
“If we don’t settle it,” Chen Jiangmu fretted, “and the Judicial Office proves Haoyu did it, rape is a capital offense under Song law!”
Torn as never before, Chen Jianglin steeled himself. “Flawed as he is, Haoyu is my son. I choose to believe him.”
*Wait till he loses his head—then you’ll cry too late!* Chen Jiangshu thought with glee, keeping a pious face. “If Eldest Brother trusts Haoyu, I’ll trust him too. He’s a Chen lad—how could he do anything so vile?”
Seeing Second Brother fall in line, Chen Jiangmu dropped it. “Then I’ll trust him too.”
Heading back to Moxiang Courtyard, Qianli Xun walked through gathering twilight that felt brighter than her mood, her steps dragging slow.
Heaven loved jerking her around. Just when she thought she’d marry Wenyuan, the rug got pulled out. Now she had bowed to reality and started warming up to Chen Haoyu—only for two days of chaos to hit like a fresh cosmic joke. Maybe Heaven just couldn’t stand seeing her love life run smooth.
“Young Madam! I saw the Judicial Office hauling Young Master away at the gate—what happened?”
Qinggua’s voice made her look up; she had already reached Moxiang Courtyard’s gate. “Oh, Qinggua. When did you get back?”
Seeing her hollow stare, he knew disaster had struck. “Don’t worry, Young Madam—I’m running straight to the Judicial Office to ask them to lock me in with Young Master so he has someone to wait on him!”
“Suit yourself,” she murmured, dragging herself inside. Qinggua sprinted off out of the manor toward the Judicial Office.
A breeze drifted off the courtyard pond, carrying crisp water, broad lotus leaves, and a whiff of silt. Fat lotus buds pushed upward from the dark water, brimming with life. Too drained to look, Qianli Xun shuffled into Suxin Thatched Hall, crossed to her bedchamber, and flopped face-down onto the bed.
No tears came—she didn’t even hurt enough to cry. She was just bone-tired and needed to lie still until she gathered strength for the next round with life.
Moments later Kuqiao stepped in. “If you’re sleepy, Miss, let me help you out of your outer robe so you can rest comfortable.”
Not a twitch from the bed.
“I’ll go whisk you a bowl of tea.”
Kuqiao went to the outer room and whisked tea. Bringing it in, she saw her miss hadn’t moved an inch. “Tea’s ready, Miss. Care for a sip?”
Still dead silent.
Holding the bowl, Kuqiao rattled on at random. “Guess who Bai Feng brought for me to see today?” No answer, so she answered herself. “Kujue! He came into town to update Prince Yi on the bamboo cottage and dropped by to see me. Starting today, Bai Feng’s heading out to the bamboo grove with Kujue to help build it—he’s thrilled to meet my parents. The nerve of him, though, making Kujue call him brother-in-law already!”
Still nothing. Of course—babbling about herself wouldn’t budge her miss. Racking her brains, Kuqiao landed on one surefire topic.
“Miss, I don’t know if I should tell you this—Bai Feng told me today.” Knowing she wouldn’t answer, Kuqiao forged straight ahead. “Bai Feng said Prince Yi and Princess Consort Yi never consummated their marriage in Bianliang. Only after they came back to Lin’an and Young Master Qiao paid Prince Yi a visit and talked him round did he consummate it with her.”
A slight shift rippled through her miss’s shoulders. Glad she had broken the freeze, Kuqiao watched her sit up on the edge of the bed, looking washed-out.
Kuqiao offered the bowl. “Just the right temperature.”
Qianli Xun drank it down in silence and handed the bowl back. “Keep that between us. Don’t go repeating royals’ private business.”
“I know my place, Miss—I only spoke up because I was worried about you.”
“I’m fine. Just weary.”
Matron Wei’s voice carried from the outer hall. “Miss in there?”
“Right here, Matron—come on in!” Kuqiao called back.
Matron Wei entered alone, took one look at her wilted miss, and guessed why. Taking the chair Kuqiao set out, she chuckled. “Nothing major even happened—why are you drooping like a frostbitten eggplant?”
Even disasters sounded light out of Matron Wei’s mouth. Qianli Xun managed a weak reply. “Nothing major, sure—I’m just tired.”
Matron Wei turned to Kuqiao. “Keep watch outside and don’t let anyone in. I need a private word with Miss.”
Taking the hint, Kuqiao slipped outside to stand guard.
“What’s so secret you sent Kuqiao out, Matron?”
Matron Wei dropped her voice. “A few days ago I met a friend for tea at Yongtai Teahouse. Guess who I spotted?”
“Chen Haoyu? He’s learning tea-making there, so no surprise.”
“Eldest Young Master Chen—together with the woman who raised hell here today.”
Qianli Xun perked up at once. “Wait—what?”
“Going to Yongtai Teahouse separately would be nothing, yet they were sharing the same private booth.”
“How do you know?”
“My friend went upstairs to meet an acquaintance and saw a woman slip into Eldest Young Master’s booth—dressed head to toe like a Chunxiang Brothel girl. When the woman came downstairs later, I took a good look—same airs as Chun Jinqiao. Just now coming back to the manor, I saw the Judicial Office constables escorting that exact woman out.”
“If she knows our elder cousin, why come here raising hell?”
“You’ve got too good a heart to think ugly thoughts, Miss. If you ask me, her showing up today has Eldest Young Master’s fingerprints all over it—there’s more to this than meets the eye.”
The news revived Qianli Xun’s spirits, though her irritation lingered. “Even if Cousin is behind it, Chen Haoyu still started it! If he hadn’t pulled sleazy stunts, nobody could dig up dirt on him. I’m not letting him off easy.”
“Young Master was foolish before, yes, yet owning up and changing counts for plenty. Having his old misdeeds come home to roost now is actually a blessing—think of it as helping him square his accounts. Once every old debt is settled, good days lie ahead.”
“His mistakes—why should I help him clean them up? Back at Lin Manor you always sided with me! Why are you sticking up for Chen Haoyu now?”
“I’m always on your side, Miss—I only back what’s good for you. Helping Young Master clear his past makes the two of you real partners who can grow old together.”
For the first time, Qianli Xun pushed back. “Out of all the couples in the world, when did one spouse have to mop up the other’s sins just to grow old together? Look at my mother-in-law—did she ever have to clean up Father-in-law’s messes?”
Matron Wei’s face turned grave. “Every person walks a different road, Miss. The road you chose demands you walk beside Young Master while he makes amends.”
Qianli Xun had no comeback. She had chosen this road with open eyes, bumps and all. Everyone paid the toll for their own choices—so she might as well face the ugly messes head-on.
“Fine, Matron. I’ll help him clear his debts.”
Matron Wei smiled. “You’ve already started, Miss. Heard you sorted out those six maids yesterday—clean work, too.”
“Wait—Grandmother and my in-laws didn’t know! How did you hear?”
“Smart as you are, how’d you turn dense all of a sudden? A mob making a scene at the front gate, and you think the elders wouldn’t hear? You handled it so well they didn’t have to lift a finger, so why bring it up?”
“Sister-in-law warned Butler Wang and the servants not to breathe a word!”
“Granduncle Lu was right there, and you shut him down flat—think he wouldn’t go gripe to the elders and spill the whole gate scene?”
“You weren’t even in the manor then! How do you know every detail?”
Matron Wei owned up. “I’ve been at Chen Manor long enough to know plenty of the help. I’m friendly with Butler Wang—and Steward Fang over at Xinglong Pavilion too.”
A mischievous glint lit Qianli Xun’s eye. “Butler Wang has a wife, so he’s out. Steward Fang’s a widower—single and available! Thinking it over, Matron?”
Matron Wei froze a split second, then laughed. “If you’re back to cracking jokes with me, you’re fine. I’m off to bed.”
Qianli Xun called after her back, “Running off the second I hit a nerve? Guilty conscience! Just wait—I’m bringing it up again tomorrow! Heh!”
#CH 279
#TITLE: Haodong Hunts for Scarface by Night
Right after dusk, Chen Haodong had servants pack bedding, salve, and snacks, dismissed both servants and driver for the night, and drove the carriage toward the Judicial Office himself.
Once well clear of Chen Manor, he swung the horse toward Chunxiang Brothel instead. Parking down the street, he strolled past the front doors so he’d be spotted, then climbed back inside to wait.
Before long, Chun Jinchan popped open the carriage door. “Eldest Young Master hasn’t looked for me in ages—don’t tell me we’re fooling around in the carriage tonight?”
“Quit joking, I’m in no mood. Go fetch Scarface for me.”
“How funny—Scarface doesn’t work at Chunxiang Brothel. Why look for him here?”
“He hangs around here all the time—where else would I look?”
“Haven’t you heard? Ever since that tigress reined in your cousin, Scarface showed up once and vanished.”
“He hasn’t been by Yongtai Teahouse to see me either. Any idea where he went?”
“How should I know? Without Young Master Chen bankrolling them, rats like Scarface just drift around the streets.”
“Know where he lives?”
Chun Jinchan shook her head. “We never ask patrons where they live—only heard it’s somewhere on the east side.”
Panic pricked Chen Haodong: if the yamen got to Scarface first, he was sunk.
Chun Jinchan sidled closer. “What’s got you down, Eldest Young Master? Want me to keep you company tonight?”
“Not tonight—tomorrow, same time, same spot.”
She leaned against his chest. “Everything about you is great except you’re too sneaky. When can you stroll right into Chunxiang Brothel openly like your cousin used to?”
“Give me one more year.” He wrapped an arm around her. “If only my wife were half like you.”
“Don’t mention her when we’re together.”
After holding her a moment, he let go. “Head back inside. I still have to hit the Judicial Office.”
Chun Jinchan clearly knew what was up. “Jinlan’s little stunt?”
“Who else?”
“She really packs a punch.”
“Got you to thank too—without you egging her on, she wouldn’t have raised half that storm at Chen Manor.”
“Not just me,” Chun Jinchan said with a smirk. “Madam Jiang did the heavy lifting.”
“Madam Jiang?”
“Chun Jinmo.”
“Oh, her surname’s Jiang. Why’d she help me?”
“She wasn’t helping you—she was helping herself. Married Master Jiang, sure, yet she still pines for Second Young Master. That tigress marched in and hauled him off right under her nose, and he never came back. Bitter as gall, she jumped at using Chun Jinlan to stir up Chen Manor.”
“Makes sense. Slip back in before the madam comes looking and spots my carriage.”
Chun Jinchan threw him a sultry wink and hopped down. For a moment his pulse stirred, until the thought of Scarface doused it cold.
With Scarface drifting around the east quarter—not far from Chunxiang Brothel—he had to comb the streets tonight no matter what. Driving slow through the dark, scanning pedestrians, stalls, and storefronts, he finally struck gold: one of Haoyu’s old sidekicks stood scarfing soup dumplings by a street stand.
Pulling up, Chen Haodong hopped down and hurried over. “Where’s Scarface?”
Swallowing a dumpling whole, the lackey recognized him. “Oh, Eldest Young Master! Long time no see. Scarface is over at the vegetable market.”
Ditching him on the spot, Chen Haodong ducked into the side alley. Lanterns hung on bamboo poles over night vegetable stalls, crowds still shopping.
Scarface swaggered past with two lackeys lugging bamboo baskets, stuffing produce in free of charge while Scarface waved a club in a vendor’s face. “I like taking stuff for free—what’re you gonna do about it? Next time I walk up, load the baskets yourselves, got it?”
The vendors seethed in silence.
“Scarface!” Chen Haodong called. “Why rob them? Whatever vegetables you want, I’ll buy!”
Seeing his meal ticket, Scarface’s eyes crinkled into slits. Lowering his club, he jogged over. “Out after dark looking for me, Eldest Young Master?”
“Sure am. My carriage is right outside—let’s talk there.”
Dropping the shakedown, Scarface followed him out while his two sidekicks wandered off for dumplings.
Inside the carriage, Scarface grinned right away. “Don’t blame me for not stopping by Yongtai Teahouse, Eldest Young Master! I’d love to earn your silver, except you need fresh dirt for that, and since your cousin dropped the boys, I’ve got no news on him.”
“He’s stuck at the silk shop all day—I know his schedule better than you. No need for news.”
Scarface’s grin turned puzzled. “Then what’s the late-night rush?”
“Remember the girl you sold to that Yangzhou brothel?”
Scarface’s smile vanished. “Why bring her up?”
“She’s at Chunxiang Brothel now, calling herself Chun Jinlan.”
Scarface hadn’t set foot in Chunxiang Brothel much since Chen Haoyu reformed—lucky break, or he’d have bumped straight into a hornet’s nest. “Why tell me about her?”
“She stormed Chen Manor demanding Haoyu take her as a concubine or she’d make someone die. My cousin denied everything, so the Judicial Office stepped in and locked them both up. See these bundles? I’m on my way to deliver bedding to Haoyu.”
Scarface tensed. “How’d the Judicial Office get dragged in?”
“Chun Jinlan already named you. Tomorrow they’ll haul you in for questioning.”
Chen Haodong sounded breezy, yet Scarface’s stomach dropped—wait a minute, all he did was sell her! Putting on a casual air, Scarface countered, “How come Eldest Young Master isn’t nervous? You’re the one who defiled her and ordered me to sell her! If they start swinging bamboo paddles at the Judicial Office, I might not hold out and spill the truth!”
Chen Haodong had figured the old affair might resurface, yet he had scrubbed every trace a year ago—never expecting the Judicial Office to wade in and risk exposing him. Everything hinged on locking Scarface down.
“You pocketed every copper from selling her, and you swore not a third soul would ever hear.”
“I kept my word! I’m just scared of taking a beating at the Judicial Office!”
“Do as I say, and not one paddle will touch you.”
“I’m all ears, Eldest Young Master.”
Leaning close, Chen Haodong lowered his voice. “Take the whole rap yourself and confess upfront to the Judicial Investigator. Why would he beat a man who confesses?”
Realizing he was being cast as the fall guy, Scarface balked. “Not a chance! You stay clean while I rot in jail? Rape gets you executed! Asking me to take that rap is asking for my neck! No amount of silver buys my head!”
Expecting that pushback, Chen Haodong had his angle ready. “Who said anything about rape? Claim it was consensual! Hardest thing on earth to disprove—stick to that story and Chun Jinlan can’t touch you.”
Scarface chewed on it. “How much silver are we talking?”
Asking a price meant he was hooked. “Name it.”
“Twenty taels,” Scarface ventured.
*Cheap pauper—never seen real money.* If the man was taking the fall, pay him right. “I’ll give you a hundred taels.”
Scarface slid halfway off the bench. “No kidding?! A hundred taels?!”
“Have I ever gone back on my word?”
Scrambling back up, Scarface asked, “And if I lie in court to pin it on Second Young Master, I still get the hundred?”
“Of course—that’s Plan B, which you try first! Tomorrow in court, swear up and down Haoyu defiled Chun Jinlan and told you to sell her to Yangzhou. Only fall back on Plan A if Plan B fails. I guarantee you walk out whole.”
“Wish you’d led with Plan B instead of scaring me half to death with Plan A!”
“I prefer laying out the worst case first.”
“Leave tomorrow to me, Eldest Young Master—you’re clean as snow.”
Chen Haodong tossed him a pouch of silver. “Bonus spending money upfront.”
Grinning ear to ear, Scarface tucked it away. “Haven’t seen this much silver in ages—thanks on behalf of the boys!”
Chen Haodong turned sharp. “Do your buddies know anything about Chun Jinlan?”
“Remember? I sent them all away that night—they don’t know squat. I’d almost forgotten myself till you brought it up.”
“Good. Not a word to anyone. Off you go.”
Watching Scarface vanish into the dark, Chen Haodong exhaled and drove on to the Judicial Office to play the doting elder cousin.
At the Judicial Office gate, he spotted Qinggua pacing outside, torn over whether to knock. Needing hands to haul the bundles anyway, he reined in. “Qinggua? What are you doing here?”
Qinggua brightened. “Came to keep Young Master company, only I’m scared to go in.”
Hopping down, Chen Haodong said, “Grab the bedding, snacks, and salve from the carriage while I knock.”
“Eldest Young Master thinks of everything!” Grateful his master had such a decent cousin, Qinggua hoisted the bundles as Chen Haodong rapped three times.
A constable poked his head out. “What’s your business here this late?”
Chen Haodong pressed a silver ingot into his palm. “I’m Chen Haoyu’s elder cousin. The family’s worried about him and sent bedding.”
The constable broke into a beam. “A Chen! Prince Yi left orders to let any Chen family member right in. Step inside!”
Following him in with Qinggua loaded behind, Chen Haodong fished for details. “Is Prince Yi here?”
“Princess Consort Yi sent for him—His Highness went back to Youlin Retreat.”
“Chief Constable and Clerk Han?”
“Both asleep.”
“Can you authorize my drop-off?”
“Sure can. Our holding cells are just two converted back rooms—serious felons aren’t kept here, so any constable on duty has the keys.”
Prince Yi wasn’t here—better and better.
Guided to the rear courtyard, they found two padlocked rooms with tiny air vents cut high up on one wall. Unhooking a key from his belt, the constable unlocked one door. “I’m on watch tonight. Young Master Chen’s in here. Drop the stuff off—fifteen minutes tops.”
“Much obliged.”
Chen Haodong stepped in with Qinggua right behind, and the constable shut the door from outside.
Inside stood just a brick kang bed, a table with a flickering oil lamp, and not even a chair. Lying on the kang was Chen Haoyu.
“Young Master!” Qinggua blurted.
Sitting right up, Chen Haoyu blinked. “Brother Dong? What brings you here?”
“Uncle and Aunt were worried sick and sent me with bedding.” Chen Haodong glanced around. “Managing all right in here?”
“I’m fine anywhere—take life as it comes.”
Staring in misery, Qinggua forgot to put the bundles down. Chen Haodong chided him, “Put that stuff down already! Want your master sleeping on bare brick?”
Setting the bedding on the kang, Qinggua peered at his arm. “Does it hurt, Young Master?”
“Not a bit. How’s Young Madam?”
“No idea about Young Madam, I just see you looking rough! Eldest Young Master, could you talk to the guard outside and let me stay here with Young Master?”
“I don’t need a babysitter—go home,” Chen Haoyu refused.
“Let me stay! At least you’ll have someone to talk to!”
#CH 280
#TITLE: Chen Haodong’s Crocodile Tears
Touchiness softened into gratitude inside Chen Haoyu. Locked in this bare room, Qinggua still came running to keep him company—a truly loyal servant, unlike the fair-weather crowd who would have bolted faster than rabbits once the silver stopped flowing. Ashamed of how he had treated Qinggua before, he couldn’t bring himself to say anything sappy and fell back on his old teasing. “Call you shameless, and you prove me right.”
The familiar insult sounded sweeter than praise. Qinggua beamed. “I love being shameless for Young Master!”
Sick of their sappy banter—and eager to visit the cell next door without Qinggua tagging along—Chen Haodong cut in. “All right, knock it off. I’ll talk to the constable and toss him extra silver if needed. Easy fix.”
“Thank you, Eldest Young Master!” Qinggua cheered.
“Supplies and company delivered—time I got going before the guard loses patience.” Stepping out and shutting the door behind him, Chen Haodong slipped two more silver ingots into the constable’s hand. “That servant always waits on Haoyu. Mind letting him stay the night?”
Two ingots landing straight in his palm stretched the constable’s grin ear to ear. “No big deal—let him stay.” Locking the door, he turned to find Chen Haodong still lingering. Two ingots at once meant another favor coming. “Something else?”
“Sharp eyes, Brother!” Chen Haodong flattered. “That girl locked next door is a fierce one—she stabbed Haoyu’s arm. Think I could slip in a minute to talk some sense into her?”
The constable hesitated. “You’re not her kin, so it’s against the rules... tell you what, for the silver, I’ll look the other way. Fifteen minutes max.”
“Much obliged, Brother.”
The padlock clicked open, the door swung wide, and Chen Haodong stepped inside before the constable shut it behind him.
Chun Jinlan was already waiting right inside the doorway. “You came, Eldest Young Master! Heard the noise next door and knew you’d drop in to see me.”
Chen Haodong put on his straitlaced gentleman act—the only mask that worked on a woman who hated men so fiercely she refused clients at the brothel and only survived under Chun Jinmo’s wing. Stepping back a polite pace, he said, “I can’t stand seeing anyone suffer. Came to check on Second Brother, so I stopped in to see you too, Miss Jinlan.”
That deliberate distance filled her with respect. “Thank you, Eldest Young Master.”
He piled on the noble act. “My brother’s wrongdoing broke my heart. Hoping to give him a chance to make amends, I tracked you down everywhere until Heaven let me find you—all so Haoyu would take you in, giving you a secure home and letting him right his wrong.”
“I know your good intentions, Eldest Young Master.”
“No—today I realized I blundered. I thought making a scene would get the elders to smooth things over and make Haoyu take you in. Instead both of you got locked up here. It’s my fault you’re suffering.”
“Don’t blame yourself—you meant well.”
Glancing around the twin cell—bare straw mat on the kang, not even a thin blanket—he cranked up the sympathy. “I came alone and smuggled a thin quilt for you, only Haoyu’s servant caught me at the gate and hauled everything into his room...” Shrugging off his own outer robe, he laid it on the kang. “Good thing the weather’s warming up. Take my robe to cover your stomach tonight so you don’t catch a chill.”
Staring at the robe, Chun Jinlan’s eyes welled up. Ever since being sold to a brothel, every man she met had only used and abused her; never once had a man looked at her without lust and treated her with such kindness. Dropping to her knees, she pleaded, “You’re such a good man, Eldest Young Master! Since you pity me, why not let me belong to you instead?”
Realizing he had laid the act on too thick, Chen Haodong recoiled. “Out of the question! Not that I look down on you, yet Haoyu’s the one who wronged you and must answer for it—besides, my wife would never allow a concubine.”
Tears spilled down her cheeks. “Why can’t I ever meet a good man? The one time I do, he’s already married and devoted to his wife!”
With fifteen minutes ticking down and her emotions primed, it was time to plant the hook. “Get up—I’ve got something crucial to tell you.”
Wiping her eyes, she rose. “Go ahead, Eldest Young Master.”
“Haoyu’s wife is jealous and won’t tolerate a concubine. My bet is she’s out tonight buying off Scarface and Haoyu’s cronies to give false testimony tomorrow.”
“What do I do?”
“If all else fails, threaten to take your own life.”
“I don’t want to die!”
“Nobody’s asking you to die—just scare them! Prince Yi is soft-hearted. Seeing you put your life on the line, he won’t swallow those witnesses’ lies; he’ll dig to the bottom, get you justice, and force Haoyu to take you in. Once Prince Yi backs you, nobody dares object.”
Doubt flickered in her eyes. “Will Prince Yi really stand up for me?”
“He cares about commoners more than anyone—heard about Lazybones Village?”
“I did.”
“Then what’s to doubt? A royal lowering himself to visit Chen Manor without a single complaint—you saw it with your own eyes! Plus Chief Constable Wen is a top martial artist; he’ll stop you at the last second for sure. You just have to make the act look real.”
Hardening her resolve, Chun Jinlan nodded. “All right, I’ll do it. Only Chen Haoyu’s wife took my dagger...”
Chen Haodong pulled a hidden blade from his clothes. “Already brought you a spare. Here’s hoping tomorrow makes you one of the Chens—a good home for you, and a chance for Haoyu to straighten up.”
Taking the twin dagger, she tucked it into her sleeve just like the first one.
Satisfied she was hooked, he added one last warning before leaving. “If anyone asks why I saw you tonight, just say I came urging you to drop the charges against Haoyu and the Chens. Not another word—I mean well, yet people love to gossip.”
“Don’t worry, Eldest Young Master. You’re a good man—I’d never drag you down.”
His eyes fell on the outer robe on the kang. Foolish to leave physical proof behind. Walking over, he scooped it up. “Actually, leaving my robe here is a bad idea—these Judicial Office men are sharp, and if they suspect anything improper between us, it’ll hurt your case. Better I take it back.”
Slipping it back on, he left Chun Jinlan disappointed yet admiring his caution. “You think of everything, Eldest Young Master. Knowing you care is enough for me.”
Smoothing his sleeves, he sneered inside at how easy she was to play. Everything was in place.
Stepping back out into the yard while the constable relocked the door, Chen Haodong handed over one final silver ingot. “Stubborn woman—wasted my breath trying to talk her round. Pretend tonight never happened, Brother, and don’t breathe a word to anyone lest it bother Prince Yi.”
Pocketing the silver, the constable beamed. “Not worth mentioning—set your mind at ease!”
Chen Haodong strode out of the Judicial Office without a care in the world.
#CH 281
#TITLE: Contradictory Testimony
The next morning, Chief Constable Wen brought in the witnesses, and Shen Jianchen reopened Chen Haoyu’s hearing.
Chen Jianglin and Chen Haodong attended from the spectators’ seats behind the constables. Qianli Xun stayed away—just as well, Shen Jianchen thought, sparing her the ugly details.
Constables escorted Chen Haoyu and Chun Jinlan in to kneel.
“Bring in the witnesses!” Wen Xin called.
Two constables led three men forward: Scarface, plus two young masters who had grilled meat by West Lake and played chuiwan with Chen Haoyu. All three knelt. “Greetings, Your Lordship!”
Shen Jianchen questioned the two young masters first. “One day last spring, did you send for Chen Haoyu to return from the countryside?”
The barbecue enthusiast who had once flirted with Hongdou spoke up. “Remember it clear as day! My birthday, the third day of the third month. Wanted the gang together, heard Young Master Chen was out at his country house, and figured it wouldn’t be lively without him—so I sent someone to fetch him back.”
“Did he stay with you all night?”
The second young master answered. “Yes, Your Lordship! We partied all night in the entertainment quarters—Young Master Chen stayed with us till dawn and even napped there before heading off.”
With their testimony favoring Chen Haoyu, Chun Jinlan was sure the poetess had bought them off and burst out, “Don’t believe them, Your Lordship! They must’ve taken Chen Haoyu’s silver to lie for him!”
“I was locked up all night!” Chen Haoyu shot back. “When did I get a chance to see them?”
“Your wife had plenty of time and chances! Wouldn’t she buy them off for you?”
Shen Jianchen would not let anyone smear Qianli Xun. Crack! His gavel slammed the desk. “Another outburst and you each get twenty strokes of the paddle!”
Chun Jinlan clammed up in fright.
Shen Jianchen turned to Scarface. “You’re Scarface?”
“Yes, Your Lordship.”
“Used to run with Chen Haoyu?”
“Used to. Young Master Chen won’t hang with us anymore.”
“Recognize the woman kneeling here?”
Scarface nodded straightaway. “Sure do—I’m the one who sold her to the Yangzhou brothel.”
Seething since Scarface walked in, Chun Jinlan snapped at his admission. Since he knelt right beside her, she threw herself sideways and rained fists on him. “You ruined my life! I’ll kill you!”
Not daring to hit back, Scarface dodged and took the weak blows—until she lunged to sink her teeth into him and two constables hauled her off and pinned her down.
“One more stunt, Chun Jinlan, and you go back in ropes,” Shen Jianchen warned. She bit back her rage, and the constables stepped back.
“Scarface, why did you sell her to a Yangzhou brothel?”
Scarface launched right into his lie. “Young Master Chen saw how pretty she was, couldn’t hold back, and forced himself on her. Only she had a nasty temper and wasn’t any fun, so he got disgusted, knocked her out, and told me to haul her to Yangzhou and sell her.”
“Was that the night of the third day of the third month last year?”
“Sure was! Remember clear as day—Young Master Chen took us out to fly kites that day, spotted her washing clothes by the creek, and had the boys snatch her into the country house.”
Much as she despised Scarface, his words backed her up, cooling her anger.
Now Chen Haoyu panicked and snapped at him, “I never treated you shabby—why spout lies?! That day when I went back to town, I told you boys to watch her till I returned the next day! Don’t tell me you did the dirty deed yourself, sold her off, and pinned it on me?!”
Scarface kept spinning. “You treated me decent, Young Master Chen, and I haven’t forgotten—yet facts are facts, and I can’t lie in court! Besides, how would I dare touch a girl you snatched?”
Chun Jinlan jumped in. “He sold me to Yangzhou with his own hands, Your Lordship—his word counts most!”
The barbecue young master scratched his head. “Hold on—Young Master Chen was with us all night! Can he split himself in two?”
Three witnesses, two clashing stories—somebody was lying.
Shen Jianchen weighed their motives. Flighty as the two young masters were, they had no stake in the case and zero reason to perjure themselves. Scarface, on the other hand, was neck-deep in it and had driven the girl to Yangzhou himself—maybe he really had done the deed and blamed Haoyu.
“Scarface, you said ‘we’ earlier. So other men came with Chen Haoyu?”
Wary of the sharp investigator, Scarface chose his words with care. “Yes, others came, yet after Young Master Chen returned to town, the rest went home—leaving just me at the house.”
“So you admit Chen Haoyu got called back to town?”
“Sure do—only Young Master Chen couldn’t stop thinking about the girl, came back in the dead of night, finished his business, and ordered me to sell her.”
Shen Jianchen shifted to Chen Haoyu. “Were there any servants at that country house?”
The question jolted Chen Haodong’s hand; tea sloshed from his bowl onto his sleeve. Setting the bowl down quick, he brushed his robe and flashed his uncle an awkward smile. *Relax—the servants had all been pulled away that night, and the one caretaker left slept like a dead pig. Nobody knows except Scarface.*
Chen Jianglin listened with bated breath; if someone could punch a hole in Scarface’s story, his son walked free.
“Every outside Chen residence has servants,” Chen Haoyu replied. “That country house included.”
Shen Jianchen addressed the hall. “With conflicting witness accounts, no verdict can be reached today. Adjourned until the Judicial Office gathers further proof.”
Chun Jinlan felt the hidden dagger in her sleeve—still too unclear to play her trump card.
Once constables led Chen Haoyu and Chun Jinlan away and the witnesses left, Chen Jianglin and Chen Haodong rose to bow.
“Thank you for taking trouble over my wayward son, Your Highness,” Chen Jianglin said. “If he truly committed this crime, punish him by law—I will not shield him.”
Shen Jianchen cared nothing for Chen Haoyu himself, only for Qianli Xun—if Haoyu swung for this, she’d be widowed right after her wedding. For her sake, he hoped Haoyu was clean.
“No one knows a son like his father,” Shen Jianchen said. “I believe Master Chen already knows the truth in his heart; we just need solid proof.”
Impressed by the young prince’s insight, Chen Jianglin bowed. “Your Highness speaks true.”
Shen Jianchen turned his eyes on Chen Haodong—standing there pious and silent, yet tension leaked from his gaze. Time to poke him. “Does Eldest Young Master Chen know Chen Haoyu well?”
Caught off guard, Chen Haodong stiffened. “Haoyu is my cousin—of course I know him well.”
“Then whose testimony do you think is false?”
Chen Haodong put on an earnest front. “Since Your Highness asks, I’ll speak bold: Haoyu and I are like brothers, so of course I side with him and hope with all my heart that Scarface is lying to frame him.”
Shen Jianchen played right along. “Splendid! Since you hope for that so much, I’ll have my men dig up proof that Scarface perjured himself.”
Panic tightened Chen Haodong’s throat. “Pay no mind to my words, Your Highness—I only spoke out of family feeling.”
“What, don’t you want me to prove Scarface lied?”
Flustered, Chen Haodong stammered, “That isn’t what I meant...”
“Relax—just asking.” Shen Jianchen looked back at Chen Jianglin. “Master Chen, have you heard the saying ‘calamity starts inside one’s own walls’?”
Baffled, Chen Jianglin answered, “I have.”
“It doesn’t apply just to imperial houses—ordinary households too, most of all wealthy clans like the Chens. Run along now; I still have evidence to hunt down.”
Dismissed, both men bowed and backed out of the hall.
All the way out, Chen Jianglin chewed over those words. *Calamity inside one’s own walls—applicable to the Chens?* Climbing into the carriage beside his anxious nephew, a wild thought flashed across his mind: *Was Prince Yi hinting Haodong had a hand in Haoyu’s mess?*
He brushed it off almost at once. Absurd! The cousins got along fine, and why on earth would Haodong sabotage Haoyu? Well—for the business inheritance, maybe. Yet Haofei cared only for imperial exams, and Haoyu’s wild past had already sunk him with the elders—just look at how Chen Lu chewed out Haoyu and his wife the other day. Haodong already had the inheritance locked up, and he knew it. Why risk hurting Haoyu?
In the end Chen Jianglin concluded royals just tossed out cryptic remarks for show—best not to overthink it.
#CH 282
#TITLE: Two Stalkers, One Target
Back inside the Judicial Office, Shen Jianchen issued his orders. “Chief Constable Wen, take men out to the Chens’ country residence and comb the room where Chun Jinlan was held—we might turn up fresh evidence. Clerk Han, take constables and track down Scarface’s buddies to ask what happened that day.”
“At your command, Your Highness!” Both bowed and led their squads out.
Just then the constable who had let Chen Haodong in the night before stepped up and laid a small cloth packet on the prince’s desk. “You’re a prophet, Your Highness! Eldest Young Master Chen really did show up last night—here are the four taels of silver he slipped me, didn’t pocket a single coin.”
Shen Jianchen picked up the packet and pressed it straight back into the constable’s chest. “Since Eldest Young Master Chen gave it to you, keep it.”
Startled, thinking it a trap, the constable waved both hands. “No, no, no! A Judicial Office constable can’t take bribes!”
Shen Jianchen smiled. “Take it—consider it my reward.”
Relieved, the constable tucked the silver away, beaming. “Can Your Highness really tell the future? Eldest Young Master Chen actually went in to see Chun Jinlan too!”
“What did he say to her?”
“Voices were low and the door’s thick, so I only caught bits—sounded like Eldest Young Master Chen was the one who fetched her to Lin’an, and she even knelt begging to be his concubine.”
Everything clicked into place. If Chen Haodong brought Chun Jinlan in, ten to one he bought Scarface too—why else would Haoyu’s own lackey knife him in court? Chen Haodong was the key; somebody had to tail him.
Tailing him undetected took real light-foot skill. Wen Xin could fight yet lacked agility, and Bai Feng was out in Zhuli Village building the cottage. Shen Jianchen decided to go himself.
Dismissing the constable, he changed into plainclothes in his quarters, still found them too fine, and walked into the bustling market to pick out an ordinary dark-colored robe at a modest tailor shop. Changing inside the fitting room, he liked the simple, comfortable fit.
Stepping out, the shop clerk beamed. “A natural clothes-hanger, Young Master—even plain cloth looks high-class on you!”
Shen Jianchen smiled. “Got a scrap left over from this same fabric?”
“Lucky break—plenty of trimmings! Let me grab a piece.” Fetching a black strip from the back room, the clerk handed it over. Pleased, Shen Jianchen tucked it inside his robe, paid in silver, and added, “Have your shop deliver my changed robes to the Judicial Office.”
Staring at the silver, the clerk gaped. “Wait—aren’t you the Judicial Investigator?”
Shen Jianchen walked out without answering. The clerk muttered to himself, “Same bearing, just way too young—and buying cheap cloth like that? Couldn’t be him.”
Hiring a carriage, Shen Jianchen staked out the Chen silk shop first, saw no sign of Chen Haodong, and collared a clerk stepping out. “Excuse me, is Eldest Young Master Chen in today?”
The clerk eyed him up and down. “Who’s asking?”
Shen Jianchen bluffed on the spot. “Judicial Office runner—the Judicial Investigator wants Eldest Young Master for questioning.”
The clerk turned polite at once. “He didn’t come in this morning—heard he went to the Judicial Office.”
“He left after the hearing. Didn’t come here?”
“No, sir, though he’s been spending most days at the tea shop lately. Try there.”
Thanking him, Shen Jianchen rode over to the Chen tea shop. After a long wait, Chen Haodong finally stepped out and climbed into a Chen carriage. Just as Shen Jianchen moved to board his hired rig, a familiar figure in young-master attire strolled out of the adjacent tea stall.
*So you suspect Chen Haodong too—great minds think alike.*
Stepping right into her path, he murmured, “Dressed loud as a peacock—I spotted you in one glance, and you expect to fool Chen Haodong?”
After Matron Wei’s tip last night, Qianli Xun had suspected Chen Haodong too and slipped into one of her two sets of men’s robes to tail him. Stung that he blew her cover on sight, she fired back, “And you think cheap plainclothes hide the fact that you’re Prince Yi?”
No time for bickering. “Same target—hop in my carriage?”
“What are you standing around for? We’ll lose him!” Qianli Xun marched straight over and climbed aboard first.
Smiling to himself, Shen Jianchen climbed in after her and told the driver, “Tail that Chen carriage ahead.” Paid handsomely, the driver asked zero questions and kept a safe distance behind.
It was their first time riding together in a carriage since crossing over—so close. In the old days Shen Jianchen would have grabbed her hand and poured out sweet words. Now both were married, and he hadn’t even kept his vow of chastity; he had no right left. Besides, the second he climbed in, she shifted over to put polite space between them.
He kept it civil. “Why are you tailing Chen Haodong?”
Same goal, and he was the judge on the case—no point hiding anything. “Matron Wei spotted him drinking tea with Chun Jinlan in a private booth at Yongtai Teahouse. Figured they’re in cahoots, so I came to dig up clues.”
After a pause, he asked, “For Chen Haoyu?”
“He’s my husband,” she answered without blinking. “Helping him is helping myself.”
Words slipped out before he could stop them. “I’ll help you. So long as you’re happy, I’m happy.”
A tug of warmth hit her—then Mengrong’s bitter words echoed back, snapping her awake. “No! Don’t go out of your way for me. Put your energy into treating Mengrong right.”
Mention of Qiao Mengrong reminded him of the pamphlets. “About those pamphlets—I apologize on Mengrong’s behalf. Don’t hold it against her.”
“I never really blamed her. I just miss what we used to have—knowing our friendship can never go back.”
“My fault.”
“Not yours—just fate playing tricks.” Sitting with that helplessness a beat, she called out, “Prince Yi.”
“Why not use my name?”
“Zhao Pu.”
“You know that’s not the name I mean.”
“From now on, you’re Prince Yi, Zhao Pu, to me. That other name—I’ll lock it away in my heart and look back on it now and then.”
Silence hung a moment before he nodded. “Deal. I’ll lock ‘Qianli Xun’ away in my heart too, and savor it now and then.”
They shared a quiet smile.
Lightening the air, she asked, “How did you start suspecting Chen Haodong?”
Shen Jianchen puffed up to lift the mood. “Pure genius—razor-sharp observation, seeing right through the surface to spot Chen Haodong’s guilt at one glance.”
She rolled her eyes. “Keep bragging, then—forget I asked!”
“All right, all right. At Chen Manor I caught Chen Haodong and Chun Jinlan trading shady looks. Then last night he used delivering bedding to Haoyu as cover to sneak into Chun Jinlan’s cell. Dead giveaway.”
“Chen Haodong can’t be that dumb—meeting Chun Jinlan right in the middle of an investigation? Not scared of getting caught?”
“He’s not dumb—I’m just so brilliant I make him look dumb.”
She scoffed, secretly relieved they could still banter like old friends.
Lifting the window curtain, Shen Jianchen saw dusk settling and open countryside rolling past. “Why’s Chen Haodong heading outside the city at this hour?”
“Plays the saint in public—who knows what dirt he gets up to in the dark.”
“Why do you think he’s setting Chen Haoyu up?”
“The Chen family business inheritance, obviously.”
“How’d you figure that?”
“I’m not blind—takes two minutes of watching to see it.”
“Eldest Master Chen doesn’t seem to see it. Why not tip him off when you get home?”
“Not touching Chen business politics with a ten-foot pole. Doesn’t concern me, and Chen Haoyu has zero ambition for it anyway. All I want is this mess over so life goes back to normal.”
Concern shaded his voice. “Doesn’t Chen Haoyu’s mess upset you?”
“What’s to be upset about? I knew his track record the day I married him and braced for everything.” She wasn’t about to let Shen Jianchen think she was disillusioned with Haoyu and nurse false hopes.
Reading between the lines, he thought, *No need to put on a front with me. I won’t act reckless anymore, and I’ve stopped hunting for a tree-climbing pig. Still, if I ever stumble across one, I’m not giving up—though odds are I’ll never see a pig climb a tree.*
The carriage halted. The driver called in, “Young Master, that carriage stopped at a house ahead, and the passenger went inside.”
Both hopped down, and Shen Jianchen handed over silver. “Pull off the road and wait—we’ll need a ride back once we’re done.”
“Don’t worry, Young Master—I spotted a thick, low-branched tree on the way in. I’ll park underneath; once dark hits, nobody’ll see us.” The driver eased the rig away on quiet wheels.
“Not bad,” Qianli Xun praised. “Your driver’s got solid security instincts.”
“Look who hired him.”
“Bragging again?”
Shen Jianchen pulled the scrap of black cloth from his robe and tied it over his face. “Spying at night takes a mask so nobody spots us. Only got one—what about you?”
Qianli Xun fished out a silk handkerchief. “Covered.”
“Way too bright—and look at your loud outfit! Wait out here while I go in.”
“I’m coming too. Almost pitch dark—who’s going to notice?”
Never one to fight her stubborn streak, he gave in. “Fine—stick close.”
“Why stick close? Think I can’t climb a wall myself?” Before the words left her mouth, she sprang cleanly atop the courtyard wall with her light-foot technique. Shen Jianchen kicked off and landed right beside her.
She whispered, “Small place—just scanned it, zero servants. Think this is the Chens’ country house?”
“Doubt it,” he murmured back. “The Chens’ country house would be way bigger and staffed with servants.”
“How would you know? You’ve never seen it.”
“Chen Haoyu testified there are servants at the country house—simple deduction.”
Before she could argue, carriage wheels rumbled up outside. In sync, both vaulted onto the roof and crouched low to peek out.
Twilight still lingered enough to spot the flashy carriage—identical to the Chunxiang Brothel rig Chun Jinmo had arrived in.
“Chunxiang Brothel,” Qianli Xun muttered. “Chen Haodong’s sneaking out to the sticks to meet a courtesan—no wonder Eldest Sister-in-law despises him.”
Shen Jianchen watched in silence as a woman stepped down, pushed open the gate, and entered: Chun Jinchan.
Chen Haodong stepped out of the house and swept her into his arms, speaking loud and loose since they had the place to themselves. “At the Judicial Office and the tea shop all day, all I could think about was how wild you are—come inside and show me a good time!”
Chun Jinchan spoke just as blunt. “I turned down clients seven straight days and scrubbed myself spotless waiting for you—you’d better pay for all seven days, or the madam will pitch a fit.”
“When have I ever been stingy with silver?” Covering her face with kisses, he hauled her indoors.
Cheeks heating, Qianli Xun shifted back. “Just a sleazy tryst—we’re not learning anything useful. I’m heading back—”
Shen Jianchen caught her arm on reflex, then let go at her awkward look. “People always talk after—he might spill something vital. Stay and listen.”
“Fine. We’ll listen.”
Picking a spot above the bedchamber, Shen Jianchen lifted a roof tile and peered through—right onto Chen Haodong half-undressed and tumbling across the bed with Chun Jinchan.
#CH 283
#TITLE: Caught in the Act
Qianli Xun refused to look—the sounds rising through the gap left zero doubt what was happening below. Heart hammering and face burning, she kicked herself for staying, yet her feet felt nailed to the tiles.
Glancing over, she saw Shen Jianchen peering right through the hole without blinking, neither blushing nor flustered, cool as a spectator at a fair.
Thank goodness the handkerchief hid her red face. She whispered with a scoff, “Hmph! Staring so intent—spoken like a man who’s already consummated his marriage.”
Shen Jianchen jerked his gaze away, sheepish. “I didn’t want to miss anything important they might say.”
“Hmph! Use your head—they’re busy rolling around, not sharing secrets! If you want to watch, own up to it like an honest man.”
Flustered, he gave in. “Fine, fine—I won’t look.”
Sitting straight, he tilted his face up at the bright moon. She knew about his consummation—Bai Feng must have told Kuqiao, who told her.
Keeping his voice barely a breath so nobody below heard, he asked, “Do you blame me?”
Caught off guard, she shot him a puzzled look.
Meeting her eyes in the moonlight, he murmured, “I broke my word. I didn’t keep myself chaste.”
Oh—that vow he had made before returning to Bianliang. It had touched her at the time, even lingered in her thoughts now and then, yet it had never been realistic, so she hadn’t held him to it. “I never took it seriously—so you didn’t break your word.”
Unsure whether to feel crushed or relieved, he gave a wry smile. “What about you and Chen Haoyu? Have you consummated too?”
Determined to snuff out any lingering hope in him, she fibbed. “Of—of course.” Nerves over the lie made her shift her hand off her knee—bumping the loosened roof tile.
Clatter! The tile slid down the eaves and shattered into shards in the yard.
“Who’s out there?!” Chen Haodong barked from inside.
Quick as lightning, Shen Jianchen let out a crisp “Meow!”
Chun Jinchan’s voice followed. “Just a stray cat, Eldest Young Master—ignore it and keep going.”
Lush, giggling laughter floated back up from inside.
Neither spoke after that, sitting side by side on the roof beneath the moon, lost in private thoughts. Qianli Xun fumed at the racket below and felt awful for Ye Anya—earlier she had worried over her sister-in-law wanting a divorce, yet walking away from Chen Haodong was clearly the smartest move possible. Meanwhile, Shen Jianchen ached over thinking both of them had moved on; nine times out of ten life denied you what you wanted most.
At last the commotion below quieted. Chen Haodong lay back with his eyes shut in smug bliss while Chun Jinchan draped herself over him.
“Eldest Young Master’s vigor could sweep half the women in town off their feet,” she purred. “What’s wrong with your wife, not appreciating what she’s got?”
“Thought you didn’t like bringing her up?”
“Now and then doesn’t hurt.”
“Once I inherit the Chen family business, I’ll find a chance for you to teach her how to serve a man properly.”
“No way! What if she learns and you stop coming to see me?”
“Never—nobody matches your fire. I couldn’t give you up.”
Giggling in delight, Chun Jinchan asked, “Any chance Jinlan becomes Chen Haoyu’s concubine?”
Finally—the real goods! Both leaned over the hole at the same instant and knocked foreheads with a dull thud. Wincing, they clutched their brows until the sting faded, and Shen Jianchen yielded the spot to her—the voices below carried loud enough anyway.
Pressing her ear close, Qianli Xun caught Chen Haodong’s answer. “Hard to say. Today’s hearing didn’t go smooth. Scarface confessed everything, yet two of Haoyu’s friends swore he was with them all night. With clashing testimonies, the Judicial Investigator is hunting more proof—might not go my way.”
“Who do you hope is lying, Eldest Young Master?”
“You already know—why ask?”
“That’s why you went looking for Scarface last night?”
“Of course. Scared Scarface would side with my cousin and perjure himself, so I laid out the stakes, reasoned with him, and got him to swear he’d tell the truth.”
“So those two young masters lied?”
“Makes sense—they party with Haoyu all the time. Covering for a buddy is normal.”
“Why not tell the Judicial Investigator those two are lying?”
“Prince Yi is the Judicial Investigator—think I carry that kind of pull? Besides, courts run on proof, not hot air.”
So Chen Haodong was definitely behind Chun Jinlan’s outburst, yet nothing proved he paid Scarface to lie.
Below, the pair started getting rowdy all over again. Too embarrassed to keep listening—and needing to get back before any Chen elder noticed her missing from Moxiang Courtyard—Qianli Xun stood. “Nothing left worth hearing. Leave the rest to you—I’m out.”
“Take the carriage.”
She accepted without fuss and vaulted off into the night.
Left alone, Shen Jianchen decided to catch Chen Haodong off guard and shake the truth out of him. Dropping into the yard, he pocketed the black mask and stepped up to the door. Assuming they had the house to themselves, the pair hadn’t even latched it—a clear crack showed light inside.
He pushed the wooden door open with a slow creak.
“Who’s there?!” Chen Haodong yelped in panic.
Stopping in the outer hall, Shen Jianchen spoke loud and cool. “Sneaking out for a tryst—having a grand time, Eldest Young Master Chen?”
That steady, unmistakable voice sent cold sweat pouring down Chen Haodong’s back. Scrambling off Chun Jinchan, he yanked his clothes on crooked, mind blank and knees like water, and stumbled into the outer room.
Prince Yi stood right there. Dropping to his knees, Chen Haodong shook head to toe. “Your—Your Highness... what brings you here?”
Shen Jianchen gave a half-truth. “Out for an evening stroll when I stumbled right onto your tryst—and overheard a few things I shouldn’t have.”
Cold sweat beaded Chen Haodong’s brow. “My wife... treats me like ice, so I had no choice but to call a courtesan from Chunxiang Brothel here... Please keep this quiet, Your Highness!”
“I couldn’t care less about your bedroom affairs. I interrupted your fun to ask a few questions.”
“Ask anything, Your Highness.”
“Did you dig up Chun Jinlan and put her up to making a scene at Chen Manor?”
No hiding it after what the prince overheard. “Yes... I egged her on.”
“Over the Chen family business?”
“Yes—though I meant well too! Giving Haoyu a chance to reform and finding poor Miss Jinlan a home—her suffering tore at my heart. Plus, sure, it helped me out.”
“Hmph! Slick excuses.”
“Not excuses—straight from the heart!” Catching his breath, Chen Haodong calmed down; being caught cheating and scheming for the family business was embarrassing, not fatal.
“Did you see Scarface last night?”
“I did.”
“What did you say to him?”
Thinking a beat, Chen Haodong answered smooth. “I feared he’d perjure himself in court and warned him lying would get him harsher punishment once exposed. Told him to tell the truth—that way the Chens would let Haoyu take Chun Jinlan in to save his neck. Best outcome for all.”
“And do you believe his testimony today was true?”
“Every word.”
“Are you trying to get Chen Haoyu executed? Rape is a capital crime under Song law.”
Chen Haodong’s forehead nearly grazed the floor. “We’re family—how could I wish him dead?! I only pushed Chun Jinlan to force him to take a courtesan as concubine, never dreamed it would land at the Judicial Office! Please urge the Chen elders to settle this privately, Your Highness!”
“The Chen elders came to the Judicial Office because they trust me and refuse to coddle a guilty kinsman. I admire their integrity—why would I urge them to sweep it under the rug?”
Chen Haodong broke into crocodile tears. “Waaah... if Haoyu gets sentenced to death, it’ll be on my head! Spare his life, Your Highness!”
Every word confirmed Chen Haodong would stab his own cousin in the back for the family fortune—handing the Chen empire to a snake like him spelled disaster. Still, his answers were airtight for now. “Shame you didn’t become an actor, Eldest Young Master Chen. We’re done here.”
Relieved, Chen Haodong called after his retreating back, “Please keep tonight’s matter confidential, Your Highness!”
Shen Jianchen scoffed to himself—doing dirty deeds and begging witnesses to keep quiet!
Vaulting up into a tall tree outside, knowing Qianli Xun had taken the carriage back to town, he leaped treetop to treetop toward Youlin Retreat under the moonlight—only to spot Wen Xin marching beneath him with a squad of constables after ten trees.
Dropping right in front of them, he asked, “Still on duty this late, Chief Constable Wen?”
Startled by the prince dropping from the sky, the men bowed. “Greetings, Your Highness!”
“At ease. Where are you headed?”
“The Chens’ country house is right nearby,” Wen Xin explained. “Turned up nothing in daylight, so since the crime happened at night, I figured we’d inspect it after dark—might catch something fresh.”
“I’ll tag along and take a look.”
Back at the tryst house, Chun Jinchan straightened her clothes and helped Chen Haodong up. “Think he saw us going at it?”
Collapsing into a chair, Chen Haodong muttered, “Odds are yes.”
Chun Jinchan giggled. “Doesn’t bother me—serving a prince along with Eldest Young Master is a bonus!”
“How can you still laugh?!” Glancing around, he snapped, “This place is burned—I’ll have someone sell it tomorrow.”
“Good riddance—dusty, never cleaned, and sneaking around like rats gets old fast.”
Ignoring her gripe, he tossed out several bank notes. “Take these and head back to town. I’ll sit a while longer.”
Eyes lighting up at the silver notes, Chun Jinchan tucked them inside her bodice. “Generous as ever! Send word once you find a new spot—somewhere closer to town, not out in the sticks.” Planting a kiss on his forehead, she swayed out.
Alone in the chair, Chen Haodong replayed every word he had spoken to Chun Jinchan and Prince Yi—not a single slip. Exhaling long, he muttered, “Prince Yi showing up proves he’s onto me. Sharp prince—locked on this fast! Good thing I covered my tracks clean; he won’t dig up a shred of proof.”
Meanwhile, the Chens’ country residence loomed ahead—smaller than the main manor in town, yet rivaling Youlin Retreat in size and kept spotless despite sitting empty.
Greeting the constables at the gate, the caretaker noticed the plainly dressed young man whose presence commanded Wen Xin’s deference, guessed it was the Judicial Investigator, and bowed low. “Greetings, Your Lordship.”
“Take His Lordship and me back to the room where Young Master Chen locked people up,” Wen Xin ordered.
“Right this way, Your Lordship.”
Walking along, Shen Jianchen asked, “How many servants work here?”
“Not many—fifty or sixty.”
“Chief Constable Wen came by today, so you know why we’re here?”
“Yes, Your Lordship—Second Young Master’s old mess.”
“Did he bring girls here often?”
“Used to—no idea where he found them, turning them into maids here and at other outside properties. Two days ago a bunch marched over to the main manor demanding Second Young Madam find them husbands. Good-hearted lady that she is, she sorted every one out.”
Shen Jianchen felt a twinge for Qianli Xun—Chen Haoyu had snatched so many girls he’d have lost his head ten times over if they had sued. How unfair of fate to lay such mismatched paths for the two of them.
#CH 284
#TITLE: Princess Consort Yi’s Midnight Raid
Watching servants hurry past even at night, Shen Jianchen asked, “No masters live here—why are the servants so busy?”
“Masters away or not, chores still have to get done.” Stopping outside a door, the caretaker said, “Here’s the room, Your Lordship—every girl Second Young Master snatched got locked in here.”
“Open it.”
Pushing the door wide, the caretaker bowed. “If you’ll excuse me, I’ve got duties to see to—”
“Hold on. I’ve got questions for you.” Shen Jianchen stepped inside, Wen Xin and the constables trailing while the caretaker waited outside.
Even at night, the room looked plush, bright, and scented with incense. Stepping into the inner chamber, Shen Jianchen eyed the soft bed where Chun Jinlan had been bound, then ran a finger along a wooden chest—zero dust. Any clue from a year ago had long since been scrubbed away by diligent maids.
“Combed every inch in daylight,” Wen Xin said. “Not a single trace.”
“Questioned the maid who cleaned up the morning of the fourth day of the third month last year?”
“Did. She said it’s too long ago to recall—which means nothing stood out.”
“What about the rest of the staff?”
“Tried—caretaker says all the servants got pulled away right after Chen Haoyu left on the third day of the third month.”
“Pulled away on that exact day? Awfully convenient.”
“Thought so too.”
“Call the caretaker in.”
Summoned back, the caretaker bowed. “Your orders, Your Lordship?”
“Why were all the servants transferred out on the third day of the third month last year?”
“Master’s orders—not my place to ask. When the staff got back, they said Chen Manor was deep-cleaning all its city residences and ran short on hands.”
“Which master? Eldest Master Chen?”
“No, Your Lordship—Eldest Young Master.”
Alarm bells rang in Shen Jianchen’s head. *Him again!* “Chen Haodong?”
“Yes.”
“And you stayed behind?”
“I did.”
Taking a seat, Shen Jianchen pressed, “Then you’d know whether Chen Haoyu came back that night.”
“I stayed, yes, only I drank a few cups too many and passed out cold—didn’t wake till noon the next day. Saw Second Young Master then, sure, yet I know nothing about the night before.”
“Did Chen Haodong come in person to transfer the staff?”
“No—Butler Wang came from the main manor to fetch them and brought them back three days later.”
Too many coincidences—Chen Haodong’s secret tryst house sat right down the road, and he pulled the staff away that very day. Every arrow pointed straight at him, yet hard proof remained missing.
Dismissing the caretaker, Shen Jianchen told Wen Xin, “Nothing left inside. Comb the grounds outside under night cover, then question every servant to see if anyone missed that transfer on the third day of the third month. Tomorrow verify the transfer with Butler Wang at Chen Manor.”
“Yes, Your Highness.”
Leaving Wen Xin and his squad at work, Shen Jianchen headed back to Youlin Retreat—Mengrong leaned on him more every day, and staying out too late would set her off.
Sure enough, back at Youlin Retreat, Qiao Mengrong had watched night fall with no sign of her brother. Sending a servant to check the Judicial Office only brought back word that Prince Yi had walked out alone during the day and never returned.
Dread chewed at her nerves. Where had he gone alone—a secret rendezvous?
“Xilan! Hitch the carriage—we’re going to Chen Manor!”
Reading her mistress’s mind, Xilan had the carriage ready in minutes. Pulling up outside Chen Manor, Qiao Mengrong didn’t even wait for Xilan’s arm—she vaulted down and marched straight for the main gate while Xilan sprinted ahead to pound the knocker.
A gatekeeper cracked the door. “Who is it?”
“Princess Consort Yi stands before you!” Xilan snapped. “Open wide and let Her Highness in!”
Terrified, the servant flung the gate wide and dropped to his knees. “Greetings, Your Highness! Let me summon my masters to welcome you—”
“Don’t disturb anyone,” Qiao Mengrong said, forcing her voice slow and steady. “I’m here to see Youqi Jushi—Second Young Madam. Lead the way.”
Scrambling up, the gatekeeper said, “Second Young Madam lives in Moxiang Courtyard. Right this way, Your Highness.”
Blind to the manor’s nocturnal scenery, Qiao Mengrong cared about one thing alone: was Zhu Shuzhen home? If not, she had to be out meeting her brother—and Qiao Mengrong prayed she was here, or she’d lose her mind. Still, noticing Chen Manor dwarfed even Youlin Retreat sparked fresh venom: why did a shameless flirt like Zhu Shuzhen get both her brother’s heart and the richest family in Lin’an?
At the moonlit plaque of Moxiang Courtyard, Qiao Mengrong dismissed the guide. “Leave us—no need to alert anyone else. Private visit.” Hesitating, the servant bowed off—and went straight to alert his master anyway, knowing better than to hide a royal visit.
Xilan knocked on the courtyard gate. On night watch, Chouya opened it and gasped. “Princess—I mean, Princess Consort Yi! Here to see my miss?”
“Is Youqi Jushi in?” Xilan demanded.
“Yes, of course—she should’ve just gone to bed. Please come in, Your Highness, I’ll go tell Sister Kuqiao!”
Relief washed over Qiao Mengrong, though she wouldn’t trust it until she laid eyes on her. Crossing the flower-filled courtyard past the lotus pond and a shadowy tree, she followed Chouya to Suxin Thatched Hall.
Chouya called outside the door, “Sister Kuqiao, Princess Consort Yi is here to visit!”
Expecting Zhu Shuzhen to step out right away, Qiao Mengrong watched the door crack open—only for Kuqiao to slip out looking rattled and pull it shut behind her.
Qiao Mengrong’s heart leaped into her throat. *She’s not here!*
Kuqiao dropped to her knees, Chouya kneeling beside her. “My miss ought to greet Your Highness in person, only women have been banging on the door these past days demanding to be Young Master’s concubines, and now Young Master is locked up at the Judicial Office. My miss was heartbroken and just fell asleep—I’m afraid she can’t come out to receive Your Highness.”
Sure Chen Haodong’s scheme had made Zhu Shuzhen miserable, Qiao Mengrong still smelled a lie—what if the Judicial Office case had thrown her brother and Zhu Shuzhen together right now?
Her face darkened. “If she’s heartbroken, all the more reason I should comfort her! Who gave a servant the right to speak for Youqi Jushi?”
Kuqiao stayed glued to her knees in cold sweat. Her miss had snuck out in men’s robes and still wasn’t back! Judging by how hostile the Princess Consort had been at the poetry salon, she sure hadn’t come to offer sympathy.
Guessing her miss was out, Chouya chimed in to cover. “Please don’t be angry, Your Highness—Miss is feeling unwell today and fears offending you.”
Kuqiao grabbed the lifeline. “Yes! Low spirits and unwell—she’d hate to show disrespect.”
Xilan snapped, “Hiding indoors is real disrespect! Her Highness makes a rare trip to Chen Manor and you stall at every turn—do the Chens have zero regard for royalty?!”
“Forgive our late welcome, Princess Consort Yi! The Chens hold royalty in utmost esteem.”
Informed by the gatekeeper, Chen Jianglin had avoided waking Old Madam Xia or his brothers and hurried over with Yao Qingyan, baffled why the Princess Consort had come calling at night.
Qiao Mengrong wheeled on him. “Fine words, Master Chen—yet these two maids have stalled me at the door since I arrived! Is Youqi Jushi even inside, or did she sneak out after dark for a secret tryst?!”
Alarmed by her fury, Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan dropped to their knees.
“Calm your anger, Your Highness—don’t misunderstand!” Yao Qingyan pleaded. “Shuzhen is definitely inside! There must be a reason she hasn’t come out. I’ll open the door and fetch her myself right now!”
Mollified, Qiao Mengrong waved them up. “Rise—making it look like I’m bullying you.”
Getting to her feet, Yao Qingyan ordered Kuqiao, “Open the door.”
*We’re doomed,* Kuqiao thought, heart pounding as she pushed the door open.
Yao Qingyan stepped inside with Qiao Mengrong right on her heels. The layout echoed Wangyue Pavilion’s bedchamber so much that a stab of nostalgia hit Qiao Mengrong—remembering nights huddled beside a brazier chatting with Shuzhen—only to curdle straight back into hatred over her betraying their friendship and stealing her brother’s heart.
Trembling behind Madam as they entered the inner chamber, Kuqiao braced for an empty bed—and nearly collapsed in relief! Instead of the neat quilt she had folded, her miss lay tucked underneath, just one corner of her discarded men’s robe peeking out from beneath the covers.
Darting ahead, Kuqiao pulled the quilt smooth while jamming the men’s robe out of sight underneath.
Yao Qingyan leaned over with a gentle call. “Shuzhen, Her Highness came to see you—best get up.”
Qianli Xun blinked open groggy eyes, playing freshly woken to perfection. “Who...? Who came to see me?”
“Princess Consort Yi,” Yao Qingyan stressed.
Qianli Xun shot upright. “Kuqiao! Why didn’t you wake me sooner? We nearly slighted Her Highness!”
Kuqiao played right along. “You were so upset, Miss, and had just fallen asleep—I hated to wake you.”
“I’m fine—Her Highness comes first!” Qianli Xun scolded for show. “Remember: whenever Her Highness visits, even if I’m sick in bed, get me up right away!”
Yao Qingyan smiled in approval while Kuqiao bowed her head. “Yes, Miss.”
“Let me dress quick and greet Her Highness.”
Slipping out of bed with Kuqiao’s help, Qianli Xun came out to the main room with Yao Qingyan and curtsied beside her mother-in-law.
The sight of her former best friend standing right there wiped every trace of panic from Qiao Mengrong’s face.
“Forgive the wait, Your Highness,” Yao Qingyan said.
“Your Highness honors my humble room,” Qianli Xun added. “Forgive any slight.”
Relieved and back to frosty disdain, Qiao Mengrong rose. “Nothing important—now that I see you’re here, staying is boring.” With a flick of her sleeve, she swept out as Chen Jianglin called out from the yard, “Safe journey, Your Highness!”
Inside, Yao Qingyan frowned in bafflement. “Leaving just like that? What was that whole stunt about?”
Qianli Xun knew the score—she had lost track of Prince Yi and freaked out. Good thing she got home in time, or Mengrong would’ve sworn they were together—which, well, they had been, just not like that. “Royals throw whims around because they can, Mother-in-law. So long as we didn’t offend her, no harm done.”
Yao Qingyan sighed. “Shuzhen, I know the Princess Consort holds a grudge over Prince Yi’s wedding raid. Be extra careful going forward so nobody finds an excuse to smear your reputation.”
Touched that the Chens never blamed her for the wedding raid and still watched out for her, Qianli Xun nodded. “I’ll remember, Mother-in-law—I won’t bring trouble on you and Father-in-law.”
“Don’t talk about trouble—we’re family. As for Haoyu...” Yao Qingyan hesitated, worried the courtesan scandal had soured Shuzhen on her son.
Deciding to set her mother-in-law’s heart at rest, Qianli Xun said, “Don’t worry, Mother-in-law. Flawed as he is, Chen Haoyu is my husband. If he’s innocent, I’ll stand by him through this storm; if he’s guilty, I’ll live out my life as his widow right beside Grandmother and you and Father-in-law.”
Eyes reddening, Yao Qingyan clasped her hand. “Marrying you was the greatest blessing of Haoyu’s life.”
“Having Grandmother and parents-in-law like you is my blessing too.”
Watching the warm bond between mother-in-law and daughter-in-law brought tears to Kuqiao’s eyes.
#CH 285
#TITLE: Returning Late at Night
Once Eldest Master and Madam left and Chouya went back to the west wing, Kuqiao bolted the door, hurried into the inner room, and fished the men’s robe from beneath the quilt. “Nearly scared the soul out of me! One second later and we’d have been busted! How on earth did you get in?”
Pouring herself water from the teapot and gulping it down, Qianli Xun grinned. “Your miss walks roofs and vaults walls, remember? I was already on the roof when my in-laws showed up, slipped straight through the back window—just in time to save your hide from a paddling.”
Tossing the robe into the laundry basket, Kuqiao protested, “Master and Madam are kind—they’d never paddle me! Still, what got into Princess Consort Yi, barging in out of nowhere?”
Sitting on the bed’s edge, Qianli Xun sighed. “What else? Lost track of Prince Yi and jumped straight to suspecting me. Not one word I taught her before stuck—clutching that tight will scare off any man.”
“Suspect all she likes, she shouldn’t pin it on you!”
“Actually... she guessed right. I really was with Prince Yi tonight.”
Kuqiao gasped. “Miss! Don’t do anything reckless!”
“Not what you’re thinking! Both of us tailed the exact same suspect for Chen Haoyu’s case and bumped into each other—pure coincidence.”
“Oh! Still, if Princess Consort Yi found out, she’d lose her mind.”
“Prince Yi won’t talk, I won’t talk, you won’t talk—how could she ever know?”
“My lips are sealed!”
“Not even to Bai Feng!”
“Not a single syllable!”
Meanwhile, riding home from Chen Manor, Qiao Mengrong felt the knot in her stomach loosen, yet fresh doubts crept in. If her brother wasn’t with Zhu Shuzhen, where was he? A brothel? Courtesans were brazen and shameless—few men resisted them.
“Xilan, think Brother went to a brothel?”
“Not a chance!” Xilan denied flat-out. “Who is His Highness? A man of his rank would never set foot in a place like that!”
Qiao Mengrong nodded. “You’re right—Brother isn’t that type. I’m overthinking.”
“Don’t torture yourself, Princess,” Xilan soothed. “Since Youqi Jushi is sitting safe at home, His Highness must be tied up on official business and heading back to Youlin Retreat right now.”
Qiao Mengrong thought back to the old days when Zhu Shuzhen had urged her at Youlin Retreat not to cling so hard to her brother lest she get hurt. Back then she was head over heels for Young Master Qiao and hadn’t fallen for Brother yet. Once Zhu Shuzhen stole Brother’s heart, every word she ever said rang hollow. Consummating the marriage had only made Qiao Mengrong prize him more—and terrified of losing him, she wanted the threat erased for good.
Chen Haodong was working his angle. Left to her, she’d have had Master Jiang’s men kill Zhu Shuzhen straightaway, yet Eldest Young Master Chen insisted on setting off his two traps first. Needing him as her errand boy for now, she let Zhu Shuzhen breathe a few extra days.
Arriving at Youlin Retreat, Qiao Mengrong’s first question to the gatekeeper was, “Is Brother back?”
“His Highness stepped through the gate the moment before you arrived, Your Highness!”
Her face lit up. Unable to wait, she hiked her skirts and sprinted along the lantern-lit, freshly swept walkways toward his courtyard, Xilan jogging right behind.
Spotting her brother’s back just about to cross his threshold, she cried out breathless, “Brother!”
Turning in surprise at her urgency, Shen Jianchen barely caught his footing before she threw herself into his arms and clung tight. “Why are you back so late, Brother? I thought I’d lost you!”
Pity softening him, Shen Jianchen stroked her hair. “How could that happen? No matter how late, I’ll always come home.”
“The cloth on your robe is so coarse.”
“Bought it on the fly—easier to tail a suspect without standing out.”
“Working a case?”
“Yes.”
“Chen Haoyu’s case?”
Not wanting to set off a storm of questions and suspicions, he fibbed. “No—a different one.”
Uninterested in Chen Haodong’s minor harassment anyway since it wouldn’t kill Zhu Shuzhen, Qiao Mengrong let go and took his hand. “Come inside and let me help you change.”
Once they stepped in, Xilan pulled the door shut from outside. When His Highness was home and Her Highness was happy, they didn’t need a third wheel hovering indoors; she’d wait outside in case they called.
Helping him out of his robe inside the bedchamber, Qiao Mengrong asked, “Where’s Bai Feng?”
She could never know Bai Feng was out in the bamboo grove—that grove belonged to Youqi Jushi, and he had only started building the bamboo cottage after hearing Kujue mention the grove’s owner wanted one. Telling the truth would spark endless interrogation, so he lied smooth. “Judicial Office is swamped—I had him stay over there a while to save the commute.”
“You can’t go without someone waiting on you.”
Pulling her into his arms, Shen Jianchen murmured, “If not for you waiting at Youlin Retreat, I wouldn’t have come back tonight either. With you taking care of me, who needs Bai Feng?”
It worked like magic. Dropping the questions, she pressed her cheek against his chest. “I want to give you a child, Brother.”
“Whatever you want, I’m yours.” All he hoped was to make her happy and melt away her hatred for Qianli Xun—maybe one day the two could even mend their bond.
Outside, seeing the bedchamber lamp go out, Xilan smiled to herself and went off to sleep.
At dawn, Shen Jianchen had barely sat down at the Judicial Office when Han Gu stepped in and bowed. “Your Highness, I missed you when I got back last night, so I came first thing to report.”
Setting down his dossier, Shen Jianchen asked, “Turn up any solid leads?”
Han Gu shook his head. “Nothing solid—Scarface’s cronies all swear Chen Haoyu did it.”
“Did they see it with their own eyes?”
“No. They said right after Chen Haoyu returned to town, Scarface handed them silver and sent them off to roam the city.”
“Scarface reeks of guilt. Did they mention the staff being pulled away from the country house?”
“Right—before they left, orders came from Chen Manor pulling every servant out except the caretaker. They thought nothing of it except figuring Young Master Chen would be mad when he got back. One more detail...”
“Out with it.”
“Scarface had a jug of fine wine ready. The boys wanted a taste, and Scarface snapped no—claimed it was saved for Young Master Chen and Chun Jinlan.”
“Odds are that wine was meant for the caretaker.”
Right on cue, Wen Xin returned with his squad to deliver his full report from the night before.
#CH 286
#TITLE: Clues Buried Under Fallen Petals
After Prince Yi had left the country house the night before, Wen Xin had his constables pull every servant out for individual questioning while he prowled the grounds outside the detention room by moonlight.
Lush flowers and shrubs filled the yard, anchored by a mature camphor tree in full bloom, drifting sweet fragrance through the air. Stepping up to inspect it, Wen Xin noted the trunk was thick enough to hide a person—and around its roots lay a neat ring of swept-up fallen petals.
Spotting a male servant passing by, Wen Xin hailed him. “Come here a second.”
“Yes, Chief Constable?”
Pointing at the heap around the trunk, Wen Xin asked, “Why pile dead petals under the tree?”
“Chen Manor’s gardener ordered it, sir. Says fallen petals rot into rich soil that feeds the roots so next year’s blooms grow brighter.”
“So last year’s petals stayed piled right here too?”
“Stands to reason, sir.”
Last year on the third day of the third month, the camphor wouldn’t have bloomed yet—still, even if someone hid behind the trunk and dropped something, somebody could have swept it up later. Dismissing the servant, Wen Xin smirked at himself for obsessing over a tree—right as a constable jogged up.
“Chief Constable Wen, we questioned every single servant. Turns out one maid wasn’t transferred away on the third day of the third month!”
Wen Xin’s eyes lit up. “Who? Bring her here!”
“Maid named Aye—she took leave that day to visit her family.”
“Leave?” Disappointment prickled, yet he pressed on. “Bring her anyway—and the caretaker.”
Moments later the caretaker walked up with Aye in tow.
“Ask whatever you like, Chief Constable,” the caretaker offered.
“You’re Aye? You took leave on the third day of the third month last year?”
Aye tensed up. “Yes, sir—my mother fell ill, so I had to go home. The caretaker can vouch for me.”
“True enough,” the caretaker confirmed. “I approved her leave myself.”
“When did you get back to the house?” Wen Xin asked.
Aye stammered, sweating. “I... I... I...”
“Timid girl—scared speechless in front of officials,” the caretaker jumped in. “I’ll tell you: I only gave her two shichen off, yet she didn’t show back up till the third day! Gave her a thorough chewing-out for it too.”
Still a dead end. Discouraged, Wen Xin waved them off.
With nothing else to go on, his gaze drifted back to the camphor tree’s roots. Walking around the trunk, he unhooked his sword and poked through the withered petals with the scabbard—nothing. Digging deeper into the dirt beneath, turning the soil over twice, he was about to quit when the scabbard tip struck something hard.
