Crouching down, he picked it out and brushed off the dirt: a tiny, carved wooden peach-heart charm about the size of a thumb joint, knotted with a thin cord so soil-stained its original color was gone.
Rolling it in his palm, unsure if it mattered, he had the caretaker line up the entire staff and held the charm up. Too dark to see clearly, most shook their heads even after he described it.
One maid spoke up. “Too dark to tell, sir—mind bringing it closer?”
Stepping over, Wen Xin held it in the moonlight. The maid peered close. “I know that! It’s Aye’s! She lost it ages ago—can’t believe you found it, sir!”
Every eye turned to Aye. Parting the crowd, Wen Xin walked straight up and held the wooden heart right before her face. “Look close—yours?”
Aye folded at once. “Yes—yes, it’s mine.”
“How did it end up under the camphor tree?”
“I—I don’t know... must’ve dropped it by accident.”
Years of detective work told Wen Xin she was hiding something big. Time to bluff her wide open.
Pocketing the charm, he spoke dead-certain. “By accident? Don’t think so! Let me tell you what really happened. On the third day of the third month last year, you went home on leave and came back four shichen later after dark. Not a servant in sight. Looking around, you came right here, saw something you shouldn’t have, panicked, and ducked behind that camphor tree—dropping your wooden charm in the dark. Too tiny and dirt-colored to spot, and once the camphor blossomed, fallen petals buried it before you could find it. Am I right or am I right?”
Terrified, Aye hit her knees. “Spare me, Your Lordship! Nothing to do with me—I just saw it by accident!”
Dead on target! Wen Xin let out a triumphant breath, hauled Aye back to the Judicial Office that night, put her up in a room, and brought her before Prince Yi at dawn.
Kneeling in the Judicial Office hall, Aye spilled every word.
“Your Lordship, I got back right after dark. The main gate stood closed yet unlocked, so I pushed in. Not a soul around, and the caretaker was passed out dead drunk. Heading back to the maids’ quarters, I remembered the girl Young Master Chen had locked up and went to check if she was still there. And then...”
“And then what?” Shen Jianchen urged.
Hesitating one beat, Aye blurted, “I saw Scarface—the thug who always trails Young Master Chen—slip into her room and defile her while Young Master was away! Scared out of my wits, I hid behind the camphor tree, hoping to untie the girl once Scarface left. Next thing, the door opened and Scarface hauled out a bulging sack—plainly a person inside, had to be that girl! No way to save her, so I waited till Scarface cleared off—and just as I turned to go, someone else walked out of that room!”
Paydirt! Shen Jianchen leaned forward. “Who?”
“Couldn’t tell—they wore a long black cloak hiding head and face completely, though the build looked like a man’s. Petrified all over again, I stayed crouched behind the tree—must’ve dropped my wooden heart right then, searched for it later and never found it. Scared sick I’d get dragged in if anything blew up, I ran straight back home that same night—and somehow your officer caught me anyway.”
“Was the man in the black cloak Chen Haoyu?”
Aye shook her head hard. “No! Young Master Chen would never skulk around in disguise—he’s locked plenty of girls in that room, always bold as brass in broad daylight. Had to be someone else.”
“Why did you assume Scarface defiled her rather than the cloaked man?”
Aye looked torn. “I didn’t see the cloaked man at first—only saw Scarface go in and heard the girl screaming, so I assumed it was Scarface.”
“Aye, dare you testify in court on Chen Haoyu’s behalf?”
“Much as I despise Young Master Chen’s ways,” Aye said firm, “he didn’t defile that girl. I’ll testify for him.”
#CH 287
#TITLE: Cracking the Case Open
Chen Haoyu’s hearing resumed, with Chen Jianglin, Chen Haodong, and Chen Haofei attending from the gallery.
Chen Haodong came in braced this time—knowing Prince Yi would try exposing his hand, he had his defenses locked tight.
Constables brought in Chen Haoyu and Chun Jinlan to kneel, then flanked the hall.
Shen Jianchen opened. “Yesterday’s investigation turned up new leads. Bring in Butler Wang of Chen Manor.”
Butler Wang entered and knelt. “Your Lordship, I told Chief Constable Wen everything I know last night.”
“Repeat it in front of everyone.”
Butler Wang recited clear. “On the third day of the third month last year, I personally sent orders pulling every servant out of the country house because Chen Manor’s city residences needed a thorough cleaning and ran short on hands.”
“Who gave you that order?”
“Eldest Young Master.”
“Did the Chen Masters know?”
“Eldest Young Master always handles those chores—no need to inform the Masters.”
*Still gunning for me,* Chen Haodong thought. *Good thing transferring servants proves nothing on its own.*
Shen Jianchen turned to him. “Care to explain why you pulled every servant out on that exact day, Eldest Young Master?”
Rising to bow, Chen Haodong answered smooth. “Chen Manor deep-cleans its town properties every year, Your Lordship. Landing on the third day of the third month was pure chance—how could I know Haoyu had locked a girl up there that day?”
Shen Jianchen gave a cool smirk. “Sit—I’ll have more questions shortly.”
Chen Haodong sat, uneasy yet unafraid—he had his firewall in place.
Dismissing Butler Wang, Shen Jianchen called, “Bring in Aye and Scarface!”
Both entered and knelt. Seeing a new witness—one of the country-house maids—tightened Chen Haodong’s nerves. *No way she recognized me in that cloak!*
Shen Jianchen warned Scarface first. “Last chance to tell the truth, Scarface. Once proof shows you perjured yourself, your penalty doubles.”
Scarface stuck to his script. “Every word I spoke is true, Your Lordship! Young Master Chen did it all; I was just his lackey.”
“Which Young Master Chen?” Shen Jianchen asked sharp. “Three of them sit in this hall—mind you don’t misspeak.”
Scarface gulped. “Second... Second Young Master Chen.”
Turning to Aye, Shen Jianchen said, “Tell everyone what you witnessed on the night of the third day of the third month last year.”
“Yes, Your Lordship. When I got back from home that night, every servant was gone and the caretaker was dead drunk. I went to let the girl Second Young Master snatched go—only to see Scarface enter the room, so I hid behind the tree to wait. Screams came from inside, so I figured Scarface was violating her. Then Scarface hauled her out in a sack—and right after, a second person walked out wrapped head to face in a black cloak!”
Chen Haodong exhaled a little. *Missed one maid on leave—lucky she never saw my face.*
“Was that cloaked man Chen Haoyu?” Shen Jianchen asked.
“No!” Aye said flat-out. “Second Young Master never sneaks around for this sort of thing—plus that man stood shorter than Second Young Master, right around... around Eldest Young Master’s height!”
Jolted cold, Chen Haodong barked to cover, “Watch your mouth, girl! This is a court of law—don’t go comparing random people!”
Aye flinched. “Forgive me, Eldest Young Master—spoke out of turn in my nerves.”
Chen Haodong appealed straight to the bench. “Don’t put stock in a jittery maid’s chatter, Your Lordship! Likely there was no cloaked man at all—just her eyes playing tricks in the dark!”
“Believe me, Your Lordship!” Aye insisted. “My eyes didn’t fool me!”
“Hold your conclusions, Eldest Young Master—I’ll judge her credibility myself.” Turning to Chen Haoyu, Shen Jianchen asked, “Describe returning to the house the next day.”
Seeing the tide turn in his son’s favor lifted the boulder off Chen Jianglin’s chest, though Haodong’s jumpy reaction left him baffled.
“I got back around noon the next day,” Chen Haoyu explained. “Wondered a second why no servants were around, then brushed it off and went straight to the room. The girl was gone, and Scarface and the boys were nowhere to be found. Figured she’d escaped and the boys hid out of fear I’d chew them out, so I dropped it and forgot—never even brought it up when I saw them again.”
Chun Jinlan’s world spun—had Scarface been the one who ruined her? All she remembered from that pitch-black room was someone throwing a cloth over her face, untying her body only to lash her ankles together, pinning her down with brute strength while she screamed her lungs out, tearing her clothes open, and taking her in silence—not a single word, only heavy, coarse breathing in her ear.
“Lies!” Chun Jinlan shrieked. “The maid’s lying, Your Lordship! Chen Haoyu snatched me—who else could it be?!”
“I decide who’s lying, not you!” Shen Jianchen snapped.
Cowed, she felt for the dagger in her sleeve. Things were tilting bad—one threat on her own life might flip the board.
Shen Jianchen bored into Scarface. “Well, Scarface? Your explanation?”
“The maid’s making it up, Your Lordship! I didn’t touch her!”
“Who was the man in the black cloak?”
“Who else? Young Master Chen!”
Chen Haodong’s heart lurched. *Is Scarface folding?!*
“Which Young Master Chen?”
“Second... Second Young Master Chen.”
Chen Haodong exhaled—worth every tael of that hundred!
Next, constables brought in the country-house caretaker, who kowtowed low.
“Where did the wine you drank that night come from?” Shen Jianchen asked.
“Scarface brought it, Your Lordship. Said we were old acquaintances from his trips with Second Young Master and offered a drink since the place was empty. I love my wine, so I said yes—stuff must’ve been strong, knocked me cold after just a few cups.”
“Not strong wine—drugged wine.” Shen Jianchen snapped his eyes back to Scarface. “Confess! Why did you drug the caretaker?”
Scarface flopped flat. “I swear I meant well, Your Lordship—just sharing a drink! How could I know he couldn’t hold his liquor?!”
“Stubborn, eh? Twenty strokes of the paddle!”
Constables pinned Scarface down and brought the heavy bamboo down right across the court. Screaming bloody murder, Scarface broke on the tenth stroke. “Mercy, Your Lordship! I’ll talk! I’ll talk!”
Shen Jianchen raised a hand, halting the blows. “One lie and you get ten more.”
#CH 288
#TITLE: Second Young Master Cleared
Scarface darted a glance at Chen Haodong, who gave one microscopic nod and looked away.
*A hundred taels of silver—worth the gamble!*
Taking the whole fall onto his own shoulders, Scarface blurted, “Second Young Master never came back that night, and he never touched Chun Jinlan! It was me! Saw how pretty she was, got itchy while Young Master was away, sent the boys off so they wouldn’t get jealous, and slipped Sleep-Sage Powder into the caretaker’s wine so he’d pass out...”
Fury consumed Chun Jinlan. Lunging at Scarface before constables pinned her down, she screamed, “Liar! It was Chen Haoyu!”
Scarface spun his tale smooth. “Not lying! You fought at first, till I swore I’d sneak you home and look after you if you gave in—so you agreed! Your Lordship, it was consensual! Only afterward I got greedy for silver, knocked her out, and sold her to Yangzhou!”
Pinned tight, Chun Jinlan couldn’t reach her sleeve. Biting back her rage, she went limp, waiting for the constables to loosen their grip.
Shen Jianchen saw straight through Scarface’s cover-up for the cloaked man. “And the man in the black cloak?”
“Never existed—the maid made him up!”
“I didn’t make it up!” Aye cried. “I saw him with my own eyes!”
“Easy to see shadows in the dark,” Scarface shot back.
Seeing Chun Jinlan calm down, the constables stepped back to their posts. While all eyes stayed on Scarface, Chun Jinlan slid her hand into her sleeve and wrapped her fingers around the dagger hilt.
Shen Jianchen spotted the holes in Scarface’s story a mile away: consensual, yet he sent his crew away, drugged the caretaker, and Aye heard terrified screams? Tie that to the transferred staff, and the cloaked man had to be Scarface’s accomplice—a Chen family insider. Haoyu was cleared, Haofei had no motive, every finger pointed straight at Chen Haodong—yet without proof, his hands were tied. Crafty rat that Scarface was, twisting rape into “consensual” made proving a capital charge impossible with no physical evidence. At least Chen Haoyu was cleared, lifting a huge stone off Qianli Xun’s chest.
“A minute ago you swore the cloaked man was Chen Haoyu—now you claim he never existed?”
“I was lying before, Your Lordship—every word now is gospel truth!”
Relief washed over Chen Haodong. Everyone else sat mulling over the contradictions—missing the sudden shift in Chun Jinlan.
Until she cried out, “If Chen Haoyu won’t take me as his concubine, I’ll die right here in court!”
Having heard her cry wolf since yesterday, nobody took her seriously—not even the constables. And since Chen Haodong had planted the idea, he sure wasn’t stopping her.
Before the echo faded, Chun Jinlan whipped the dagger from her sleeve and drove it straight into her own chest. As cold steel sank home, her eyes locked onto Wen Xin in stunned disbelief—*why didn’t he save me?*—before her knees gave out and she crumpled.
Gasps froze the hall. Shen Jianchen shot a look at Chen Haodong—catching a unmistakable flash of relief across his face.
Caught half a step slow because he hadn’t believed her, Wen Xin reached her side as she hit the floor and checked her nose—still breathing faintly.
Forcing her eyelids open, Chun Jinlan whispered barely above a breath, “Why... didn’t you... save me?” With that final puzzled question, her breath stopped.
Blood on the floor crushed Chen Haoyu with remorse. Innocent of the crime, sure—yet if he hadn’t snatched her from that creek, she’d still be alive.
Wen Xin ordered two constables to carry the body out.
With Scarface confessing, Chun Jinlan dead, and the real mastermind still walking free, the case wasn’t over—yet this chapter had to close.
“While loose ends remain, the core facts stand clear,” Shen Jianchen announced. “Second Young Master Chen Haoyu is acquitted. Hold Scarface in custody pending further inquiry.”
Wen Xin and his constables hauled Scarface out. Chen Haoyu climbed to his feet, cleared of all charges yet too sick at heart to crack a smile.
Chen Jianglin stepped forward with his two nephews and bowed. “Thank you for clearing my son’s name, Your Highness. Since Haoyu played a part in Chun Jinlan’s tragedy, the Chen family will pay for her burial and provide for her kin.”
“A decent gesture toward the dead, Master Chen.” Turning to Clerk Han, Shen Jianchen added, “Take the constables and witnesses outside. I need a word with Master Chen and the young masters.”
Han Gu cleared the hall.
Shen Jianchen fixed his eyes on Chen Haodong. Every instinct screamed he was the cloaked man, yet with Scarface eating the blame, he’d have to break Scarface in interrogation later. Still, Chen Haodong’s meddling in bringing Chun Jinlan to Chen Manor was already proven—high time he tasted some heat.
“Will Eldest Young Master tell him yourself, or shall I tell him for you?”
Before baffled Chen Jianglin could even process the question, his eldest nephew dropped flat to his knees with a loud thump, stunning him speechless. Haoyu stayed lost in guilt, while Haofei stared in shock. “Brother Dong, what’s going on? Why kneel to Eldest Uncle?”
“Get up and speak, Haodong,” Chen Jianglin said. “No need to kneel.”
Tears poured down Chen Haodong’s cheeks. “Uncle! I did wrong! Until you forgive me, I’m not getting up! Waaah!”
*Forcing forgiveness before even confessing—shameless!* Shen Jianchen fumed inside.
“You’re the best of our younger generation and never put a foot wrong,” Chen Jianglin coaxed. “What’s all this?”
“Promise you won’t blame me first, Uncle, or I can’t say it!”
“All right, I promise I won’t blame you.”
Turning off the sobs on a dime, Chen Haodong spun his yarn. “One day at the country house, I caught Chun Jinlan peeking around outside. I scolded her, she mistook me for Haoyu and attacked, and servants restrained her. Once she learned I was Haoyu’s cousin, she told me her tragic story. My heart went out to her—and thinking Haoyu really did it, I figured if she joined his household, she’d have a good life and Haoyu would avoid prosecution, so I suggested she make a scene at Chen Manor. All my fault for trying to help!”
Stuck by his own promise—and blaming Haodong’s soft heart plus Haoyu’s own recklessness—Chen Jianglin could only let out a heavy sigh.
Chen Haoyu spoke up in self-reproach. “Get up, Brother Dong—none of this is on you. I’m the idiot who went snatching girls in the countryside.”
Disgusted by the masterclass in theater, Shen Jianchen remarked dryly, “If Eldest Young Master wants a good cry, save it for kneeling in front of your elders at home.”
Taking the cue to settle dirty laundry behind closed doors, Chen Jianglin bowed. “Forgive the spectacle, Your Highness. I’ll take the boys home to sort out family affairs.”
“Go right ahead, Master Chen.”
Chen Haofei helped his cousin up, and the three young men bowed to Prince Yi before trailing Chen Jianglin out.
#CH 289
#TITLE: Two More Years Before Consummation
Back at Chen Manor, Chen Jianglin didn’t summon the elders to air Chen Haodong’s meddling—family shame stayed behind closed doors. Keeping only Chen Haodong in the reception hall, he had servants shut the doors.
Chen Haodong dropped right back to his knees. “I was wrong, Uncle—give me whatever punishment I deserve.”
Sitting back with a sigh, Chen Jianglin asked, “Was this over the family business inheritance?”
No point hiding it now. “Yes. I feared the elders might pick Haoyu, throwing away everything I’ve worked for since childhood.”
“Use your head, Haodong,” Chen Jianglin reasoned. “Where does Haoyu even measure up to you? Improved as he is, he doesn’t have a fraction of your business savvy—why treat him as a rival? One day Chen Manor passes to your generation. You three brothers have to pull together, not tear each other apart over scraps and wreck the house.”
Hanging his head in mock shame, Chen Haodong swore, “Never again, Uncle.”
“Enough. Go back—and don’t breathe a word to your parents. Consider it forgotten.”
Covering it up meant Uncle wanted his record spotless—which meant Uncle still backed him for heir! Thrilled inside, Chen Haodong bowed out to hurry straight home and brief his parents.
Sitting alone in the hall a while to think, Chen Jianglin finally headed back to his own courtyard, where Yao Qingyan rushed out to meet him. “How did the hearing go?”
“Haoyu’s cleared and released.”
Relief flooded her face. “Then why didn’t he come greet his own mother?”
“Chun Jinlan died, and guilt’s eating him alive. He went straight to Moxiang Courtyard—he’ll pay respects once he collects himself.”
“Died?! How?”
“Come sit inside and I’ll tell you everything.”
Seated on the couch, Chen Jianglin walked her through the entire trial. Yao Qingyan’s smile faded. “Still Haoyu’s own fault—if he hadn’t snatched her, none of this happens. He ought to feel guilty. Least we can do is give her a decent burial.”
“Already seeing to it. Now there’s another matter I want your take on: choosing the business heir.”
“That’s huge—shouldn’t you ask Mother and your brothers?”
“I will, yet I want your thoughts first.”
“Since you ask: as a mother, of course I’d wish for Haoyu, yet he’s miles behind Haodong, and Haofei has zero interest. Haodong is the only real fit. If Haoyu just pitches in to help him, I’ll be content.”
Chen Jianglin nodded. “Glad you see it that way. The selection wasn’t due till next year—I want to move it up to this year.”
“Move it up? Is there any precedent for changing the schedule?”
“None, yet I set the date the day I took over, so I can change it too.”
“Fine by me if the elders agree. Only why the sudden rush?”
He rarely kept secrets from his wife, yet airing Haodong’s scheme would only upset her and sour relations with her sister-in-law. “Just as His Majesty names a Crown Prince early to steady the realm, naming our heir early steadies the house. A steady house keeps the juniors at peace—and peace makes the family prosper.”
“You’ve got my full backing.”
With her blessing, Chen Jianglin escorted Yao Qingyan to report the trial outcome to Old Madam Xia and raise moving up the selection.
Meanwhile, Chen Jiangshu had just returned from his shops and taken a sip of tea when Chen Haodong rushed in and waved the servants out.
“What’s the fire? How’d the trial go?”
Shutting the door once the help cleared out, Chen Haodong lifted the teacup right out of his father’s hand and set it on the table.
“Hey—I was drinking that! What’s the idea?”
“Eldest Uncle knows I put Chun Jinlan up to it.”
Chen Jiangshu’s hand twitched—now he saw why his son took the cup away. Stepping from the inner room, Madam Bao asked in panic, “If Eldest Brother knows, will it sink your inheritance?!”
Chen Jiangshu sat grim, fearing years of groundwork had blown up.
“Uncle doesn’t know you two were in on it,” Chen Haodong added. “Even warned me not to tell you.”
Chen Jiangshu’s shoulders dropped in relief. “That means he’s keeping it under wraps—you’re still in the driver’s seat! Makes sense: out of the three boys, you tower over the rest. Eldest Brother has no other choice, so he won’t tarnish your name.”
“If it was already in the bag,” Madam Bao sighed, sinking into a chair, “why pull all those stunts behind the scenes?”
“Needed them!” Chen Jiangshu argued. “Sunk Haoyu for good and nudged Eldest Brother into deciding early—letting him know Haodong pulled the strings wasn’t even bad. Wait, how did the hearing end? Did Haoyu take the courtesan as concubine?”
“Prince Yi’s sharp—dug up a witness proving Haoyu wasn’t there that night and forced Scarface to take the fall. Out of moves, Chun Jinlan threatened suicide and killed herself right in court.”
Madam Bao clicked her tongue. “Scarface did it?! Haoyu’s got devil’s luck—picked every flower in sight and dodged the one with thorns.”
“Never mind who did it—good that a Chen lad came home safe,” Chen Jiangshu said firm. “When the yamen stepped in I broke a sweat too—if he’d been guilty, I’d have urged him to take her as concubine just to save his head. Remember this, Haodong: scheme all you want, never gamble with a kinsman’s life.”
“Understood, Father.”
“Worked out best for us anyway—Haoyu’s home safe, yet his reputation with the clan is mud. Nobody stands in your way now, so quit while we’re ahead.”
*Time to let Master Jiang make his move,* Chen Haodong thought silently.
Over at Moxiang Courtyard, Chen Haoyu arrived with Qinggua, everything looking the same while his heart sat heavy as lead. Days ago he felt sure he’d win his wife over; now he felt unworthy to stand in her shadow.
Without waiting to be scolded, Chen Haoyu dropped to both knees right outside Suxin Thatched Hall. Understanding his mood, Qinggua knelt beside him.
Touched after Qinggua had already kept him company at the Judicial Office, Chen Haoyu scolded, “You didn’t do anything wrong—why kneel?”
“I’m your man, Young Master. You kneel, I kneel.”
“Don’t bother—go check on Chouya and let her know you’re safe.”
Mention of Chouya popped Qinggua straight up. “I’ll check on Chouya and come right back to kneel with you!”
Watching him dash off toward the west wing, Chen Haoyu smiled wry. “Lucky guy—walks straight back to the girl he likes with a clear conscience, while your master...”
Early summer sun beat down fierce even as afternoon slanted west, beading sweat across his brow in minutes.
Indoors, Qianli Xun sat at her desk writing her huaben—the last few days had sparked fresh plot twists, and writing beat stewing over Chen Haoyu.
Aware of the scene outside, Kuqiao stepped up and whispered, “Young Master’s back, Miss—kneeling out in the yard.”
Qianli Xun paused her brush, relieved—home meant cleared. Still, none of this would’ve happened if he hadn’t snatched girls in the first place. Let him kneel and pay a little penance. She dipped her brush and kept writing. Seeing her temper still up, Kuqiao left her be.
Soon after, Old Madam Xia arrived with Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan, unable to stay away once she heard her grandson was cleared. Spotting him kneeling in the sun, Old Madam Xia raised a hand to halt everyone. “Leave them be—the young couple has scores to settle.”
Yao Qingyan winced. “Kneeling in this blazing sun...”
“Better kneeling in the sun than rotting in jail awaiting the axe!” Old Madam Xia turned right back out, Yao Qingyan trailing with a backward glance.
After half an hour of writing, Qianli Xun remembered his stabbed arm—sweating in the heat would infect the wound. Softening, she set down her brush and opened the door.
Head bowed and brow drenched in sweat, Chen Haoyu wore a fresh robe over his bandaged arm.
“Get up and come inside.”
Her voice hit like a cool summer breeze. “Coming!” Scrambling up on numb legs, he hobbled toward the door.
“Kuqiao! Help Young Master in!”
Kuqiao hurried out to steady him—and Chen Haoyu beamed inside: the first time his wife had called him “Young Master” in front of a servant, owning him as her husband!
Once Kuqiao seated him and went to whisk tea, Qianli Xun returned to her desk and asked flat, “Arm any better?”
“Much better with Qinggua looking after me.”
“Tell me how the trial went.”
“Scarface confessed. Chun Jinlan hid a dagger and killed herself in court.”
Qianli Xun almost mentioned Chen Haodong’s hand in sending Chun Jinlan over, then bit it back. Trouble had just blown over, and Haoyu looked beat up enough without learning his own cousin had knifed him. “Quit pulling reckless stunts.”
“Never again, I swear! Can you forgive me?”
“Forgive or not doesn’t matter—I married you, so I’m stuck with my bed.”
A relieved grin broke over his face. “Long as you don’t ask for a mutual divorce, I’ll do anything you say!”
Her temper flared. “Is that what you think of me?! I only went through a mutual divorce once because I had zero choice! What woman treats marriage like a joke, tossing divorce around every five minutes? Even if I didn’t mind leaving you, I’d never bear leaving Grandmother and my in-laws—don’t bring up those words around me again!”
“Never! Swear I won’t breathe them!”
Kuqiao brought over the freshly whisked tea. Parched from the sun, Chen Haoyu downed it in one gulp and handed back the bowl. “Soon I’ll be making all my wife’s tea.”
Kuqiao teased, “Young Master’s busy learning business—where will you find time to whisk tea?”
“Give me a few more days with the tea master—once I’m sharp, whenever I’m home, don’t you dare fight me for tea duty.”
“Deal—all yours!” Setting the bowl down, Kuqiao tucked away her miss’s huaben manuscript discreetly.
“Chun Jinlan was a pitiable soul,” Qianli Xun said. “At the root, you dragged her into this—see to her burial and look after her family.”
“Don’t worry—Father already arranged everything.”
A brief hush fell before Qianli Xun spoke up. “Chen Haoyu, since you said you’ll do anything I ask, I’ve got one condition...”
“Name it, Wife.”
“I’d been trying to warm up to you and consummate our marriage—if none of this had hit, maybe we already would have. Now... who knows when I’ll feel ready.”
His shoulders sagged.
“I’ll give you two years,” she went on. “Keep away from other women and stay chaste for me for two full years, and at the end of two years, I’ll consummate our marriage.”
Hope flared like sunrise in the dark. “Deal! Think of it as two years to scrub every stain off myself—I’ll work my tail off to become a man worthy of you!”
“Shaken and sealed!”
Standing behind her miss with the stacked manuscript, Kuqiao frowned in worry.
Chen Haoyu rose and bowed solemn as a scholar. “From this day on I swear off all women and treat my wife with utmost respect, never stepping a single inch out of line until the day you’re satisfied.”
His dead-serious formality threw Qianli Xun right off balance, leaving her blinking dumbly as he marched out of Suxin Thatched Hall.
#CH 290
#TITLE: Ambush Beneath the Divine Willow
The moment Young Master left, Kuqiao couldn’t hold back. “Miss, isn’t two years way too long? Everybody else’s kids will be toddlers while you still have nothing!”
“Chen Haoyu spent way longer than two years pulling sleazy stunts—giving him two years is cutting him slack! In an era where women don’t pick their own husbands, at least I still control my own heart, and I’m not handing it to an unreliable guy on a whim. Time tests a person best; if he passes two years, then he’s earned me.”
“What if the Chen elders object?”
“After Chun Jinlan’s mess, even if they hate it inside, they’ll respect my call.”
Relieved, Kuqiao nodded. “So long as you know what you’re doing, Miss.”
With Chen Haoyu home safe, a weight lifted off Qianli Xun’s chest. Wanting fresh air, her mind jumped straight to the Divine Willow—high time she paid it a visit.
Reaching the foot of the hill, a swell of nostalgia hit her. Youlin Retreat sat right nearby; once she could stroll right up to see her friend without a care, yet now a stone’s throw felt like oceans away.
Walking up the path toward the willow, a full year’s worth of memories since crossing over washed through her—one year that felt like half a century. She had family now, had known close friendship, tasted love, even married. Some things were gone, others still in her hands; she no longer clung to what she’d lost, only feared losing what she still held.
Celebrated poetess in public, secret huaben author Scholar Qianli Xun on the sly—which one was the real her? She still couldn’t tell, just like the Divine Willow draped in red silk ribbons before her. She insisted it was the same willow from her modern world, yet back then nobody tied red ribbons onto its branches.
*Stop obsessing over riddles with no answers,* she told herself. *Just like this willow—hiding its mystic power behind plain bark, letting mortals tie ribbons all over its limbs without complaint. Accept what fate hands you, meet every twist with a clear head, and maybe that’s how you actually reshape your destiny.*
The Divine Willow really worked wonders—standing beneath it untangled the knots in her head.
Lifting her eyes to the red-draped crown, she spotted a single ribbon tied high up in the upper boughs. *Someone copied me? Could it be him?*
Curiosity tugging, she sprang up into the high branches with her light-foot technique and flipped the ribbon open—her name and Shen Jianchen’s stared back.
*Already consummated with Mengrong and still hanging our names together? What if Mengrong walks under here and it drops right in her lap? I’d never wash my name clean! Better take it down just in case.*
Untying the ribbon, she rolled it neat, slipped it into the pouch at her waist, retied the pouch, and floated back to the ground.
Her feet had barely touched dirt when a pack of black-clad, masked men brandishing heavy sabers swarmed out of nowhere, encircling her!
No time to wonder who sent them—Qianli Xun barked at Kuqiao, “Get behind the Divine Willow—now!”
Shaken out of her freeze, Kuqiao sprinted behind the huge trunk and poked half her head out to watch.
Unarmed yet confident in her fists, Qianli Xun didn’t flinch as gleaming blades slashed in from every angle. She ducked one sweep and drove a punch square into an attacker’s chest, sending him staggering back. Outnumbered, she pivoted and retreated in bursts, weaving between saber arcs with fists and kicks blurring through the dust.
Spotting an opening, she unleashed a thunderous kick that launched one masked man straight into the willow’s trunk. Kuqiao ducked back behind the bark, peeking a moment later to see the thug slumped on the grass coughing blood, done for the fight.
The rest surged back in. Slipping between them like a darting dragon, Qianli Xun struck with pinpoint precision—snatching one man’s wrist to drop his blade, using his own momentum to slam him down, then slamming a heel into another’s ribs until he doubled over.
Even so, a blade nicked her arm, slicing a shallow red line. Ignoring the sting, she kicked into higher gear, whipping through the ring like a gale as heavy blows landed thud after thud. After she dropped several more, the surviving thugs saw the tide turn and bolted.
Letting them run, Qianli Xun stood catching her breath and eyed the gash on her arm.
Kuqiao dashed out from behind the tree and bound the cut with her handkerchief. “Does it hurt, Miss?”
“Just a scratch.”
“Who were those men? Clearly they came straight for you!”
Noting the downed thug groaning beneath the willow, Qianli Xun walked over. Kuqiao darted ahead and kicked his rear. “Dare jump my miss?! Get back up and try again if you’ve got the guts!”
Groaning unable to rise, he let Kuqiao rip off his black mask. “Ambushing my miss in broad daylight behind a mask—coward!”
Staring at the plain, unfamiliar face, Qianli Xun asked, “Who are you people, and who sent you to kill me?”
Kuqiao scooped up his dropped saber and leveled the point at his nose. “Spill it, or I’ll carve lines across your mug!”
Terrified, the man begged, “Mercy, heroine! Promise you’ll let me go if I talk!”
Qianli Xun sneered. “Your life isn’t worth a single copper to me.”
“Talk!” Kuqiao jabbed the blade closer.
“We’re Master Jiang’s men from the Qiantang Gang! Somebody paid for your head, so Master Jiang sent us!”
Mengrong—straightaway Qianli Xun knew. A hollow ache washed through her chest. Saying nothing more, she turned to leave; Kuqiao dropped the blade, gave the thug one parting kick, and hurried after her.
Three steps down the path, Qiao Mengrong stepped out to block their way, Matron Zou and Xilan right behind her.
Seeing only a bandaged cut on Zhu Shuzhen’s arm, Qiao Mengrong clicked her tongue. “Useless fools! Still alive and kicking, eh?”
Any plea would bounce off a wall of hatred. Qianli Xun met her gaze cold and flat. “Killing me won’t be that easy.”
Stepping closer, Qiao Mengrong stared icy-eyed. “Not easy, no—yet I’ll find ways to make it easier. Consider today a warm-up.”
Unable to bite her tongue, Kuqiao piped up bold. “My miss is married and just wants a quiet life with Young Master—she’s no threat to Princess Consort Yi! Why won’t Your Highness leave her in peace?!”
“Who let a servant run her mouth?!” Qiao Mengrong snapped. “Xilan—slap her!”
Xilan stepped up to strike, only for Qianli Xun to block her arm hard. “Nobody touches my maid—not even royalty!” Shoving Xilan aside, she sent the maid stumbling back to catch her balance.
Knowing rank meant nothing against those fists out here, Qiao Mengrong sneered. “Strong fists—I’ll let your maid slide for now. Still, she’s talking garbage! No threat to me? Then what are you doing sneaking up to the Divine Willow today?!”
Refusing to swallow false accusations, Qianli Xun shot back, “Wrong, Princess Consort Yi. I visited the Divine Willow long before you and Prince Yi ever set foot in Lin’an—what scheme is there in that?”
“Times changed! Since moving into Youlin Retreat, Brother comes here all the time—dare swear you didn’t come hoping to ‘accidentally’ bump into him? Nobody knows you better than me: preaching virtue while playing the harlot behind closed doors! Laughable!”
Anyone else spitting that filth would have caught a fist to the jaw; against Mengrong, Qianli Xun’s hands stayed heavy at her sides.
“Think whatever you like—my conscience is clear.” Reining in her hurt, she curtsied. “Enjoy the view, Your Highness. I have duties at home—excuse me.”
“Hit a nerve and running away?”
Ignoring the jab, Qianli Xun stepped past with Kuqiao—only for Matron Zou to block her path. “How should I address you?”
First time meeting her—around Matron Wei’s age. “Call me whatever you please, Matron.”
“Everyone calls you Youqi Jushi, so I’ll use that. Allow me to introduce myself: I’m Prince Yi’s wet nurse. I raised him from infancy and watched Princess Consort Yi tend his sickbed day and night with her own hands. Back then His Highness had eyes for her alone—until he recovered, came to Lin’an, and put someone else in his heart: Youqi Jushi. Since we crossed paths, let an old woman offer some advice.”
Every word looked like fact from the outside—arguing was pointless. Qianli Xun kept silent.
“Imperial sons follow strict rules when taking wives or concubines,” Matron Zou went on. “You’ve already had one mutual divorce, your reputation in Lin’an is tattered, and you’re another man’s wife now. Aside from decent birth and a pretty face, you don’t fit a single mark. Drop any wild dreams. Keep your head down, and I’ll persuade Her Highness to spare your life.”
Grating as the lecture was, Qianli Xun wanted out fast—and decided to aim her parting plea at the matron instead of deaf Mengrong. “Sound advice, Matron. I know my place and never harbored wild dreams. All I want is a peaceful life—neither bothering anyone nor being bothered. Please urge Princess Consort Yi to stop treating me as an imaginary rival. I’m not afraid to die; I just hate being dragged into someone else’s drama for nothing. And don’t worry—I won’t come back to the Divine Willow again.”
She brushed past Matron Zou with Kuqiao.
Qiao Mengrong laughed bitter at her back. “For nothing! Talking like you’re pure as snow—when you admitted right at the poetry salon that you fell for Brother!”
Etiquette forbade ignoring a royal’s words outright. Halting a few paces on, Qianli Xun turned back, curtsied silent with Kuqiao beside her, then turned clean away and strode off.
A pang of loss pricked Qiao Mengrong—no more nights sharing a bed, talking till midnight and sleeping till noon.
Matron Zou sighed. “She looks like zero threat to you now, Your Highness. Why not let her go—and give yourself peace too?”
“She talked you round in three sentences—doesn’t that prove how dangerous she is?!” Qiao Mengrong snapped. “Slick tongue, and unmatched at hooking men without lifting a finger! Hooked Young Master Qiao till he went on a hunger strike, got Brother risking disgrace to raid a wedding, and even turned a playboy rake like Chen Haoyu into a reformed saint! Tell me she isn’t dangerous!”
“Dangerous or not, she’s married and clearly settling down.”
“No—you don’t know her! Anywhere, any time, she doesn’t even have to speak a word to steal Brother’s soul! How could I ever sleep easy? She has to die!”
Matron Zou sighed in defeat. “Stubborn as you are, nothing I say gets through—I’ll quit preaching. Still, you saw her martial skills today; taking her down won’t be easy.”
Glancing at the groaning thug on the grass, Qiao Mengrong spat, “Hmph! Master Jiang’s men are useless trash—a whole mob couldn’t kill one woman! We need tougher killers. Go see Chen Haodong again, Matron.”
“I’ll head to Yongtai Teahouse tomorrow.”
#CH 291
#TITLE: Tea Outside the Qiantang Gang
First thing next morning, right after Chen Haoyu called outside the door that he was off to the silk shop, Qianli Xun had Kuqiao summon Hongdou. Confirming the veteran hands could run the congee shed fine alone, she told Hongdou to skip it today and round up all nine courtyard servants—they were paying the Qiantang Gang a visit.
“Two went out early with Matron Wei on errands,” Hongdou reported.
“No matter—eight including you is plenty.”
Moments later Hongdou assembled seven more: Asheng, Amei, Chouya, Sanbao, Jiaxiang, Fulu, and Ayun.
“You usually go with Matron Wei, Ayun—why home today?” Qianli Xun asked.
“Matron said she didn’t need extra hands and left me for Miss to use.”
Qianli Xun nodded. “Today I’m having a long chat with Master Jiang, and fists might fly. The Qiantang Gang has numbers—anybody scared?”
“Not scared!” all eight shouted.
“Good—move out!”
Since taking over the congee shed, she came and went without asking the elders’ leave, sparing endless questions—better not worry them over a brawl anyway. Boarding two carriages with her eight servants, she rolled straight for the Qiantang Gang. She wouldn’t lay hands on Mengrong, yet Master Jiang had crossed her line and owed her a reckoning. Knowing she couldn’t fight and would only get in the way, Kuqiao stayed back tidying the quarters.
Over at the Qiantang Gang, the ambush squad had limped back bruised and empty-handed the day before. Before Master Jiang even spoke, Chun Jinmo had harped in his ear from dusk till dawn, nagging him to send his best killers to finish the poetess off.
Much as Master Jiang doted on his new wife, he didn’t lose his head over big stakes. Killing the Prefect’s daughter meant war with the yamen—bad business—and once he dug up her link to Prince Yi, he wanted zero part of it.
Since marrying Chun Jinmo, Master Jiang had quit rising early, preferring to lounge in bed holding her close.
Chun Jinmo pouted against his chest. “Princess Consort Yi backs Chen Haodong! Wipe out Youqi Jushi, hitch yourself to royalty, and who cares about the Prefect?”
Kissing her cheek, Master Jiang asked, “Tell me—who’s stronger, Prince Yi or Princess Consort Yi?”
“Prince Yi, obviously—yet she’s his wife! If he’s powerful, she is too.”
Master Jiang chuckled patient. “Living in Chunxiang Brothel, you didn’t see how Lin’an’s elite circles work. Now that you’re my wife with status, mingle with respectable ladies—gossip carries real intelligence.”
Shoving him away, Chun Jinmo rolled onto her pillow facing the wall. “Save it! The Qiantang Gang is underworld trash, and I’m ex-brothel—what noble lady would give me the time of day?”
Master Jiang wrapped his arm back around her. “Once I hook onto Prince Yi, nobody will dare look down on you.”
“Hmph! Won’t even lift a finger for Princess Consort Yi and dreaming of Prince Yi? Keep dreaming!”
“Ever hear about Prince Yi raiding Chen Haoyu’s wedding?”
Spinning back around, Chun Jinmo gaped. “Prince Yi raided the wedding—for Youqi Jushi?!”
“Now you get why Princess Consort Yi wants her dead?”
“All the more reason to kill her—stealing a royal’s husband!”
“If I want Prince Yi’s favor, touching a hair on her head is suicide. I asked around: Prince Yi picked the Princess Consort up as a kid, and His Majesty only decreed the match because she tended him while he lay sick. Without that illness she’d never have made Princess Consort—she’s got zero court backing. Hooking onto her gets us nothing.”
“Then why is Eldest Young Master Chen working for her?”
“No idea—just trust me, Youqi Jushi is untouchable! Not only is her dad Prefect, Prince Yi himself is off-limits. Know what happened when Prince Yi heard Chen Haodong paid me to trap Chen Haoyu into marrying her? He stormed into my hall in person—calling himself Young Master Shen—all to stand up for Miss Zhu! Only dug up later that he was Prince Yi. Think: how huge does a woman have to be to get a prince swinging fists for her?”
“Poor Young Master Chen—married a woman a prince won’t let go of.”
“Chen Haoyu’s problem, not ours. Me, I just keep out of Prince Yi’s crosshairs. I already brushed off the Princess Consort—sent weak scrubs who couldn’t touch the poetess so she’ll give up on my gang.” Pulling her close again, he grinned. “Bet a VIP shows up at our door today—”
Right on cue, a subordinate knocked outside. “Chief! Somebody’s at the gate making trouble—calls herself Youqi Jushi!”
“Speak of the VIP!” Master Jiang rolled out of bed. “Get dressed and come greet her—she’s Jiangnan’s top poetess and one of Lin’an’s premier ladies. Getting friendly with her opens every door in high society for you.”
Bitter over her stealing Chen Haoyu, Chun Jinmo bristled, yet couldn’t argue with his logic. Sneering, she got up to see what kind of fuss the poetess planned to kick up.
Skipping breakfast, Master Jiang marched out with only a few men, grinning ear to ear.
Outside the gate, Qianli Xun stood flanked by her servants, braced for an armed mob to pour out—only to blink in bafflement as Master Jiang strolled out beaming like a host welcoming kin.
His first line baffled her even more. “Had breakfast yet, heroine? If not, our kitchen’s got a spread ready—eat with me?”
Snapping out of her daze, Qianli Xun turned stern. “Already ate—and even starving I wouldn’t share a table with a guy who tried to kill me! Be smart: get on your knees, own up, and swear off sending thugs to murder people, and maybe we talk. Otherwise I’m pounding you flat till you beg!”
To her shock, Master Jiang dropped straight to his knees. “I was wrong, heroine! Shouldn’t have sent men after you! I swear I’ll stick to running gambling houses with my boys and quit blood-for-hire for good!”
*Why’s this mob boss folded so fast?*
Rising and stepping close, Master Jiang murmured, “Cut me some slack, heroine—had no choice yesterday, so I picked my sorriest fighters on purpose, or how else would you be standing here today?”
Half her steam deflated. “What’s your angle?”
“Just want to invite you inside for a calm chat.”
Sizing up his broad, dark face—earnest enough, though caution never hurt—she refused. “Skip going inside. Let’s sit in the pavilion right outside your gate.”
“Still don’t trust me—fair enough, your call.” Turning to a henchman, he barked, “Bring my breakfast to the pavilion, grab a jug of rice wine for the heroine, and call Madam out!”
“No alcohol—tea only,” Qianli Xun cut in.
“West Lake Longjing tea, then! After you, heroine.”
Hongdou and the other seven trailed to stand guard outside the pavilion, until Master Jiang dismissed his own thugs after tea and breakfast were set, and Qianli Xun waved her eight back to rest out of the sun inside the carriages.
Pouring her tea, Master Jiang grabbed chopsticks. “Help yourself while I grab a bite.”
“Quit calling me ‘heroine’—sounds weird.”
“Then I’ll call you Youqi Jushi like my wife does.”
Taking a polite sip, Qianli Xun cut straight to it. “What do you want to talk about, Master Jiang?”
Setting down his chopsticks, he came clean. “Word is you’re tight with Prince Yi. Could you put in a good word and introduce me to him?”
*Aha! Now the polite act makes total sense.* “You already work for Princess Consort Yi—getting to Prince Yi through her should be effortless. Why take the long way round?”
Scooping congee, Master Jiang explained. “I’ve seen Prince Yi—sharp, upright man. The dirty work Princess Consort Yi hires me for can’t see daylight; going through her would only make Prince Yi suspicious. Going through Youqi Jushi is clean, no shady deals—plus Prince Yi hangs on your every word.”
Hearing him hint at gossip buzzing behind her back soured her stomach. “If I help you after you put it like that, I just confirm the rumors! I’m happily married into the Chen family. My elders treat me well and grant me freedom—I won’t risk my good name outside. Sorry to disappoint.”
Rejected flat-out, Master Jiang stayed sunny. “No harm done! Even if you won’t make the intro, making friends with you works too—makes talking to Prince Yi easier.”
“My friends are noble ladies and scholars—never ran with gang bosses.”
Turned down twice, he still grinned. “Fair enough—sitting down for a chat today is already a great start.”
Right then Chun Jinmo’s syrupy voice cut in. “Well! Wondered who the VIP was—Youqi Jushi herself!” Having dressed and grabbed a quick bite, she had come out both on Master Jiang’s orders and to dig up Chun Jinlan’s trial results—plus take a fresh dig at her rival.
The second his wife appeared, Master Jiang hopped up and rushed to take her hand. “Watch the steps, Wife.”
Watching the tough gang boss fuss over his wife stirred unexpected warmth in Qianli Xun.
Swaying into the pavilion, Chun Jinmo sat beside her, waving a round fan and eyeing her up and down with disdain. To break the awkward stare, Qianli Xun remarked, “Lucky Madam Jiang—having your husband steady your arm over a single step.”
“Treating one’s wife right is only proper,” Master Jiang beamed. “I look rough, yet I’m tender with my woman.”
Ignoring him, Chun Jinmo sneered, “No need to envy me, Youqi Jushi—turning Young Master Chen into a saint has everyone green with envy.”
“Each person has their own path,” Master Jiang cut in gently. “Quit pining for Chen Haoyu—with him you’d only be a concubine, nowhere near being my primary wife.”
Disgust flashed across Chun Jinmo’s face as she looked away, trapped in silent bitterness.
A pang of pity hit Qianli Xun—even a rough underworld boss turned humble before the woman he loved. “All right, Master Jiang. If I run into Prince Yi, I’ll put in a word for you.”
Overjoyed, Master Jiang leaped up and bowed hands clasped. “Thank you, Youqi Jushi! Pull that off and you’re the savior of the Qiantang Gang!”
Taking another sip, she glanced sideways. “You call me savior—doubt Madam Jiang agrees.”
Sitting back down with an apologetic look at his wife’s turned back, Master Jiang murmured, “Bear with her, Youqi Jushi—newly wed, still adjusting, she’ll come around. Actually... one more tiny favor?”
*Give an inch, take a mile!* Still, moved by his devotion to his wife, she relented. “Spit it out.”
“Youqi Jushi’s fame spans Jiangnan, Prefect’s daughter, married into Lin’an’s richest clan—top tier among Lin’an’s noble ladies. My wife can’t compare, yet since she married me, I want to lift her standing. Could you take her along and ease her into high-society circles?”
Amused—yet sympathetic to a man wanting better for his wife—Qianli Xun offered a gentle reality check. “Your devotion to your wife moves me, Master Jiang. Still, think about why I move in those circles: my father is Prefect of Lin’an, and my in-laws are the wealthiest family in town. Were I an ordinary girl, nobody would let me in either. If you want Madam Jiang accepted, you’ll have to earn that standing yourself.”
#CH 292
#TITLE: An Invitation Arrives
Catching the hint—an underworld gang had zero respectability—Master Jiang nodded. “Dead right. Ever since marrying my wife, I’ve wanted to turn the Qiantang Gang legit, earn clean silver, and give her real face when she steps outside.”
Touched all over again, Qianli Xun softened. “Truth is, I rarely attend society teas—hate gossip and empty feasting. Poetry salons, though, I go to plenty. If Madam Jiang wants, next time I’m invited, I don’t mind bringing her along.”
“Thank you!” Thrilled, Master Jiang nudged his wife. “Thank Youqi Jushi, Wife!”
Chun Jinmo shot it down cold. “I’ve got someone else to bring me into high society—and zero interest in poetry salons.”
Flushing awkward, Master Jiang apologized. “Forgive me, Youqi Jushi—spoke out of turn without asking my wife.”
Relieved—dragging an unwilling rival to poetry gatherings would’ve been torture—Qianli Xun smiled easy. “Even better if she already has a sponsor.”
Chun Jinmo turned to her husband. “Give Youqi Jushi and me a moment alone.”
His goal already met, Master Jiang rose without hesitation. “My wife speaks, I obey! Excuse me, Youqi Jushi.”
Watching him stroll off, Qianli Xun marveled: came here spoiling for a brawl, wound up sipping tea and discovering the mob boss was a hopeless romantic.
Once Master Jiang was out of earshot, Chun Jinmo cut straight to business. “Looking pretty cheerful—so Chun Jinlan didn’t become Young Master Chen’s concubine?”
“Sorry to disappoint, Madam Jiang. Chen Haoyu was acquitted, and Chun Jinlan killed herself in court out of shame.”
“What?! Dead?! Wasn’t Young Master Chen the one who ruined her?”
“Scarface confessed to the whole thing.”
A shadow crossed Chun Jinmo’s eyes. “A courtesan’s life—worth less than dirt, gone in a blink.”
“Cherish what you have, Madam Jiang. Master Jiang treats you sincerely.”
“Too bad I don’t love him—I had no choice.”
“Don’t you think marrying for love in this era is a rare luxury anyway?”
Chun Jinmo jumped on the opening with a smirk. “Still pining for Prince Yi, Youqi Jushi? Nearly cost you your neck already—quit trying to steal a royal’s man!”
Kicking herself for getting philosophical with someone incapable of real talk, Qianli Xun stood. “No idea where you pick up such gutter talk. Enjoy talking to yourself—I’m leaving.”
Chun Jinmo called after her, “Don’t think dodging one bullet means you’re safe! Princess Consort Yi won’t let you off!”
Pausing without turning, Qianli Xun replied, “Too bad your husband already did.”
“Hmph! My husband’s a spineless coward! Plenty of killers out there ten times deadlier, and Princess Consort Yi has oceans of silver to hire them!”
Turning back one last time, Qianli Xun said coolly, “Whoever she hires, bring them on—I’m not scared. Just treasure the man right in front of you and stop spitting on Master Jiang’s heart.”
Walking back to her carriages, she pitied Master Jiang—then shrugged it off: the guy had done plenty of dirt in his life, maybe an unloving wife was his karma. Behind her, Chun Jinmo spat, “Cocky bitch! Wait till you get what’s coming!”
Funny how life twisted—marched out to beat up a gang boss and ended up volunteering to play matchmaker with Prince Yi. At least Master Jiang was off Mengrong’s payroll now; maybe Mengrong would finally cool down.
Rolling back up to Chen Manor’s south gate, Qianli Xun spotted Dongfang Chenze’s page boy Huayu peeking in with an invitation card.
*Another poetry salon?*
Stepping down with Hongdou’s help, she greeted Huayu as he hurried over. “Lucky timing—saved waiting for Sister Kuqiao! Young Master is hosting a poetry salon and sent me to deliver your invitation personally.”
Opening it, Qianli Xun saw the venue: Juxiang Tower. “The usual crowd?”
“Not this time! Only poets with real standing in Lin’an’s literary circles—plus high-ranking Lin’an officials. From the old crowd, Young Master Qiao is invited of course, and Young Masters Cao and An made names for themselves critiquing your verse, so they’re in—and Youqi Jushi goes without saying. Madam Miao has no literary standing, so she wasn’t invited.”
Clearly a major event—no reason to decline. “Tell your master I’ll be there on time.”
“Young Master also asks that you bring a brand-new ci lyric for the occasion.”
“Of course.”
Seeing Huayu off, Qianli Xun tapped the card against her palm. Even though her in-laws granted her freedom, this salon would be packed with men—proper respect meant clearing it with the elders and checking with Chen Haoyu too, since he reported his comings and goings to her every day. Sending the servants back to Moxiang Courtyard, she walked straight to Old Madam Xia’s courtyard where Yao Qingyan would be sitting.
With the storyteller out sick two days running, the three daughters-in-law were keeping Old Madam Xia company, everyone in high spirits today.
Seated on the couch, Old Madam Xia looked over all three. “Haoyu came to see me last night. Only a few days and he’s matured leaps and bounds—swore he’ll never pull foolish stunts again. Good came out of bad: our most worrisome boy turned into a new man! Remember, all three of you: teach the three cousins to stick together and pull as one.”
“Yes, Mother!” all three chorused.
“One more thing so you aren’t caught off guard.” Sipping tea, Old Madam Xia went on, “Yesterday Jianglin asked to name the business heir this year instead of next. I approved—no reason to object.”
Joy flared inside Madam Bao—just as her husband predicted, the courtesan scandal pushed Eldest Brother to act fast, and her son had the clan elders locked up! Hiding her smirk, she played puzzled. “Isn’t there still a full year left? Why rush?”
“Jianglin has his reasons—just go along without questioning. None of you hold a deciding vote anyway; just know it’s happening.” That shut Madam Bao right up.
Unbothered since her bookish son wasn’t competing—once Haofei passed the civil exams and took court office, the Chens would be leaning on him anyway—Madam Cui still chimed in to stay visible. “Has Eldest Brother picked a date yet, Mother?”
“Not yet. He’ll sound out the clan elders and pick an auspicious day together.”
Just then a maid announced Second Young Madam. Inviting her in, Old Madam Xia listened as Qianli Xun curtsied and presented the invitation. “Grandmother, Mother-in-law, Young Master Dongfang just sent an invitation to a poetry salon tomorrow. Huayu says every notable figure in town is going, so I accepted tentatively to ask your leave first—with mostly men attending, I wouldn’t want to stir up gossip.”
Old Madam Xia didn’t even glance at the card. “No need to show me! Inviting you honors both you and Chen Manor—of course you go! Mostly men? Let tongues wag! Grandmother backs you.”
“Go right ahead, Shuzhen,” Yao Qingyan agreed. “No need to ask us over poetry salons—decide for yourself. Come sit.”
Taking a seat beside Yao Qingyan, Qianli Xun smiled inside: sure she knew they’d say yes, yet asking first showed respect and won even more trust.
Madam Cui leaned over eager. “Shuzhen, is that salon at Juxiang Tower?”
“Yes, Third Aunt.”
Madam Cui beamed. “Haofei got an invitation this morning too! Same salon—look out for him tomorrow, will you?”
“Of course—we’re family.”
Old Madam Xia sighed fondly. “Used to envy literary salons when I was young, only I don’t know a rhyme from a radish! Now Chen Manor has two guests invited—Shuzhen goes without saying, and Haofei’s made a name for himself too. Wonderful news!”
Puffed with pride, Madam Cui added, “Guess who’s bankrolling tomorrow’s salon, Mother!”
Old Madam Xia played along. “By that grin, don’t tell me it’s Prince Yi?”
“Bull’s-eye! Haofei says Prince Yi put up the silver to make Young Master Dongfang’s salon number one in Lin’an! With Prince Yi attending, what Lin’an official dares skip? Having Haofei invited makes me proud as punch!”
Sour envy twisted Madam Bao’s stomach. *Poetry salon—big deal! Wait till Haofei flunks the exams and see who’s crying!*
Qianli Xun frowned inside: why was Prince Yi funding Dongfang Chenze’s salon? Worse, if Mengrong showed up, she’d have to steer wide clear.
“All the more reason not to decline if Prince Yi’s behind it,” Old Madam Xia said, then changed topics. “Just back from the congee shed, Shuzhen?”
Unwilling to worry them with the truth, Qianli Xun fibbed, “Yes... just back.”
“Prince Yi’s relief project has the whole city cheering—before long our congee shed can wind down. Father-in-law says the Prefect suggested shutting it now to put the silver toward building cottages and cave dwellings, and I said no! Until those roofs are up, homeless folks still need hot meals. Chen Manor will cover the government’s share down to the last copper without starving the poor. Sorry to keep you busy a while longer.”
Respect for the Chens’ integrity swelled in Qianli Xun’s chest. “Since the Lazybones Village folks went back to the hills, the shed runs smooth even without me—no trouble at all.”
“Good.” Old Madam Xia shot Yao Qingyan a glance.
Catching her cue, Yao Qingyan said gently, “Shuzhen, Haoyu’s messes put you through plenty of stress and fright these past days. Now that peace is back, Grandmother, Father-in-law, and I agreed we won’t meddle between you and Haoyu anymore. Live your marriage your way—so long as you’re building toward good days, nothing matters more.”
Emotion tightened Qianli Xun’s throat. She had fretted over how the elders would react to her two-year chastity pact, and instead they handed her total trust—making her want to stay in this family and give Haoyu a real shot more than ever. “Shuzhen thanks Grandmother and Mother-in-law—I’ll take every word to heart.”
#CH 293
#TITLE: Mengrong Insists on Going
Inside the Judicial Office interrogation room, Shen Jianchen presided in person, determined to smoke out Chen Haodong.
Row upon row of torture gear lined the dim walls, chilling the blood at a glance. Bound to a chair with thick rope, Scarface hung his head while Han Gu poised brush and paper to take notes.
Wen Xin tapped a heavy whip against his palm. “Head up and answer His Lordship straight, or you get twenty lashes first!”
Eyeing the whip and irons, Scarface half regretted taking the fall—then pictured those hundred taels of silver and dug his heels in. Turn on Eldest Young Master Chen and he’d never see a copper!
Eyeing the prince—rumored across Lin’an as kindhearted and compassionate—Scarface tried turning on the waterworks. “Merciful Prince! I’m a poor lonely guy whose family can’t afford a wife! Seeing a pretty girl made me weak, so I slipped up with Chun Jinlan! I swear it was consensual—she loved it, just denied it hoping to snag Second Young Master! My only crime was selling her to a brothel! Punish me however you want, just spare my life—I’ve got a seventy-year-old mother to feed! Waaah!”
Cold steel rang in Shen Jianchen’s voice. “Drop the act. One more fake sob and we pour chili water down your nose.”
Scarface shut off the tears on the spot. “Ask whatever you want, Your Highness.”
“Who was the man in the black cloak?”
“Nobody! Aye made him up!”
“Aye has zero reason to lie—you, on the other hand, are spinning fairy tales.”
“Every word’s true—I’d never fool Your Highness!”
“Hmph! Two blatant lies. First, claiming it was consensual. Chun Jinlan’s dead, so she can’t face you, yet one detail blows your story sky-high. Bring Aye in.”
Wen Xin opened the door. Terrified by the torture rack, Aye stepped in with eyes glued to the floor.
Shen Jianchen softened his voice. “Don’t be scared. Tell me what Chun Jinlan’s screams sounded like that night.”
Puzzled, Aye replied, “Just... loud screaming...”
Scarface cut in, “Having fun with me, of course she screamed—”
Wen Xin flicked the whip. “One more peep and you taste leather!” Scarface clamped shut.
“What I mean,” Shen Jianchen clarified, “did she sound like she was enjoying herself, or terrified and furious?”
Aye got it at once. “Terrified and furious! I’ll never forget that voice—she was shrieking, ‘I’ll kill you!’”
“Why didn’t you say that in court?”
Aye shrank back. “Your Lordship... didn’t ask that detailed...”
*Blaming me now?* “All right, you may go.”
Opening the door for Aye, Wen Xin hid a grin—obviously the prince couldn’t grill a maid over bedroom noises in open court. Shutting the door again, he caught the prince’s look and signaled constables to bring in a tub of water.
Untying Scarface, Wen Xin dragged him to the tub and shoved his head straight under. Scarface held out a few seconds before lungs burned and he thrashed, gasping as Wen Xin hauled him up. “Mercy! I’ll talk! I’ll talk!”
Kicking the backs of his knees to drop him before Prince Yi, Wen Xin barked, “Talk straight!”
Confess to rape and he swung; rat out Chen Haodong and bye-bye hundred taels. Thinking fast, Scarface pulled a scapegoat out of thin air. “Fine—it wasn’t consensual, yet I didn’t touch her either! It was... it was...”
“Denying the cloaked man was lie number two! Out with it—who was he?”
“Black-Fiend Tiger!”
Shen Jianchen blinked. “Black-Fiend Tiger?”
“Bandit chief up on Mount Tianmu,” Wen Xin explained. “Leads a mob burning, looting, and murdering.”
Unconvinced, Shen Jianchen snapped, “Dragging in a mountain bandit to fool me? Fetch the thorn switches!”
Worse than paddles—Scarface scrambled to sell his lie. “Every word’s true! Black-Fiend Tiger’s real name is Li Hu—used to be my neighbor before he ran afoul of the law and turned bandit! On the first of the third month last year he slipped down the mountain, hid at my place, and demanded I get him a woman. Knowing the Chens’ country house sat empty on the third, I drugged the caretaker and snuck him in—he wore the black cloak so nobody’d spot him! Black-Fiend Tiger defiled Chun Jinlan, not me!”
*Brilliant!* Li Hu really had been his old neighbor and really had dropped by once. Blame an untouchable mountain bandit, keep Chen Haodong spotless, walk out alive, and pocket a hundred taels!
Skeptical, Shen Jianchen pressed, “Then why hide his name earlier?”
Scarface’s forehead kissed the floor. “Terrified you’d charge me with colluding with bandits!”
Airtight—zero thread linking back to Chen Haodong. Shen Jianchen had to hand it to Eldest Young Master Chen: not a single loose end, even holding a greedy rat like Scarface in check. Clearly Haodong had dangled a fat payout.
No point chasing ghosts up Mount Tianmu right away—better lock Scarface up a spell, turn him loose, and tail him straight to the silver payoff to catch Chen Haodong red-handed.
“If Black-Fiend Tiger did it, I’ll muster Lin’an’s garrison to wipe out Mount Tianmu! As his accomplice—plus trafficking a free citizen—you escape death yet not punishment: twenty strokes of the paddle and one month in jail.”
Collapsed on the floor, Scarface exhaled—alive! Once he got out, he’d hit Eldest Young Master up for extra hazard pay!
Once constables hauled Scarface out, Wen Xin frowned. “Chen Haodong stinks to high heaven, Your Highness—are we letting it drop here?”
Han Gu agreed. “Rumor says Black-Fiend Tiger drags every woman he touches back up Mount Tianmu. If true, Scarface is lying—the cloaked man is Chen Haodong.”
“Dead right,” Shen Jianchen confirmed. “Once Scarface gets out in a month, we put a tail on him. No way Chen Haodong stays clean.”
Both lit up. “Brilliant plan, Your Highness!”
Shen Jianchen added half-joking, half-grave, “Song penalties for human trafficking are way too soft—ought to carry the death penalty. Still, today it works for us: keeping Scarface alive gives us bait to reel in the big fish.”
Neither official commented on imperial law. Walking out of the interrogation room, a constable handed over an invitation card from Young Master Dongfang.
Opening it, Shen Jianchen chuckled—he had funded the salon and nearly forgot amid the case! He didn’t want to bring Mengrong tomorrow, yet hiding it would blow up worse if she heard later. With work slow, he headed back to Youlin Retreat at noon to tell her straight.
Surprised to see him home so early—a first since taking the Judicial Office post—Qiao Mengrong lit up, then noticed Bai Feng still missing. “Home early means work’s light—why didn’t Bai Feng come back with you?”
Shen Jianchen teased, “Now my feelings are hurt.”
“Why?”
“I walk through the door and all you ask about is Bai Feng—not one word about me?”
She dropped Bai Feng at once. “Don’t be silly—my heart’s all yours! Only asked because I worry nobody’s tending you.”
Taking her hand, he led her over. “Sit—got something to tell you.”
“Just in time for lunch—let’s eat and talk.”
Xilan had maids lay the table. Sitting beside her, Shen Jianchen asked, “Where’s Matron Zou?”
Matron Zou had gone to Yongtai Teahouse to see Chen Haodong, yet Qiao Mengrong covered smooth. “Still new to Lin’an and enchanted by the scenery—went out to West Lake this morning, won’t be back till dark.”
Letting it pass, Shen Jianchen ladled her a bowl of nourishing soup and pulled out the invitation. “Young Master Dongfang sent this for tomorrow’s poetry salon.”
Her eyes sparkled as she took it. “Taking me along, Brother?”
Seeing her excitement made his words catch. Choosing them with care, he said, “This salon’s different—strictly literary figures and Lin’an officials, not a social banquet. Bringing wives isn’t proper.”
Her smile vanished on the spot. *So that’s why he came home early—to ditch me!* Shutting the card, she handed it back. “If wives aren’t allowed, why even show me?”
“Young Master Dongfang aims to make his salon number one in Lin’an, so he’s limiting invites to poets and officials.”
She pouted. “Dongfang Chenze is just an ordinary gentleman—how’d he pull in every poet and official in town? Everybody’s going for your face, aren’t they?”
Caught dead-on, he admitted, “I put up the silver to back him.”
Suspicion sharpened her gaze. “You’re friendly with him, sure, not close enough to bankroll him like that. Why go to such lengths?”
Admitting the salon aimed to clear Zhu Shuzhen’s name over the pamphlets would detonate a bomb, so he told a white lie. “You know me—soft touch when friends beg.”
“All the more reason to take me! Young Master Dongfang wouldn’t dare mind.”
“Local poetry salons have strict traditions, Mengrong—even more so for me, I can’t be the one breaking rules. Try to understand.”
Unable to fault the logic, she picked up a piece of chicken with her chopsticks and set it in his bowl. “Is she going?”
Playing dumb, he asked, “Who?”
“Zhu Shuzhen.”
Setting a morsel in her bowl in return, he kept his face calm. “Most likely—she is Jiangnan’s poetess.”
Sipping her soup, Qiao Mengrong asked, “Will you talk to her alone there, Brother?”
He hesitated half a beat too long.
“Silence means yes?”
“Stop imagining things, Mengrong—we’ve consummated our marriage, and my heart is devoted to you.”
“I trust you, Brother—I don’t trust her. Either skip the salon, or take me with you.”
Cornered with no way out, Shen Jianchen gave in. “Fine. I’ll take you.”
#CH 294
#TITLE: Bringing Her Husband Along
Chen Haoyu got home after dusk and checked in outside Suxin Thatched Hall as usual. “I’m back, Wife! Steward Qiu says I move to the accounting room day after tomorrow to learn bookkeeping. Plus the female tea master at Yongtai Teahouse praised my progress—give me a few more days and I’ll whisk tea for you! Oh, and good news: Yongtai Teahouse belongs to the Chens now, hung with fresh paintings and calligraphy if you want to browse sometime.”
The door swung open, his wife standing inside. “Come in—need to talk to you.”
Eager to step in, Chen Haoyu felt Qinggua yank his hem. “Can I skip going in, Young Master?”
“Nobody cares if you come in.”
“Go see Chouya?”
“Scram.”
“Scramming!”
Odd how cursing Qinggua felt warm now—less temper, more affection.
Inside, Kuqiao handed Chen Haoyu a freshly whisked bowl of tea. Peering into the bowl, he started nitpicking right away. “Foam’s neither thick nor white enough.” Taking a sip, he tutted. “Taste is passable, only the water-to-powder ratio is off—too much water, and the powder’s coarse.”
Kuqiao stuck out her lip. “I’ve whisked tea for Miss for years without one complaint! Young Master’s studied three days, hasn’t even made a bowl yet, and already acts like a critic—never making tea for you again!”
“Gotta take honest feedback or you’ll never improve! Three days or not, I’m already better than you.”
“Big talk! Whisk one for Miss right now and prove it!”
“Not yet—wait a few more days and I’ll blow my wife’s mind.”
Griping all the while, he still drained the bowl—which Kuqiao snatched straight out of his hand.
“Watch the attitude, girl!”
“Draw a picture on tea foam, then we’ll talk!” Setting the bowl down at the tea table, Kuqiao pinched some tea powder between her fingers—okay, maybe a hair coarse—and sat down to re-grind it.
Smug his jab worked, Chen Haoyu turned to his wife, who had watched their bickering from her desk with an amused smile. “Actually, Wife, I really can paint on tea foam—just need more polish.”
Skipping tea talk, Qianli Xun held up the invitation. “Look at this.”
Taking it open, he blinked. “Going to a poetry salon?”
“Mm. Mind?”
“Who am I to mind? Go wherever you please.”
“Mostly men—not worried?”
Closing the card on her desk, he said earnest, “Course I worry—yet after living with you, I know your character. You keep strict boundaries with other men.”
A warm rush caught her off guard, prompting words she hadn’t even planned. “Ask Steward Qiu for tomorrow off and come with me?”
His eyes lit up. “Really? I can come?”
“With my clout in Lin’an’s poetry circle, bringing one guest is nothing.”
“Only... I don’t know a thing about poetry—what if I embarrass you?”
“Not bringing you to compose verse—just stand beside me and it shuts down half the gossip.”
Remembering his own rumor-mongering, he winced. “Ignore what people wag their tongues about.”
“Don’t want to come?”
“Course I do!”
“Then quit jabbering!”
“Yes, ma’am!” Grinning ear to ear, he backed toward the door. “Rest early, Wife—I’m heading to the rear courtyard.”
Watching him go, Kuqiao spooned fresh-ground powder back into the caddy. “Bringing Young Master to the salon is the best idea ever, Miss.”
Stretching her arms overhead, Qianli Xun teased, “Why re-grind the whole batch over one comment from him?”
“Well, even Miss calls him ‘Young Master’ now—how dare I ignore him?”
“Sleepy—time for bed.” Heading into the inner chamber, she let Kuqiao wrap up and help her turn in.
Meanwhile, backed by Prince Yi’s generous purse, Dongfang Chenze had gone all-out with Cao Ban and An Mingxuan, inviting Lin’an’s top officials and every noted poet regardless of age or gender under a bold new banner: the Dongfang Poetry Salon. Aiming to swallow every rival gathering in town, he even won over his own official father, who had once griped about his son wasting silver on salons yet now beamed with pride over Prince Yi’s backing and pitched in to help.
Salon morning arrived. Qianli Xun skipped makeup altogether—no powder, no rouge—believing respect came from conduct, not paint, and knowing a painted woman at a male-heavy literary gathering invited snide remarks. She picked a plain, refined gown designed to blend in rather than steal eyes.
Right on cue, Chen Haoyu called outside, “All set, Wife! Ready to roll?”
Opening the door, Qianli Xun blinked—Chen Haoyu stood dressed head to toe in vibrant crimson, setting off his fair skin and sharp features.
“Not a groom today—why red?”
“Not wedding robes—a casual outfit I tailored two years back and only wore once! If I’m accompanying my wife in public, folks need to know who I belong to—wear bright red and everybody spots the Poetess of Jiangnan’s husband!” Smug pride practically glowed off him.
Amused despite herself, she asked, “Where’s Qinggua?”
“Sent him to the silk shop to request my leave—plus he’s so hooked on clerking I worry he’ll ditch being my valet.”
“Going from kicking-post lackey to shop clerk is a huge step up for Qinggua,” she teased, striding ahead.
He trotted alongside. “Give me credit for growth! Haven’t kicked him in ages—plus since you taught him that phantom-step dodge, I couldn’t land a kick if I tried!”
Picturing Qinggua sidestepping and tripping Haoyu flat on his rear drew a quiet laugh from her.
Spotting the pair heading out together, Yao Qingyan’s maid Yingxue bolted straight back to her mistress’s quarters. “Madam! Madam! Huge news!”
Right as Yao Qingyan set a Go stone against Chen Jianglin, the shout scattered her whole strategy. “What’s the panic?”
“Second Young Master and Second Young Madam just went out together!”
Dropping the game on the spot, Yao Qingyan beamed. “Together?!”
Yingxue bobbed her head like a rattle-drum. “Saw them with my own eyes!”
“Here we were fretting Shuzhen would freeze him out, and look at the lovebirds!”
Gathering his black stones, Chen Jianglin smiled. “Kids sort out their own blessings. Shuzhen’s got a level head—she knows what’s best for her future.”
“Acting like you didn’t fret too! Who asked me last night whether Shuzhen would dump Haoyu?”
Chen Jianglin chuckled sheepish. “Allowed to worry once in a while.”
“She’s off to that poetry salon—must be taking Haoyu along!”
“Ten to one.”
“Wait till I tell Mother—she’ll be over the moon!” Slipping on her shoes, Yao Qingyan hurried out with Chen Jianglin tagging along.
Outside Chen Manor’s south gate, Chen Haofei waited beside a hitched carriage and stepped up as they emerged. “Carriage is ready, Second Sister-in-law—let’s go.”
Chen Haoyu stuck out his chest and sidled right up to his wife. “Blind to your second brother? Not ‘you two’—‘us’!”
Chen Haofei blinked. “Second Brother’s coming too?”
“Why not? With Jiangnan’s poetess bringing me, who dares say peep?”
“Ah—fair enough! Haven’t caught up with you in ages anyway, Second Brother—we’ll chat on the ride!”
“Chat any other day, not today! I’m riding with my wife—go hitch your own carriage.”
Flushing, Chen Haofei murmured, “My oversight—I’ll grab another rig.”
Qianli Xun grinned. “I like company—three in one carriage chatting and laughing suits me fine.”
Chen Haoyu flipped his tone in half a second. “Whatever my wife says goes! Skip the second carriage, Haofei—hop in!”
Speechless, Chen Haofei decided the old saying about every beast having its tamer was gospel truth.
Back inside, Old Madam Xia laughed till her eyes crinkled at Yingxue’s report. “Splendid! Bringing Haoyu proves Shuzhen truly accepts him—and sets our minds at rest that attending a salon won’t spark gossip.” Turning to business, she asked Chen Jianglin, “When’s the heir selection?”
“Clan elders approved moving it up a year and left the date to me—I’m setting it for next month.”
“Next month it is. Looks like Haodong has the best shot—trained under you since childhood and the eldest. Only flaw is treating Anya poorly; lean on him to do better.”
“I will, Mother.” Chen Jianglin thought briefly of Chun Jinlan—sabotaging a cousin was a major strike, yet Haoyu wasn’t ready and Haofei had no head for trade, leaving Haodong the sole viable heir. Keeping that stain hidden and locking the selection down early was the only way to keep the clan united.
Inside the rolling carriage, Chen Haoyu ignored Haofei completely, staring at his wife like he’d never seen her before. “Why dress so plain today—not even a dab of powder?”
“Not a beauty pageant—why cake on powder?”
“Bare-faced suits you even better—grows prettier every second.”
Feeling invisible as a ghost, Chen Haofei stared out the window regretting his choice of ride.
Noticing his awkwardness, Qianli Xun drew him in. “When do you head to the capital for the exams, Haofei?”
Grateful, he turned back. “Heading to Bianliang this winter for the spring metropolitan exam—pass that, and it’s on to the palace exam.”
“And after the palace exam you become a court official?”
“Top three tiers earn court posts.”
“I bet you take first place!”
“First place is a stretch—third tier is well within reach.”
A burst of awe lit her face—prouder than sitting next to a prince! “So I’m riding right beside a future court official!” Then remembering the rot of late Northern Song politics, she turned serious. “Once you wear official robes, Haofei, never become a corrupt crook.”
“Count on it, Sister-in-law—nothing I hate more than corrupt officials!” Blushing, he added, “Talking like I already passed!”
#CH 295
#TITLE: Meeting a Famous Poet at the Salon
Dongfang Chenze had booked all of Juxiang Tower for the day, hanging a “Closed to Walk-Ins” sign out front beside a bold new banner: “Dongfang Poetry Salon.”
Qianli Xun stepped inside with Chen Haoyu and Chen Haofei. Along the main ground floor stood a tasteful stage crowned by a horizontal scroll reading “Dongfang Poetry Salon,” flanked by a couplet from Tang poet Bai Juyi: “At leisure we trade refined drinking games through classics and histories; tipsy, we hear clear chanting sweeter than pipes and strings.” [TN: Translator’s rendering] At center stage stood a carved rack for hanging poem scrolls beside a guqin zither.
Early as they were, scores of guests had already arrived—not a single woman in sight, confirming how smart bringing Chen Haoyu had been.
Spotting friends across the floor, Chen Haofei fidgeted. “Second Sister-in-law, mind if I go say hello?”
“Go right ahead.”
Haofei dashed off without a glance at his brother. Chen Haoyu pouted for show. “Look at him—totally ignores me and only asks you! Wait till we get home, I’m lecturing him.”
“Third Aunt worried he’d feel lost and asked me to look out for him—turns out he knows twice as many folks here as I do!”
Right then Dongfang Chenze, Cao Ban, and An Mingxuan hurried over.
“Youqi Jushi’s arrival makes the Dongfang Salon shine!” Dongfang Chenze beamed.
Keeping up her modest poetess front, Qianli Xun replied, “You flatter me, Young Master Dongfang. With this hall packed with distinguished scholars—and royals and officials due any minute—my humble light fades away. Better I tuck myself into a quiet corner.”
Cao Ban grinned. “If Youqi Jushi hides in a corner, the rest of us will have to dig holes in the floor!”
Laughter rippled through the group. Chen Haoyu had no patience for stuffy scholars, yet since his wife was trading banter, he plastered on a polite smile—no earthly clue what was supposed to be funny.
“Brought a fresh poem, Youqi Jushi?” An Mingxuan asked.
“Of course.”
“There’s a live brush-and-ink round later—we’re counting on you to dazzle us.”
