Qianli Xun answered with a smile. Mindful not to snub her husband, Dongfang Chenze turned to Chen Haoyu. “Winning Youqi Jushi’s hand made half of Lin’an jealous, Young Master Chen—and attending a poetry salon under her influence shows true conjugal bliss!”
That hit the sweet spot—he loved the sound of “conjugal bliss.” “Right you are! Wherever my wife goes from now on, I stick like a shadow.”
The three young masters traded amazed glances—rumors of the wild playboy’s total overhaul were true after all!
Pointing to a table, Dongfang Chenze added, “We seated you over there. Only three women are attending today, so we paired you with Miss Xiangrong—hope you don’t mind.”
“Mind? Inviting Xiangrong was brilliant—haven’t caught up with her in ages! Wait, who’s the third woman? Why isn’t she seated with us?”
“Princess Consort Yi,” An Mingxuan answered. “She insisted on sitting beside Prince Yi.”
Mengrong really was coming. A wistful pang hit Qianli Xun—how she would’ve loved trading poems and laughs at the same table—yet those days were gone. “Her Highness is royalty, naturally she sits apart. Go tend your guests; I’ll head over and wait for Xiangrong.”
Taking seats at their table, Qianli Xun spotted a jug of rice wine, four cups, and—oddly—a plate of green stir-fried vegetables. “Why a dish of greens? Are we writing poems about dinner?”
Chen Haoyu peered at it. “Looks like wild mountain greens. Heard Lazybones Village’s soil only grows wild veggies, and Prince Yi asked Father-in-law to open a market—bet Father-in-law is promoting them here.”
Qianli Xun tilted her head. “Stuck in the silk shop all day—how do you know that?”
“Father stopped by the shop a couple times and chatted with me.”
Before she could reply, a knot of unfamiliar scholars—several graying at the temples—came over to pay respects. “Long admired Youqi Jushi’s fame—meeting you today is an honor!”
Careful not to tarnish Zhu Shuzhen’s reputation, Qianli Xun rose with a modest curtsy. “Sheltered in the inner quarters, my knowledge is narrow—forgive me for not recognizing you gentlemen.”
One older scholar chuckled. “Don’t mind us—yet you must meet Ye Shaoyun here.”
Blanking on “Ye Shaoyun,” Qianli Xun stared at the man in his forties, kicking herself for skipping literary history in college—unless he was a local celebrity lost to time.
The man smiled and recited softly, “Fallen petals have danced upon the wind, only to send off the dusk in rain; by dawn the courtyard lies half strewn with fading crimson...” [TN: Translator’s rendering]
Recognition snapped—she blurted before he finished, “You’re Ye Mengde?!”
“Indeed I am.”
She knew several of Ye Mengde’s lyrics—by the calendar, he’d be in his forties now, serving as a court official! “Wait—as an imperial official, shouldn’t you be arriving with Prince Yi and the Prefect?”
“Traveled from Yingchang to Huzhou for the completion of my retreat in Shilin Valley, then swung by Lin’an to see old friends,” Ye Mengde laughed. “Neither Prince Yi nor the Prefect knows I’m here—much freer this way!”
Doing quick math, she curtsied low. “Prefect Ye’s carefree spirit fills Shuzhen with admiration.” Chen Haoyu hopped up to bow too. “Lord Prefect!”
Ye Mengde waved it off with a laugh. “No prefects today—only fellow poets making friends through verse!”
Everyone chuckled warm and easy before Ye Mengde excused himself to greet other friends.
Sitting back down, Chen Haoyu asked, “Big celebrity?”
“Huge—his name goes straight into the history books.”
“What about you?”
“Youqi Jushi goes into history too, of course.”
“Think folks in future centuries will know my name?”
“Why ask that?”
“Because I’m your husband!”
Caught off guard, she offered a vague smile. “Maybe.”
Wave after wave of scholars stopped by next, Chen Haoyu bowing beside his wife, swelling with pride at her star power and scheming how to slip “husband of Youqi Jushi” into every hello.
Right then a vision of grace strolled up. In his old life, Chen Haoyu would have ogled and flirted nonstop; bound by his two-year vow, he didn’t feel a flicker of stray thought.
His wife beamed and jumped up to clasp the newcomer’s hands. “What took you so long? Sit—let’s catch up!”
Taking a seat, Xiangrong glanced curious at Chen Haoyu. “And this is...?”
Chen Haoyu chimed in proud, “She’s my wife, and I’m her husband!”
“Second Young Master Chen! A pleasure!”
“I’m that famous?”
“With Youqi Jushi’s fame, everyone naturally follows who she marries.”
“Too much fame brings nothing but gossip—better staying low-key,” Qianli Xun sighed, then introduced her. “My close friend Miss Xiangrong—unmatched on the zither and singing. Wait till she blows you away later!”
Recognizing her name from Huayun Pavilion—a refined venue he’d found too tame back in his rowdy Chunxiang Brothel days—Chen Haoyu admired her ladylike poise and decided to flex a little literary polish for his wife’s sake. “Li Bai wrote: ‘Her face is seen in flower and her dress in cloud.’ [TN: Translated by Xu Yuanchong] Miss Xiangrong’s name must come from ‘hua xiang rong’ (花想容, ‘flowers recall her face’), right?”
*Look at him pulling Li Bai out of thin air!* Qianli Xun marveled inside.
Xiangrong winked slyly at Qianli Xun. “It does come from that line, except my ‘rong’ is written with the character for floss-silk flowers (绒), not appearance (容).”
“Swapping appearance for silk-flower ‘rong’—even cleverer!” Chen Haoyu praised.
Qianli Xun shot him a mock glare. “She’s my friend—I barely got two words in and you won’t stop talking!”
Leaning close to her ear, he whispered, “Just trying to look sharp in front of your friend so she thinks I’m worthy of you!”
She whispered right back, “Save the act—all of Lin’an knows what you’re like.”
Shutting his mouth at once lest she think he was sweet on a pretty girl, Chen Haoyu sat quiet while Xiangrong hid a giggle behind her silk handkerchief.
“What’s funny?” Qianli Xun asked.
Lowering her handkerchief, Xiangrong smiled. “Street gossip claims Youqi Jushi is a fierce tigress who scared Young Master Chen straight. Nonsense—you two are in total harmony, the wife leading and the husband right behind!”
Savoring that phrase, Chen Haoyu glowed, while Qianli Xun felt the quiet truth beneath: hard as the road had been, she was willing to walk it with him.
Before they could chat more, Zhu Xiyan led Lin’an’s officials into the hall surrounding Prince Yi, Qiao Mengrong walking at his side wearing a proud, regal smile.
Every guest rose in unison. “Greetings, Your Highness Prince Yi and Princess Consort Yi!”
Prince Yi raised a hand. “Please be seated—no need for formality.”
Once the royals and officials took the center seats, Zhu Xiyan remained standing. “Gentlemen, thank you for coming! Prince Yi poured heart and silver into the Dongfang Salon, and Young Master Dongfang worked tirelessly—normally I’d step aside, yet for the people’s livelihood, allow me one brief word.” Sweeping the room, he pointed to the tables. “Notice the green dish before you? Please taste it.”
Chopsticks rose across the hall. “Crisp and refreshing—a superb appetizer!” Xiangrong praised, Qianli Xun nodding along as murmurs of praise rippled around.
“These wild greens come straight from Lazybones Village, cooked by Juxiang Tower’s chefs,” Zhu Xiyan announced. “At Prince Yi’s urging to give Lazybones Village and Lin’an’s vagrants an honest trade, I’ve decreed every tavern and inn in Lin’an serve this dish—only it needs a classy, memorable name! I’m imposing on your poetic genius today. Over to you, Young Master Dongfang!”
Stepping onstage for the shining hour of his life, Dongfang Chenze bowed to the royals, officials, and crowd. “Today’s opening round has a twist! Instead of recommending new verse by peers, nominate classical poems—about wild greens or anything fitting—and draw a dish name from the lines! Prince Yi will choose the winner!”
“Fresh and clever!” Qianli Xun praised—and caught Qiao Mengrong’s stare from across the floor, half boast, half warning. Qianli Xun returned a polite nod and smile; Qiao Mengrong froze her face and turned away.
Beside her, Chen Haoyu scanned the sea of scholars hunting for Qiao Wenxian—ready to put on a dazzling display of marital bliss the instant he spotted him.
#CH 296
#TITLE: A Fragile Truce?
At last Chen Haoyu spotted Qiao Wenxian among the scholars—gazing straight toward their table.
*Bet he hasn’t walked over only because I’m sitting here!* Smug he’d tagged along, Chen Haoyu raised a cup of rice wine to test him. Qiao Wenxian didn’t even blink—eyes locked dead on his wife! Scowling, Chen Haoyu tossed the drink back himself.
One scholar stood first. “Gentlemen, I nominate ‘Bo Xi’ from the Odes of Wei in the Book of Songs: ‘O for rain! O for rain! But brightly the sun comes forth. Longingly I think of my husband, till my heart is weary, and my head aches. How shall I get the plant of forgetfulness? I would plant it on the north of my house. Longingly I think of my husband, and my heart is made to ache.’ [TN: Translated by James Legge] That plant of forgetfulness—daylily shoots—is a wild green! Why not name the dish ‘Forget-Sorrow Herb,’ wishing every diner forgets their troubles?”
Murmurs of approval rose.
Another scholar rose. “I nominate Tang poet Xing Qun’s couplet: ‘Wild greens through winter keep their verdant green; before twelfth-month cold, mountain plums bloom everywhere.’ [TN: Translator’s rendering] Let’s call it ‘Verdant Green’!”
Several more followed before Qiao Wenxian stood. “I nominate Tang poet Li Qunyu’s ‘Fisherman on the Yuan River’: ‘Leaning on my oar by the sandy islet as evening falls: river delicacies, wild greens, and peach-blossom rice. One long song into the deepening mist, then homeward across the far green waves of the blue river.’ [TN: Translator’s rendering] Blending two phrases, I propose ‘River-Fresh Green Waves’!”
Warm applause met his suggestion.
Then Ye Mengde stood and bowed. “Greetings, Your Highness Prince Yi and Princess Consort Yi—forgive me for paying respects only now.”
Neither Shen Jianchen nor Qiao Mengrong recognized him until Zhu Xiyan rose to introduce him. “Ye Mengde, Prefect of Yingchang.”
Recognition hit Shen Jianchen—a giant of Song letters! “No formality needed, Prefect Ye.”
Zhu Xiyan teased him half-serious, “Sneaking into Lin’an without paying respects to Prince Yi or letting me play host, hiding in a poetry salon to ambush us—you owe a forfeit!”
Ye Mengde parried smooth as silk. “Left Yingchang on private errands and came here on private errands—how dare I march up and advertise playing hooky from my post? Better lie low! Only Prince Yi’s fame drew me into this salon hoping to blend into the crowd, until your noble relief project moved me so deep I couldn’t stay hidden anymore!”
Laughter swept the hall. Zhu Xiyan pointed at him. “That silver tongue of yours makes a man love and hate you at once!”
Impressed by a man who didn’t fawn over rank and kept his dignity sharp with wit, Shen Jianchen invited, “Why not propose a poem and dish name too, Prefect Ye?”
“Gladly, Your Highness—I’ll nominate a poem of my own! The title’s too long to bother reciting, yet four lines read: ‘Late spring sweeps away the lingering blossoms; thick leaves hold no cicadas yet. From the high terrace the gaze sweeps all four quarters, where green plains float in lush expanse.’ [TN: Translator’s rendering] How about ‘Lush Floating Greens’?”
Cao Ban jumped in grinning. “I know that poem! Longest title in history—let me show off my memory: ‘The Day After Tomorrow, Returning Once More with General Manager Dunli, Transport Commissioner Wu Desu, and Zhang Sitai Past Tianxi Temple to Ascend Yuhua Terrace, Using the Former Rhymes Again’!”
Laughter and nods confirmed the record.
Even if the name wasn’t his literal favorite, Shen Jianchen admired Ye Mengde’s backbone and called it. “‘Lush Floating Greens’ wins! That’s our dish name.”
Ye Mengde bowed his thanks, and Zhu Xiyan urged everyone to promote “Lush Floating Greens” across taverns and household kitchens before handing the stage back to Dongfang Chenze.
“Next up: Classic Appreciation!” Dongfang Chenze announced as Huayu unfurled a massive scroll of Su Shi’s lyric “Dingfengbo: Pay No Heed to the Rain Beating Through the Forest Leaves.”
While scholars dove into spirited critique, Qiao Mengrong cared nothing for poetry—watching her brother like a hawk, pleased he hadn’t shot a single glance toward Zhu Shuzhen. Still, she wasn’t leaving without issuing a warning. Rising from the head table, she strode straight toward Youqi Jushi.
Assuming she was just saying hello to her old friend, Shen Jianchen paid little mind.
*Not a single glance at Prince Yi all morning—what’s she marching over here for?* Qianli Xun wondered, rising with Chen Haoyu, Xiangrong, and Kuqiao to bow.
Qiao Mengrong aimed a barb straight at Chen Haoyu. “No head for poetry, yet Young Master Chen tagged along—don’t trust your wife? Fair enough, surrounded by handsome scholars! Friendly tip: keep a tight leash on her; those eyes hook men on sight.”
Swallowing his anger in public before royalty, Chen Haoyu replied stiff, “I trust my wife completely. She invited me along herself.”
“Mind stepping aside with this lady a minute?” Qiao Mengrong dismissed him. “I’d like a private word with Youqi Jushi.”
Hesitating, Chen Haoyu caught his wife’s quiet murmur—“I’ll be fine”—and squeezed over to a nearby vacated seat with Xiangrong, eyes glued to his wife the whole time.
Sitting down unruffled, Qianli Xun spoke flat. “If you came looking for a fight, Princess Consort Yi, keep your cool—don’t embarrass your rank in public.”
Smirking as she took the chair across, Qiao Mengrong shot back, “Save your lectures. Just wanted you to know: I wasn’t even invited today—Brother insisted on bringing me. Know why?”
“Royals’ private lives don’t interest me.” Qianli Xun turned her ear toward the scholars, yet Qiao Mengrong’s voice cut straight through.
“Interest you or not, hear this: since consummating our marriage, Brother’s heart belongs to me alone, with zero room for anyone else—even insists on taking me everywhere he goes!”
Sadness welled in Qianli Xun—not for herself, for Mengrong. Boasting like this only laid bare how terrified she was inside; high rank couldn’t cure an empty heart.
“Reminds me of a story,” Qianli Xun said softly. “A little girl bragged nonstop to other kids that she had a mother. The other kids laughed—every kid has a mother, why brag? Turns out she had a stepmother who treated her rotten. People always flaunt loudest what they’re starving for most. Follow me, Princess Consort Yi?”
Stung raw, unable to explode in front of Lin’an’s officials, Qiao Mengrong hissed, “Then that stepmother must’ve had other children stealing her love! If I were that girl, I’d wipe out every other child until I was the only one left—then the mother’s love would all be mine!”
“She was a good kid—she wouldn’t do that. Besides, once a mother knows who killed her children, could she ever love the killer?”
“Good?! Being good just gets you stepped on, no matter your rank—even by your own best friend!”
Quiet words, yet heavy as stones—making Qianli Xun’s chest ache. “If I were that girl, I’d use my head to heal the family instead of burning it down. Mengrong, put your energy into your life with Prince Yi and leave me be. Look over there—my husband loves me and changed his whole life for me. How many women get that lucky? I treasure what I have.”
A tremor softened Qiao Mengrong’s eyes. “You really mean that? You didn’t even love Chen Haoyu!”
“Give me your hand,” Qianli Xun asked gently.
Hesitating a beat, Qiao Mengrong laid her hand on the table, and Qianli Xun covered it with hers. Tears welled in Qiao Mengrong’s eyes—Shuzhen’s hand felt just as warm as before.
Across the room, Chen Haoyu exhaled, Shen Jianchen assumed they had patched things up, and Zhu Xiyan—tense since Her Highness walked over—relaxed at last. Standing behind her miss, Kuqiao prayed silently their friendship was healed for good.
Holding Mengrong’s hand, Qianli Xun felt real hope. “Time changes everything. Before I didn’t love Chen Haoyu—now I’m changing, and tomorrow I’ll change more. I’ve made up my mind to grow old with him, and I truly wish you and Prince Yi a lifetime of harmony.”
Meeting Shuzhen’s earnest gaze, Qiao Mengrong forced herself to believe. “All right—I’ll trust you once.” Pulling her hand back, she returned to her seat beside Prince Yi, who wisely asked nothing lest he stir fresh doubts.
The second Qiao Mengrong walked away, Qiao Wenxian strolled over and sat. “Long time no see, Shuzhen.”
Before she could reply, Chen Haoyu shot back to the table. “Not critiquing Dongpo Jushi’s lyric, Qiao Wenxian? What’re you doing over here?”
“Just finished—dropped by to share a drink with Shuzhen. Mind?” Filling Shuzhen’s cup, he poured one for Chen Haoyu too.
“Who gave you permission to call her ‘Shuzhen’?” Chen Haoyu bristled. “Careful your wife and concubine don’t throw a fit and bother my wife!”
“You call her ‘Wife,’ so what’s wrong with ‘Shuzhen’? Always called her that! Come on, drink with me.”
“Fair enough.” Lifting his cup, Chen Haoyu declared, “To the three of us!”
Qianli Xun clinked her cup with his first. “Not bad today.”
Glowing at her praise, he boasted, “I’ll shine every single day till you run out of compliments!”
Envy stabbed Qiao Wenxian—that seat belonged to him in another life. Hearing “Cheers, Wenyuan,” he raised his cup and drained it with them.
“Not critiquing Dongpo Jushi’s piece, Shuzhen?”
“Haven’t had a spare second—Princess Consort Yi barely stood up before you sat down! Plenty of scholars eager to talk anyway, so I’m slacking off.”
Itching to cut Qiao Wenxian short, Chen Haoyu cut in. “Hey, Qiao Wenxian—got a question my wife shouldn’t hear. Lean in.”
Catching his ploy, Qianli Xun played along. “I’ll go sit with Xiangrong a minute—boys’ talk.” Kuqiao trailed her over.
“Petty as ever,” Qiao Wenxian grumbled. “Can’t even let me trade two sentences with Shuzhen?”
“I’m generous on everything except my wife—don’t like other men getting cozy, you most of all!”
“Excuse me, then.” Rising to leave, Qiao Wenxian felt Chen Haoyu tug his sleeve.
“Hold up—I really do have something to ask, and now that she’s gone I don’t even have to whisper!” Chen Haoyu started chuckling before getting a word out.
“Spit it out or I’m walking.”
“Wait, wait, it’s just too funny!” Composing himself, Chen Haoyu stuck up a thumb. “Never guessed it—you, my friend, are a real man!”
Qiao Wenxian blinked in total confusion. “What on earth are you talking about?”
#CH 297
#TITLE: A New Round: Setting the Record Straight
Chen Haoyu leaned in with a sly grin. “Acting like a stiff scholar on the outside—turns out you’re a closet wild man underneath!”
“What’s that supposed to mean?”
“Your concubine Xiaguo’s been hunting high and low for an old brothel matron skilled in bedroom arts—all because of you, right?”
“Pure nonsense—don’t make stuff up!”
“Clerking at the silk shop, a buddy came in buying fabric, got to chatting, and spilled the whole thing! Own up like a man!”
“Your rowdy buddies talk trash all day.”
“Not trash—my buddy knows that exact retired brothel matron, and your concubine hired her! Come on, drop the saint act—makes you way more likable!”
Unruffled, Qiao Wenxian refilled Chen Haoyu’s cup. “The sage said appetite and desire are human nature. I’m flesh and blood just like you—nothing to laugh at.”
“Fair point.” Chen Haoyu took a sip.
Turning the tables, Qiao Wenxian asked, “How about you and Shuzhen—getting along smooth?”
Startled half a beat, Chen Haoyu puffed up. “Smooth? Couldn’t be smoother!”
A wistful sigh escaped Qiao Wenxian. “Know how much I envy you, Chen Haoyu? You married the one woman I wanted my whole life...”
“She’s my wife now—quit pining!”
“I’m not pining—just want her happy.” Downing a full cup, Qiao Wenxian locked eyes with him. “Now that you have her, treat her right for life. If I ever catch wind you’re mistreating her, I’ll find a way to steal her back.”
“Won’t give you half a chance! She’s mine forever—both you and Prince Yi better drop any ideas.”
Glancing toward Prince Yi—listening calmly to the Su Shi commentary—Qiao Wenxian murmured, “Almost forgot: before marrying you, he was the one she loved.”
“She only loves me now!”
Filling both cups one more time, Qiao Wenxian clinked and drank, then rose. “Next round’s starting—time I rejoined my friends.”
“Scram already so my wife can sit back down with me!”
Qiao Wenxian nodded a polite farewell smile toward Shuzhen, who smiled back before returning to her seat with Xiangrong.
“What were you and Wenyuan whispering about?” she asked.
Not about to tell her Wenyuan still carried a torch, he brushed it off. “Boring men’s stuff.”
Letting it drop, she turned to Xiangrong. “With a zither onstage and you invited, you’re bound to sing today—whose lyric are you performing?”
“Silly question—I only ever sing Youqi Jushi’s lyrics!”
Sheepish at forgetting, Qianli Xun asked, “When are you up?”
“Right now—watch!”
Onstage, Dongfang Chenze wrapped up the Su Shi round. “Wonderful insights—certainly Dongpo Jushi’s lyric will flow down the ages! Next comes a brand-new round I call ‘Setting the Verse Straight’!”
Curious murmurs swept the floor—no salon had ever held such a round.
“Recently,” Dongfang Chenze declared, “pamphlets handed out free on the streets trashed Youqi Jushi’s new lyric ‘Jianzi Mulanhua: Spring Complaint’!”
Across the room, ice crashed over Qiao Mengrong’s heart. *So that’s why Brother bankrolled this salon—to scrub Zhu Shuzhen’s name clean!* Only minutes ago she had almost believed Shuzhen’s warm hands and tears; now fury burned hotter than ever. *Never falling for her sweet lies again—she dies, no matter what!*
Noticing Mengrong’s face go rigid, Shen Jianchen resolved to lay the whole pamphlet matter bare back at Youlin Retreat. For now, he reached out and squeezed her hand to reassure her—yet every squeeze only fanned Qiao Mengrong’s hatred brighter.
Onstage, Dongfang Chenze fired up the room. “If the critique made sense, fine—yet reading it cover to cover, every line is drivel written by somebody out to smear Youqi Jushi who plainly doesn’t understand poetry!”
Scholars erupted in chorus. “Spot-on! Read that trash and lost my appetite!” “Tore mine up and burned it—heard Prince Yi sent Commander Lin and Chief Constable Wen to shut it down on the street! First-class move!”
Voices rose in unison: “Thank you, Your Highness, for defending poetic integrity!”
*Everyone knows you funded this salon, Prince Yi,* Qiao Wenxian thought grimly. *What kind of spot does this put Shuzhen in—and Princess Consort Yi?*
Still gripping Mengrong’s hand, Shen Jianchen gave a brief nod. “No need for thanks. Carry on.”
“So today’s round sets the record straight!” Dongfang Chenze announced. “Young Masters Cao and An will record every critique to print and distribute free after the salon!”
Cheers of “Hear, hear!” rang out. “And since Youqi Jushi herself is here, let’s invite her to say a few words!”
Realizing why Shen Jianchen had backed the salon—and catching Mengrong’s frozen glare across the hall—Qianli Xun felt every ounce of her usual eloquence evaporate. Criticize the pamphleteer? Thank Prince Yi? Anything she said would make things worse.
Right as she froze, Chen Haoyu popped straight up. “Gentlemen, Youqi Jushi is my wife—she’s feeling a touch unwell, so I’ll speak for her!”
Relief washed over Qianli Xun as Chen Haoyu boomed out, “Didn’t even know folks were printing pamphlets smearing my wife till today—if I’d caught them, I’d have ripped them to confetti and torched the lot! Seeing all you decent people today proves good folks still outnumber rats! On my wife’s behalf, thank you all—and huge thanks to Prince Yi and Princess Consort Yi, two truly great people! And if you two are that great, that proves His Majesty is the greatest of all!”
Blunt as his speech was, praising His Majesty hit the bull’s-eye. Beaming with pride at his son-in-law, Zhu Xiyan rose to piggyback right on it. “Because His Majesty is so benevolent, the realm thrives—reclaiming Yanjing at last, granting a grand amnesty across the empire! Because Prince Yi and Princess Consort Yi are so gracious, we enjoy this magnificent salon—and this round to set poetry straight! Carry on, Young Master Dongfang!”
*Flattering the throne at every turn,* Shen Jianchen thought with silent disgust. *The Jin stripped Yanjing bare before handing back an empty shell, and the court throws a victory party like it’s a historic triumph—what a farce!*
“To kick off our critique,” Dongfang Chenze announced, “Miss Xiangrong will sing ‘Jianzi Mulanhua: Spring Complaint’!”
Gliding gracefully onstage, Xiangrong sat behind the zither. Plaintive, soaring notes hushed the entire floor as her crystal voice carried the lines across the hall: “I walk alone, sit alone, sing alone, drink alone, and sleep alone. Standing lone, my spirit hurts. A light cold caresses me.” [TN: Translated by Tony Barnstone and Chou Ping]
Oblivious to the music, Chen Haoyu watched his wife’s eyes fixed across the hall—assuming she was gazing at Prince Yi, moved by his grand gesture.
Jealousy pricked him. “Moved by Prince Yi, Wife?”
Startled to realize he mistook her anxious look at Mengrong for staring at the prince, Qianli Xun lowered her eyes to her wine cup as Xiangrong’s song lingered on: “Who can see how tears have washed off half my makeup, sorrow and sickness have joined hands, how I trim the lamp’s wick till it’s gone and dream still does not come?”
“Why so quiet?”
“Nothing to say.”
“Regretting marrying me again?”
“Not what you think.”
Convinced Prince Yi hadn’t given up—yet powerless to pick a fight with a royal—Chen Haoyu vowed to double down on winning his wife’s heart. Turning his attention onstage, he found himself drawn in by Xiangrong’s singing of his wife’s words and resolved to memorize every poem she ever wrote alongside his tea lessons.
Thunderous calls for an encore followed Xiangrong’s final note, until Dongfang Chenze promised a full solo concert of Youqi Jushi’s lyrics once the critique ended.
Returning to their table, Xiangrong caught Chen Haoyu’s thumbs-up—“Great singing, and my wife’s lyric is even better!”—and teased, “Three words out of Young Master Chen and it’s all ‘my wife’! Too sweet for me—I’d better move tables!”
Qianli Xun grabbed her arm. “Ignore him! I’d be bored silly without you.”
“Bored with your husband right here?”
“Different—you’re my friend.”
Onstage, Dongfang Chenze opened the critique. “The pamphlet slammed Youqi Jushi for writing sad, wistful verse—which only proves its author understands neither poetry nor life! Dongpo Jushi himself wrote: ‘Men have sorrow and joy; they part or meet again; the moon is bright or dim and she may wax or wane. There has been nothing perfect since the olden days.’ [TN: Translated by Xu Yuanchong] The moon is only full one night a month; human fulfillment is just as fleeting. Poetry voices human feeling—if the critic stood here today, I’d ask if his life has been flawless every single day. Even half an imperfect day vindicates Youqi Jushi!”
Beside Shen Jianchen, Qiao Mengrong slipped her hand out of his grip. Relieved his palm could breathe—and noting her face looked calmer—Shen Jianchen relaxed, assuming she’d let it go.
Inside, Qiao Mengrong seethed: Bai Meiruo had no clue her own words were getting shredded in front of every scholar in Lin’an! Scanning the officials, she wished Lin Jue were here so she could whisper whose wife was being mocked and watch him blow up—not that a rough military man got invited to a poetry salon.
Then her gaze landed on Qiao Wenxian, and a cold smile curved her lips—waiting to see who tore into Miao Zhiyu’s criticism next.
#CH 298
#TITLE: Making an Excuse to Leave
Right on cue for Qiao Mengrong, Lin Jue’s second uncle Lin Zixiao spoke up. “‘I walk alone, sit alone, sing alone, drink alone, and sleep alone’—brilliant phrasing! Yet someone called repeating ‘alone’ five times a sign of running out of words! Laughable—plainly written by an amateur who dared print their ignorance for all Lin’an to ridicule! Let me analyze these lines—”
“One moment, Second Uncle Lin!” Qiao Mengrong cut in. “Why not let Young Master Qiao analyze those lines instead?”
Unaware Miao Zhiyu had coined that exact jibe at the earlier gathering, Shen Jianchen saw no harm. Qianli Xun, however, tensed at once—why drag Wenyuan into ripping apart his own wife?
Unable to refuse royalty, Lin Zixiao yielded with a bow. “No one appreciates Youqi Jushi’s verse better than Young Master Qiao—after you!”
Rising politely, Qiao Wenxian—clearly clueless his wife had written the jab—launched into his analysis. “Just as Second Uncle Lin noted, repeating ‘alone’ five times is masterful. Each beat shifts nuance and builds heavier weight, sharpening the theme of spring sorrow. Spring is meant for outings with friends, family, or a lover—yet the lady in this lyric walks, sits, sings, drinks, and sleeps all alone. How could spring not ache?”
Beside Qianli Xun, Chen Haoyu leaned close and whispered, “I’ll keep you company everywhere—and as for sleeping alone, say the word and I’m ready anytime!”
She shoved his head sideways. “Listen and quit interrupting!”
Qiao Wenxian went on, “Women locked away in inner quarters lead lonely lives, wed to men they scarcely know, bound for life for better or worse. Youqi Jushi voices the hidden ache of women across every age—reminding us men to appreciate our wives’ quiet hardship and cherish them more at home. Her poetry truly enriches every household.”
*Leave it to Wenyuan to turn a boudoir lament into a social manifesto,* Qianli Xun mused.
As other scholars took turns speaking, Qiao Mengrong rose and walked straight to Qiao Wenxian’s table. Qianli Xun’s eyes locked on her, while Shen Jianchen stayed unbothered.
Neighboring scholars cleared seats at once as Qiao Wenxian rose to bow. “Princess Consort Yi.”
Settling into a chair, Qiao Mengrong smiled cold. “Magnificent critique, Young Master Qiao. Know who you just tore to shreds?”
Puzzled, he replied, “Since Your Highness asks, you must know.”
“Of course—that’s why I walked over. The one who mocked Youqi Jushi for ‘running out of words’ is your own wife, Miao Zhiyu! How does that feel?”
Disbelief crossed his face. “Zhiyu may not match Shuzhen’s gift, yet she’s analyzed Shuzhen’s lyrics for years—she’d never miss the power of repetition!”
“Either Madam Miao plays you like a fiddle, or you’re blind—most of that pamphlet came straight from your wife’s mouth! Now you’ve publicly trashed your own wife in front of Lin’an’s finest. Going home tonight to cherish her like Youqi Jushi’s verse taught you—or read her the riot act?”
Dawning horror struck Qiao Wenxian. “Did Your Highness... put her up to it?”
“Whether I did or not, you’re the one who humiliated your own wife in public! Imagine if everyone here found out Madam Miao wrote that trash—where would you hide your face?”
Leaving Qiao Wenxian frozen speechless, Qiao Mengrong rose satisfied, venting her pent-up bile at last—hoping he marched straight home and tore into Miao Zhiyu. Passing back toward her seat, she caught Zhu Shuzhen’s troubled stare, curled her lip in a chilling sneer, and looked away.
A shiver ran down Qianli Xun’s spine. Watching Wenyuan slip quietly out of the hall moments later confirmed everything—Mengrong was lashing out through him. She hoped Wenyuan kept his temper; she’d already crushed Miao Zhiyu face-to-face once without needing a marital brawl on top.
Her appetite for the salon gone, Qianli Xun beckoned Kuqiao for brush and paper, wrote out her prepared lyric, and handed the fresh sheet to Xiangrong. “Feeling unwell—I’m heading out early. Copy this out for me during the live-writing round, all right?”
“Where do you feel unwell?” Chen Haoyu asked at once.
Glancing at the wet ink, Xiangrong nodded. “Leave it to me.”
Slipping out quietly while attention stayed onstage, Qianli Xun climbed into their carriage with Chen Haoyu right behind.
“Really sick? Want to stop at an apothecary?” he worried.
“Just stuffy in that packed hall—fresh air cleared my head. Let’s go home.”
A jealous frown crept onto his brow. “Qiao Wenxian walked out, and you bolted right after—bored once he left?”
Qianli Xun snapped, “Talk sense! I leave when I feel like leaving—what’s Wenyuan got to do with it? Quit fussing like a gossipy auntie!”
“‘Wenyuan’—all warm and cozy, while I always get full-named ‘Chen Haoyu’!”
“Give yourself a courtesy name if you want me to use one!”
“Couldn’t care less about fancy scholar names! Just drop the surname and call me ‘Haoyu’ like the family does!”
She let out a laugh. “Nope—full name rolls off the tongue too good now, habit’s stuck.”
Relaxing at her laugh, Chen Haoyu called to the driver to head home. Sitting up front, Kuqiao smiled at their warm banter—her thoughts drifting to Bai Feng out in the bamboo grove, hoping her parents liked him as much as Kujue did.
Meanwhile, Qiao Wenxian marched straight into Miao Zhiyu’s quarters the moment he got home. Setting down her book, she brightened. “Salon over already? Tell me everything!”
Holding down his anger, he sat. “Huge success—likely the only salon left standing in Lin’an, complete with a new round setting the record straight over those pamphlets slandering Shuzhen’s ‘Spring Complaint.’”
Her smile faded. “Her again? Heard Prince Yi funded it—makes sense now! Wonder how Princess Consort Yi took that.”
Not a shred of guilt! Qiao Wenxian’s temper snapped. “Miao Zhiyu! Do you know what you’ve done?!”
Never spoken to so harshly before, she blinked. “What are you talking about, Wenyuan?”
“Slandering Shuzhen in print and playing innocent?!”
*Zhu Shuzhen must’ve tattled at the salon to wreck my marriage!* “Don’t listen to Youqi Jushi’s lies—she’s driving a wedge between us!”
“Still smearing Shuzhen?! Princess Consort Yi told me herself!”
Caught dead to rights—baffled why Her Highness had ratted her out—Miao Zhiyu dropped straight to her knees in tears. “Don’t blame me, Wenyuan! Princess Consort Yi hates Youqi Jushi and dragged Bai Meiruo and me into it! You know how much I admire Youqi Jushi’s poetry, yet Her Highness is royalty and kin to the Qiaos—how could I say no?!”
Her tearful plea took the wind out of his sails. “Get up.”
Wiping her eyes, she rose while he warned, “Even royals aren’t always right—use your own judgment next time.”
“I will, Wenyuan.”
Having eavesdropped outside, Xiaguo strolled right in. “Bet Sister didn’t write that pamphlet just for Princess Consort Yi—bet she took her grudge out on Miss’s poems because Wenyuan still keeps Miss in his heart!”
Miao Zhiyu wheeled on her. “Who let you barge into my room?!”
“You barged into mine plenty—didn’t know Sister was so petty!”
Stuck for an answer, Miao Zhiyu pleaded, “Don’t listen to her, Wenyuan! I knew all about you and Youqi Jushi before we wed—why would I hold a grudge now?”
Knowing Xiaguo had nailed the truth, Qiao Wenxian laid every card on the table. “Let’s put it all in the open! Shuzhen was the only woman I ever wanted to marry—I only ended up with you two because I couldn’t have her. I’ve accepted my fate and want a peaceful home. Can’t you two stop clawing at each other?!”
Stepping up to Miao Zhiyu, he barked, “Yes, Shuzhen’s in my heart—so what? I still married you! If you couldn’t stomach that, why marry me? Now quit complaining!”
Rounding on Xiaguo, he snapped, “You chased me down to be my concubine, got your wish, and I treat you decent for Shuzhen’s sake—yet you push your luck against the primary wife! Without me shielding you, she could toss you out tomorrow!”
Pausing at the doorway, he dropped the hammer. “Keep slandering people outside or starting wars inside this house, and don’t think I won’t divorce a wife and kick out a concubine!”
Terrified by a side of Wenyuan they’d never seen, both dropped to their knees. “We won’t dare again, Wenyuan!”
“We’ll see.” Marching back to his study, Qiao Wenxian reflected that a loveless marriage ran on authority, not tenderness—and now peace would hold.
Getting up, Xiaguo apologized first. “Forgive my big mouth, Sister—no more backtalk.”
Miao Zhiyu softened too. “You’re welcome in my room anytime, Sister.”
“Quit fixating on Miss,” Xiaguo advised gently. “Her chapter with Wenyuan is closed. Watching Prince Yi around her back when I was her maid, I knew he’d capture her heart sooner or later. Princess Consort Yi’s the one losing sleep—why let her use you as a weapon and make Wenyuan mad?”
Miao Zhiyu exhaled lighter. “Smart girl—you woke me right up! Drop by often so Wenyuan sees us getting along.”
Once outside, Aimo whispered, “Really making peace with Madam, Concubine Xia?”
“Even if it’s an act, make it convincing—if Wenyuan freezes me out, she’ll chew me alive.”
Inside, Youfan asked the same. Picking up her book, Miao Zhiyu replied coolly, “If I’m done obsessing over Youqi Jushi, why sweat Xiaguo? Plus Qiao wives are known for treating concubines fair—keeps the elders happy.”
#CH 299
#TITLE: After the Dongfang Salon
Once Zhu Shuzhen left, Qiao Mengrong sat a few restless minutes, whispered an excuse to her brother, and departed too.
The Dongfang Poetry Salon didn’t miss a beat. Xiangrong sang several more of Youqi Jushi’s lyrics by popular demand, then came the live-writing round where Xiangrong brushed out Shuzhen’s fresh lyric alongside the scholars. Drawing lots to critique one another’s new works on the spot sent the hall into a frenzy—Chen Haofei shone bright enough to get his name remembered by every guest. Membership fees poured in for three-year terms, filling the salon’s coffers, and Prince Yi closed the event declaring the Dongfang Salon number one in Lin’an.
Back at Youlin Retreat, Qiao Mengrong didn’t even touch tea before summoning Matron Zou.
“Didn’t dare report yesterday with His Highness home,” Matron Zou said once seated. “Eldest Young Master Chen will keep helping, yet he wants Your Highness to pull strings in Bianliang for the Chen tea shops first.”
Qiao Mengrong fumed. “Hasn’t delivered a thing and wants upfront payment?! Tell him Bianliang waits until Zhu Shuzhen is dead!”
“Told him that exact thing, and he caved. Only Chen Manor picks its business heir next month, and he begged Your Highness to attend and back him—says royal presence seals the deal.”
Weighing it a beat, Qiao Mengrong asked, “Think I should go?”
“Commoners would kill for royalty gracing a family vote—depends if Your Highness feels like it.”
“Feels like it doesn’t matter—what matters is whether it serves me.” A smirk curved her lips. “I’ll go! Watching Youqi Jushi lose face sounds fun.”
Matron Zou blinked. “What’s the Chen inheritance got to do with her?”
“Chen Haoyu’s her husband—if he loses out, she’s humiliated. Her misery is my joy.”
“Fair enough—treat it like an outing. Chen Haodong said if you agree, he’ll approach the Shao Escort Agency—”
“Shao Escort Agency?”
“Biggest escort outfit in Lin’an—fighters just as tough as Youqi Jushi.”
“Escorts guard cargo—would they take a hit?”
“Chen Haodong says enough silver makes ghosts turn millstones.”
“Tell him to go—silver’s no object.”
Rising to leave, Matron Zou paused to plead, “Your Highness, Prince Yi dotes on you now—came home early yesterday, took you to the salon today, grants your every whim. Can’t we let the poetess go?”
Qiao Mengrong scoffed. “Tell her, Xilan.”
Xilan spilled the salon’s “Setting the Verse Straight” round and Prince Yi’s bankrolling—all to whitewash that shameless poetess.
Matron Zou frowned. “Why not host his own salon instead of funding Young Master Dongfang?”
“Cover story!” Qiao Mengrong hissed. “Now I know why Brother tried leaving me home!”
“How can His Highness still be so infatuated? Let me talk sense into him tonight—”
“Don’t you dare! He’ll think I put you up to it and call me petty! Just help me erase her in the dark.”
Sighing helplessly, Matron Zou walked out.
Shen Jianchen returned before sunset after finishing Judicial Office paperwork, relieved to find Mengrong calm and civil. Still wanting zero shadows between them, he took her hand and led her up the wooden terrace overlooking the sweeping hills to talk things through.
Determined to play unbothered so he wouldn’t watch her moves, Qiao Mengrong beat him to it. “Want to talk about the pamphlets, Brother?”
“How’d you guess?”
Turning to face him, she smiled soft. “Grew up together—I read your mind.”
Taking her hands, he said earnest, “My fault for hurting you and driving you to print them. Recalling the pamphlets and holding today’s round were only to fix my own mess.”
“Not your fault, Brother—I was petty.”
Pulling her into his embrace, he murmured, “Forgive how selfishly I chased my own feelings before and broke your heart. Never again—I’m devoted to you alone.”
*His own feelings—so this reborn Brother never really loved me at all.* “Do you love me, Brother?”
Stuck, he hedged. “We’re husband and wife—that says everything.”
“I want to hear you say it.”
“You’re the woman in my arms right now.”
“I’d rather you held someone else in your arms yet kept me in your heart.”
He forced a light laugh. “Then you’d demand my arms too! Quit poking at empty words—being right here with you is what counts.”
Hugging him tight, Qiao Mengrong felt cold inside: she held his body, never his heart—and the woman sitting inside his heart had to go.
That night, Chen Haodong came home from the tea shop riding high—clan votes lined up, Uncle leaning his way, Princess Consort Yi coming to back him next month! Feeling breezy, he strolled over to Ye Anya’s courtyard to share the news and warn her to behave.
Lamp glowing inside, he paused outside the door as Ye Anya’s voice floated out reciting love verses: “‘On high, we’d be two love birds flying wing to wing; on earth, two trees with branches twined from spring to spring.’ [TN: Translated by Xu Yuanchong] ‘Though my sash grows loose, I have no regret; for my beloved’s sake, I waste away gladly.’ ‘One day without seeing you, and longing drives me mad.’ ‘Playful and fond, unafraid of what others might guess, collapsing into his arms fully dressed.’ [TN: Translator’s rendering] Which lines fit best, Xiaocui?”
“Second Young Madam’s lines match your heart best, Miss!”
Love poems—and picking the boldest line of all?! Rage obliterated his good mood. Boom! He kicked the door clean open.
Ye Anya jumped to her feet while Xiaocui lunged to hide the paper—too late. Chen Haodong ripped the sheet from the desk, hands shaking as he saw a love letter addressed to “My Beloved.”
Shredding it in her face, he seized her collar. “Who’s your lover?! Who’s the bastard?!”
Knowing a beating was coming either way, Ye Anya spat straight back, “The man I love—certainly not you!”
Crack! His palm lashed across her cheek, splitting her lip. “Shameless whore!”
Clutching her bleeding mouth, she glared. “Look who’s talking! Think I don’t know the filth you do outside?!”
Xiaocui rushed to help, only for Chen Haodong to kick her down, drag her outside by the scruff, slam the door, and lunge back at Ye Anya. “Acting shameless for another man while playing ice queen with me?!”
“Because I don’t love you!”
“I’ll show you love tonight!” Dragging her into the bedchamber, he hurled her onto the bed.
“Animal!” she sneered. “All you know how to act like is a beast!”
Enraged, he grabbed a feather duster off the table and whipped her raw, welts bursting through her clothes while she screamed in agony—yet refused to beg. “I’ve had enough of you! Forget mutual divorce—write a divorce letter and kick me out right now!”
Exhausted and disgusted by the bloody welts, Chen Haodong flung the duster down. “With heir selection next month? Dream on! I won’t divorce you now, won’t divorce you ever, and never a mutual divorce—I’ll keep you locked beside me and torture you every day so you never fly off with your lover! Mood-killer!”
“Bastard!” she cried after his slamming door.
Rushing in to tend the bleeding welts with salve, Xiaocui begged in tears, “Just humor him next time, Miss, or you’re the one hurting!”
“I’d rather die than bend to him!” Eyes burning, Ye Anya thought of next month’s heir selection and hatched a desperate gamble. “Once these cuts heal, Xiaocui, invite Young Master Cao to Yunlai Inn.”
Xiaocui nearly dropped the salve jar. “An inn, Miss?!”
“Don’t ask—one last roll of the dice for my freedom! If Chen Haodong wrecks my life, I’ll wreck his too. Still got that medical prescription safe?”
“Safe and sound—why ask now?”
“Saved it for the exact day I needed it! You broke faith with me, Chen Haodong—don’t blame me for striking back!”
Steaming mad, Chen Haodong slipped out of Chen Manor to blow off steam with Chun Jinchan at their new hideout, sneaking back before dawn to pretend he’d slept at home. After breakfast and a quick swing by the tea shop, he headed to the Shao Escort Agency to call on Shao Zhengye.
Just back from a long escort run to Qingzhou, washed and sipping morning tea with his son Shao Zhize, Shao Zhengye welcomed Chen Haodong in assuming he had cargo to ship.
“Bringing me business, Eldest Young Master?”
“Huge business—name your own price!”
Trading suspicious glances with his son, Shao Zhengye asked, “What cargo?”
“Not cargo.” Chen Haodong drew a crisp finger across his throat. “Interested?”
#CH 300
#TITLE: Picking the Right Go-Between
Shao Zhengye shot it down on the spot. “Shao Escort Agency runs an honest trade—never murder or arson. Wrong door, Eldest Young Master.”
Stung by the blunt brush-off, Chen Haodong pulled out his trump card. “Know who sent me?”
“Wouldn’t budge if it were anyone.”
“The highest-ranking lady in all of Lin’an—Princess Consort Yi!”
To his shock, father and son traded a glance and turned the rebuke right back on him. “Want somebody dead, don’t drag Princess Consort Yi’s name through the mud! Prince Yi visited our hall—peerless fighter, upright to the bone. His wife would never hire assassins! Watch your tongue before you smear royalty. I’ll pretend I heard nothing—Zhize, show our guest out!”
Shao Zhize rose. “After you, Eldest Young Master.”
Humiliated, Chen Haodong stood to leave—then flashed a nasty smirk and turned back. “Quit playing saint, Master Shao! You’ve got blood on your hands too—think I don’t know?”
A jolt froze Shao Zhengye, masked in half a second. “Watch your mouth before walking out of here gets tough.”
“What, gonna kill me too—after that noble speech? Let’s see if one name makes you sweat: Yan Chi!”
In a flash, Shao Zhize clamped a steel grip around Chen Haodong’s throat. “What else do you know?!”
Choking yet cocky, Chen Haodong gasped, “Dare kill Yan Chi’s father, yet too righteous to help Princess Consort Yi?!”
Shao Zhize squeezed harder. “One more crazy word and you’ll be the first corpse ever carried out of Shao Escort Agency! Talk—what else?!”
Terrified of losing his neck over Princess Consort Yi’s errands, Chen Haodong caved. “Mercy! That’s all! Just overheard Yan Chi grumbling—maybe I misheard!”
Shao Zhize loosened his grip. “Keep your mouth shut and we stay friends.”
Shao Zhengye pressed sharp, “What’s your tie to this Yan Chi?”
“Barely knew him! Passing acquaintance—stayed a few days at our chuiwan grounds and vanished.”
“Friendly warning: stay clear of that man, or you’ll bring ruin down on the Chens. Out!”
Brushed out like dirt, Chen Haodong slunk off cursing his luck—no hired blade meant risking Princess Consort Yi skipping next month’s vote, and he was dead set on flaunting her backing before the clan! He’d have to dig up another killer fast. As for Yan Chi’s parting warning back then—just hot air from a drifter, nothing to lose sleep over.
Once Chen Haodong cleared out, Shao Zhengye asked his son, “When did Prince Yi get back from Bianliang?”
“A month ago—meant to tell you right before Chen Haodong barged in.”
“Promised to pay respects once he returned—can’t break my word. I’ll visit Youlin Retreat tomorrow.”
“He’s serving as Judicial Investigator now, Father—mostly at the Judicial Office. Plus while you were on the road, he sent Lazybones Village back to the hills with a trade and got the yamen building earthen homes and cave dwellings for every vagrant in town!”
“Far better than His Majesty’s Juyang Yuan, Anji Fang, and Louze Yuan combined!” Shao Zhengye beamed. “Bring two jugs of fine wine—tomorrow we call on Prince Yi at the Judicial Office!”
Meanwhile, back from the salon, Qianli Xun remembered her promise to Master Jiang. She hadn’t dared even glance at Prince Yi at the salon with Mengrong glaring, yet she never broke her word. Sneaking into the Judicial Office at night? He slept at Youlin Retreat. Visiting by day? Risk bumping into Mengrong. How absurdly complicated seeing one friend was in the Song Dynasty—unlike the modern world where Shen Jianchen came running at one phone call.
Opting for the clean route, she penned a quick note, folded it, and summoned Hongdou. “Run this to the silk shop for Young Master—ask him to drop it off at the Judicial Office for Prince Yi when he has a moment. And don’t breathe a word about why I went to the Qiantang Gang!”
“Not a syllable, Miss!”
Guided to the silk shop’s accounting room by a clerk, Hongdou heard brisk abacus clacking—and burst out laughing at the doorway seeing Young Master perched behind the head ledger like a grizzled old bookkeeper.
Setting down his brush, Chen Haoyu frowned. “What’s funny?”
“Never pictured Young Master looking this dead serious—it’s great!”
“Better! Miss send you?”
Handing over the folded note, Hongdou relayed her instructions. Opening it, Chen Haoyu muttered, “My wife says yes to everybody! Funny—why’s Master Jiang asking her to play go-between?”
“Ask Miss tonight—above a maid’s pay grade!”
Folding it into his robe, he nodded. “Tell her I’ll finish this ledger and head straight over.”
Stepping out, Hongdou spotted Qinggua smoothly pitching bolts of silk to a customer and grinning bright once he saw her off. “Come to buy silk?”
“Can’t afford this stuff! Dropped a note for Young Master and checked how you’re doing so I can brag to Chouya.”
“Don’t bother—I tell her every night! Clerking pays triple being a valet, plus Young Master says once he joins the family business he’ll bring me along! Regretting picking Asheng over me yet, Sister Hongdou?”
“Oh, tearing my hair out with regret!” she joked back.
“Too late! I’m loyal to Chouya just like Young Master is to Young Madam!”
“Guess I’ll go bash my head on a block of tofu—keep clerking, I’m off!”
Watching her go, Qinggua muttered smugly, “Pretty as Hongdou is, Chouya’s way sweeter.”
Finishing his accounts, Chen Haoyu rode to the Judicial Office—feeling a sheepish twinge at the gate where he’d once been locked up, vowing again to walk the straight and narrow.
Surprised by his visit yet receiving him for Qianli Xun’s sake, Shen Jianchen invited him to sit in his modest courtyard—leaving Chen Haoyu baffled why a prince traded Youlin Retreat for this bare little office.
Handing over the note with both hands, Chen Haoyu said, “From my wife, Your Highness.”
Reading the request for Master Jiang, Shen Jianchen nodded. “Tell Youqi Jushi not to worry—easy fix. I’ll visit Master Jiang myself.”
Watching Prince Yi say yes without blinking made jealousy prick Chen Haoyu’s gut. Rising quick, he bowed. “Errand done—I won’t intrude.”
“Stay a minute—I’ve been wanting a private word with you anyway.”
Unable to refuse a royal, Chen Haoyu sat stiffly back down.
“She asks you to run notes to me, and you don’t mind?” Shen Jianchen asked.
“Whatever my wife asks, I’m glad to do.”
A bitter smile tugged Shen Jianchen’s lips. “Funny how life works—you didn’t lift a finger and won the woman I wanted, while I fought tooth and nail and still watched her marry you. Chen Haoyu... I’d trade places with you in a heartbeat.”
“Even if we could trade, I wouldn’t.”
“True—nobody who holds her would ever give her up. So I won’t ask for anything else—just treat her right. If she’s happy, I’m at peace. If you ever hurt her, I won’t hesitate to take your place.”
First Qiao Wenxian, now Prince Yi! Dropping deference, Chen Haoyu spoke firm. “I’ll treat her ten times better and never give Your Highness an opening! Please devote yourself to Princess Consort Yi and let my wife go—the wedding raid still stings fresh. Would you really break Princess Consort Yi’s heart twice?”
Catching his slip, Shen Jianchen eased back. “Relax—just talking, didn’t mean to rattle you. Past is past; forward is the only road.” Chatting neutrally a few minutes more, he saw Chen Haoyu off—then saddled a horse and rode straight to the Qiantang Gang alone, determined to show Master Jiang he’d bet on the exact right person.
Minutes after Chun Jinmo stepped out, a runner burst in reporting Prince Yi waiting alone on horseback at the gate. Overjoyed, Master Jiang didn’t even jam on shoes—he sprinted out barefoot and dropped to his knees. “Your Highness honors our humble gang!”
Eyeing his bare feet, Shen Jianchen teased, “No shoes—that how you welcome guests?”
“So thrilled to hear Your Highness arrived I forgot shoes existed—bare feet prove how eager I am to greet you!”
Slick talker for a rough customer. “You asked someone whose face I prize very much to bridge us—so here I am. Going to invite me inside?”
*Betting on the poetess was the smartest move of my life!* Master Jiang sprang up, ordered his men to tend the horse, and ushered Prince Yi inside.
#CH 301
#TITLE: Two Visits, Two Motives
Last time Shen Jianchen came here, he’d traded punches and left—never noticing how plush the Qiantang Gang’s headquarters actually was. Clearly Master Jiang had stacked up a small fortune.
Refusing the chief’s seat before royalty, Master Jiang stood hunched even after Prince Yi sat—only taking a chair when the prince threatened to walk out, then sliding closer at his prodding.
“You had Youqi Jushi build the bridge,” Shen Jianchen teased. “I walked across it—now you go mute?”
Master Jiang perked right up. “Never dealt with royalty before, waited for Your Highness to speak first! Truth is, my boys and I have done plenty of dirt over the years—didn’t care when I was single. Now I’ve got a wife, hoping for a bouncing son, and want my brothers settled into honest lives under solid backing. Nobody in Lin’an stands taller or straighter than Prince Yi—point us onto a clean road, Your Highness!”
Shen Jianchen had already mapped out their landing spot on the ride over. “You run half—no, probably all—of Lin’an’s gambling houses?”
“One word from Your Highness and I shutter every last one!”
“Plus the black market...”
“Shut it down today! Nothing gets past Your Highness!”
“And murder-for-hire...”
“Sworn off for good!”
Seeing genuine grit behind the bluster, Shen Jianchen laid out terms. “Murder and arson stop dead—zero exceptions.”
“Already washing my hands clean!”
“Keep the black market running—if you close it, someone worse opens one. Run by you, the yamen keeps tabs and cracks cases faster.”
“Brilliant, Your Highness! Makes catching crooks a breeze.”
“Shut half the gambling dens, and donate half the profits from the rest to support the elderly and infirm in Lazybones Village.”
Delighted he got to keep half his income, Master Jiang agreed on the spot. “And what honest post do you have in mind for us, Your Highness?”
“Lin’an’s river ferries drive commercial taxes, yet the yamen can’t police them—most of all along the Qiantang River. Since you’re the Qiantang Gang, take charge of policing the Qiantang ferries—”
Before he even finished, Master Jiang hit his knees banging his forehead to the floor. “Living bodhisattva to the Qiantang Gang!”
“Counties appoint Ferry Superintendents locally; as Military Commissioner, I hold that authority. Starting today, you’re Ferry Superintendent along the Qiantang River.”
Nearly weeping with joy, Master Jiang kowtowed again. “An official post in my lifetime—changing our whole family’s destiny! Now I can hold my head high before my future son!”
Calling for brush and paper, Shen Jianchen drafted a recommendation letter straight to Prefect Zhu. Accepting it with trembling reverence, Master Jiang pledged lifelong service—and reassured the prince nobody linked him to the black market since a trusted deputy ran it single-channel, a channel he’d already used once to tip off Lin Jue on a child trafficker.
Escorting Prince Yi out until horse and rider vanished down the street, Master Jiang muttered in awe, “Thought he came just for Youqi Jushi—turns out he’s looking out for Lin’an’s people too. I underestimated him!” Turning back grinning ear to ear, he roared for his wife—only to be reminded she’d gone out with friends—and beamed as his men hailed him “Lord Ferry Superintendent!”
Little did he know Chun Jinmo hadn’t gone to meet friends—she had driven straight to Youlin Retreat to pitch an alliance to Princess Consort Yi.
Inside Youlin Retreat, Miao Zhiyu and Bai Meiruo were paying a polite catch-up visit to Qiao Mengrong, everyone carefully steering clear of the pamphlet mess: Mengrong didn’t want to alienate Miao Zhiyu after ratting her out to Wenyuan, while both ladies had resolved to stay out of Mengrong’s vendetta and enjoy the drama safely from the sidelines.
When a servant announced Master Jiang’s wife at the gate, Qiao Mengrong snapped, “Tell her I’m busy!”—still fuming over Master Jiang’s botched ambush.
Bai Meiruo jumped in eager. “Know who Master Jiang’s wife is, Your Highness? Bet you’ll want to see her!” Explaining Chun Jinmo was Chunxiang Brothel’s former star who lost Young Master Chen to Youqi Jushi and ended up wed to homely Master Jiang, she drew Miao Zhiyu’s smirk: “Youqi Jushi even stepped on brothel turf!”
Intrigued at once, Qiao Mengrong sent Xilan to fetch Madam Jiang in—while Miao Zhiyu and Bai Meiruo smoothly excused themselves, trading quiet smirks as they passed the striking beauty outside.
“Pretty thing—and Chen Haoyu still ditched her for Youqi Jushi,” Miao Zhiyu murmured.
“My husband still pines for her without ever touching her!” Bai Meiruo griped as usual, before both hurried home to watch the fireworks from afar.
Led into the inner hall, Chun Jinmo trembled before a royal wife for the first time, dropping to her knees stammering. Swallowing distaste for her courtesan past, Qiao Mengrong honored her surname “Madam Jiang” and bade her sit.
“Master Jiang took my silver and botched the job,” Qiao Mengrong began coolly. “Here to apologize for him?”
“Don’t trust Master Jiang—drop him,” Chun Jinmo said bold. “I came for myself. I only married him because I had no choice; my heart’s always belonged to Young Master Chen until Youqi Jushi tore him away. I hate her just as much as Your Highness.”
“Coming to my door takes a gift. What did you bring?”
Chun Jinmo dropped one name: “Black-Fiend Tiger.”
“Who?”
“Bandit king of Mount Tianmu—kills without blinking, makes Master Jiang look like a lapdog!”
“And how do we get him?”
“Silver—and someone willing to climb Mount Tianmu in person. He’s old buddies with Master Jiang, came down for my wedding, and couldn’t keep his lecherous eyes off me—only held back because of Master Jiang. I’ll offer myself to him on Mount Tianmu to get him down here and kill Youqi Jushi.”
Half impressed, half sneering at a courtesan’s casual trade of her body, Qiao Mengrong asked, “What’s your price?”
“Once she’s dead, treat me as a friend and bring me into Lin’an’s noble ladies’ circle.”
Repulsed at the thought of introducing an ex-courtesan to high society—yet confident her royal rank would silence whispers once Zhu Shuzhen lay in a coffin—Qiao Mengrong agreed. “Get it done, and you have my word.”
A relieved smile lit Chun Jinmo’s face.
#CH 302
#TITLE: Chun Jinmo’s Gamble with Black-Fiend Tiger
Busy relocating the Qiantang Gang to the river docks, Master Jiang was thrilled when Chun Jinmo asked to stay a few days at her country aunt’s house until the new place settled—even sending men to escort her safely.
Once the guards rode back, Chun Jinmo lasted one token night before claiming rural mosquitoes drove her mad, hired a rickety country carriage with her personal maid, warned the girl to keep silent, and rolled straight to the foot of Mount Tianmu.
Paying off the driver, she hiked up into the hills until the timber stockade of “Black Wind Stronghold” loomed ahead and shouted at the gate guards, “Tell Black-Fiend Tiger that Master Jiang’s wife is here to see him!”
Invited inside at once—leaving her terrified maid cowering behind a boulder with orders never to breathe a word—Chun Jinmo marched into the main hall where burly, scar-faced Black-Fiend Tiger sat radiating menace.
Eyeing her with hungry lust, he grinned. “Where’s Brother Jiang? Why come alone?”
Turning on full courtesan charm, she purred, “My husband doesn’t know—I fell for Chief the day of my wedding and braved these hills just to see you.”
Delighted, he swept her into a bear hug. “Dreamed of you since that banquet—now you’re here, stay forever!”
Pushing back playful, she said, “Keep it from Master Jiang—and I’m not staying as your bandit queen.”
“Every woman I take stays on Mount Tianmu!”
“Make me the exception.”
Weighing war with Master Jiang against one afternoon’s fun, Black-Fiend Tiger laughed. “Pretty as you are, fine—your way.”
Melting back into his chest, she murmured, “Give you a wild afternoon—just do one favor for me.”
“Aha! Name it—through fire and water for a beauty like you!”
“Kill somebody for me.”
“Master Jiang handles hits—why hike up here?”
“Master Jiang’s a spineless coward—nothing next to you!”
Flattered sky-high, Black-Fiend Tiger scooped her up. “Deal! Tell me who in bed!”
Sent back down to her aunt’s house by late afternoon—spinning a yarn that Master Jiang had turned her back because the new manor wasn’t ready—Chun Jinmo slipped back to Youlin Retreat the very next morning.
Thrilled by the swift update, Qiao Mengrong bade her sit. “When does he move?”
“Day after tomorrow. Chen Manor’s loaded, and Black-Fiend Tiger’s been itching to loot it anyway—he’ll kill her during the raid.”
“She’s a fierce fighter—what if he can’t take her?”
“Relax—he’s using knockout smoke first, then slitting her throat in her sleep.”
“Pity the Chens get caught in the crossfire,” Qiao Mengrong sighed insincerely.
“Serves Young Master Chen right for marrying a jinx! One thing: Black-Fiend Tiger needs a floor plan of Chen Manor to find her courtyard fast. Drop it with the innkeeper at Wanyou Inn—he’ll pass it up the mountain.”
“I’ll get the layout. Lay low at your aunt’s till it’s done.”
Dismissing her, Qiao Mengrong sent Matron Zou straight to Chen Haodong at Yongtai Teahouse. Nervous over failing at Shao Escort Agency, Chen Haodong was relieved when Matron Zou waved off the escorts—until she asked for Chen Manor’s floor plan.
“Why a floor plan?!” he balked.
“Nobody else gets hurt—just need to find her room cleanly while she sleeps.”
“Swear it?”
“When has Princess Consort Yi ever lied?”
Half an hour later, Chen Haodong circled Moxiang Courtyard on a manor map, slipped it through Matron Zou’s parked carriage window outside Chen Manor without a word, and bolted—while Matron Zou delivered it straight to Wanyou Inn.
Meanwhile, delayed a day by family chores, Shao Zhengye arrived at the Judicial Office with his son carrying two jugs of fine wine. Welcoming them warmly into his courtyard, Shen Jianchen broke out big bowls and matched them cup for cup, savoring his first hearty bout of drinking since crossing over.
Once warmth loosened tongues, Shen Jianchen brought up an old dossier he’d noticed: the twenty-year-old case of Peng Jingxin. Father and son went dead silent.
“No need if it’s painful—case is twenty years cold,” Shen Jianchen offered.
“Asking as a friend, or as Judicial Investigator?” Shao Zhengye asked.
“So long as nobody’s crying injustice, strictly as a friend.”
“Zero injustice—just an old Shao family shame.”
“Then skip it—drink!”
Yet wine unlocked twenty years of buried grief. Twenty years back, Shao Zhengye’s seventeen-year-old unwed sister Shao Yueting turned up pregnant. Threatened with her unborn child’s life, she finally whispered the father’s name: Wanyan Wuqimai.
*Emperor Taizong of Jin—the very man behind the Jingkang Disaster!* Shen Jianchen realized with a jolt. *It’s the fifth year of Xuanhe; Wanyan Aguda dies this year, putting Wuqimai on the Jin throne!*
Posing as a Han gentleman touring West Lake in his youth, Wuqimai had seduced Shao Yueting at an inn and vanished overnight. When gossip claimed she’d been raped and clan elders demanded a culprit, cornered Yueting pointed at escort guard Peng Jingxin—who secretly loved her, swallowed the heartbreak, and took the false rape charge without a peep.
#CH 303
#TITLE: Praise for His First Bowl of Tea
Outraged clan elders had dragged Peng Jingxin to the Judicial Office, where Song law mandated hanging. Shao Zhengye put up the silver for Peng’s family to commute it to life imprisonment—visiting him yearly with fine wine and bribing the jailers so Peng lived comfortably behind bars.
“Then those Jin fighters who attacked your escort hall earlier...?” Shen Jianchen asked.
Trusting the prince as a true friend, Shao Zhengye held nothing back. When Shao Yueting bore a boy, refusing to keep a Jin bastard, Shao Zhengye snatched the newborn and rode nonstop to the Jin capital of Huining. Half hoping the rough road would kill the infant—yet buying goat’s milk when the tough little tyke survived—he left him in an open inn room on a grim wager: if anyone stole the baby, he’d assassinate Wanyan Wuqimai; if not, Wuqimai lived. Finding the infant resting peacefully after locating Wuqimai’s princely palace, Shao Zhengye crept over the palace wall at dusk, thrust the baby straight into patrolling guards’ arms yelling “Wanyan Wuqimai’s bastard—keep him off Han girls!”, cut down two pursuing guards, and sliced off one’s pinky finger.
Back in Lin’an, he lied to his sister that the baby died on the road and Wuqimai lay dead by his blade. Heartbroken, Shao Yueting shaved her head and entered a mountain nunnery.
Twenty years later, that baby—now grown into Wanyan Chi—came to Lin’an posing as a grieving son hunting his father’s killers, tracked down Shao Escort Agency, and came demanding his birth mother, sparking the brawl when Shao Zhengye refused to yield.
“A son seeking his mother is human nature,” Shen Jianchen remarked.
“With Song-Jin tensions razor-thin, the Shaos can’t touch Jin royalty! Besides, my sister’s lived in peaceful seclusion twenty years—I won’t let him shatter her peace! He swore he’d even trade his life for one look at her, yet I’ll never let him see her—never!”
Remembering Shao Zhengye’s earlier remark about similar martial styles, Shen Jianchen asked, “The Jin fighter using moves like mine—that was Wanyan Chi?”
“Yes.”
*So Shen Fist traces back six hundred years—maybe born in Jurchen lands!* Settling the Peng Jingxin mystery in his mind, Shen Jianchen drank both jugs dry with father and son trading martial tales.
Over at Moxiang Courtyard, Chen Haoyu graduated from his tea lessons at Yongtai Teahouse and marched straight to the tea table—rejecting the old utensils, rejecting the worn tea whisk, and ordering Kuqiao to dump all her tea powder!
“Haven’t even made a cup and already acting picky!” Kuqiao griped.
“Great tea starts in the details—proves you’ve been slapping sloppy tea together for my wife!”
“Hear that, Miss?! Years of careful whisking and he calls it sloppy!”
Putting away her huaben manuscript—no longer hiding it since Chen Haoyu respectfully kept clear of her desk—Qianli Xun smiled. “I know how good you are to me; ignore him.”
Pouting, Kuqiao dumped the powder into her own jar next door in Yuening Room, cleaned the caddy, and returned to see her miss smiling beside the tea table watching Young Master grind fresh powder—slipping quietly inside to leave them be.
“Never thought you’d have the patience for fine work like this,” Qianli Xun teased.
“For my wife, no work is too fine.”
A sudden rush of real warmth made her wonder if she was actually falling for him. Watching his crisp, graceful movements as he whisked water into creamy foam—smoother than Kuqiao’s even on his first try—she leaned closer in delight as he dipped the wooden spoon handle into thick tea paste to paint across the white froth.
“You really can do tea-foam painting!”
“Chabaixi—anything to make my wife smile.”
Slowly a willow took shape—the Divine Willow! “You’ve never been there with me—how’d you draw it?”
“Heard servants say you visit it often, so I rode out twice myself to memorize every branch.” Adding a slender female silhouette beneath the boughs, he looked up hopeful. “That’s you. Close?”
“Seven parts likeness—too pretty to drink! What do I do?”
“Drink it—that’s what I made it for!”
Taking a proper sip, Qianli Xun’s eyes widened. “Rich and mellow with zero bitterness, pure aroma—true mastery!”
Praise lit Chen Haoyu brighter than any praise at the silk shop. Stepping out, Kuqiao pretended to sulk—“All tea duty goes to Young Master from now on!”—before strolling out to chat with the other servants.
“Morning bowl and evening bowl, every day?” Chen Haoyu beamed.
“After tasting yours, I’m spoiled—deal!”
Walking after her toward the desk, thrilled he’d mastered a skill she couldn’t do without, he ventured, “Wife, I want to read your poetry.”
Spinning around in surprise, she studied his face. “Pinch me—am I dreaming?”
“Dead serious. They say poetry mirrors the heart—I want to understand what’s inside yours.”
Touched to the core, she picked a bound volume off the desk and handed it over. “Here—everything Zhu Shuzhen wrote.”
“Funny how you say that like you’re not Zhu Shuzhen.”
Caught half off guard, she added, “One more way to understand me—listen to Scholar Qianli Xun’s storybooks.”
“Love storytelling! Only how do his tales show me your heart?”
“I love his stories—means we think alike! Don’t ask why, just listen alongside the poems!”
“Whatever my wife says!”
Beaming back to Boya Study, Chen Haoyu opened the poetry collection—flipping right to a familiar verse and murmuring aloud: “Gulls and mandarin ducks swim in the same pond, everyone knows they don’t belong together. The Lord of the East has failed as master of flowers, why must we be intertwined branches all our lives?” [TN: Translated by Ronald Egan]
The poem she had recited in the carriage on their post-wedding visit—saying they walked different worlds. “Maybe we weren’t on the same road before,” he vowed softly, “yet from now on I’ll walk right onto yours.”
Qinggua wandered in assuming he was studying ledgers, teasing him for turning scholarly, griping when Haoyu complained about being neglected for shop clerking and Chouya, and lighting up when Haoyu ordered him into the accounting room alongside him tomorrow.
“Keep learning and maybe when Young Master runs the Chen house I’ll make steward!”
“Hush—only Brother Dong has the chops to run Chen Manor!”
“Dunno, Young Master—feels way safer in your hands,” Qinggua whispered before yawning him off to bed.
Deep into the dead of night, as crickets chirped over sleeping Chen Manor, seven or eight dark shadows vaulted the outer wall. Knowing the layout cold, they slipped long bamboo tubes through every window—even the servants’ quarters—blowing thick knockout smoke through every room until the entire household dropped senseless, then unbarred the front gate to let a horde of masked bandits pour inside.
#CH 304
#TITLE: Abducted to Mount Tianmu in the Dead of Night
Black-Fiend Tiger’s masked bandits swarmed through Chen Manor ransacking chests of silver and jewels while the drugged household snored like logs.
Guided straight to Moxiang Courtyard, Black-Fiend Tiger and his crew flicked every door latch open with knives, bagged every valuable, and stepped into the inner bedchamber. Drawing his heavy saber, he stepped up to the bed where Qianli Xun lay fast asleep under the smoke.
Just as his blade rose to strike, she rolled face-up. His arm froze midair. “What a stunner! Criminal to kill her—better haul her up Mount Tianmu as my bandit queen!”
Sheathing his blade, he rolled her tight inside her quilt, slung her over his shoulder, and marched out to waiting carriages and horses outside the gate. “Move out before the night patrol shows!”
By sheer chance, Lin Jue—who had been taking breaks from Lazybones Village—had volunteered to cover a subordinate’s night street patrol for old times’ sake. Riding near Chen Manor, he caught the rumble of heavy hooves and wheels, spotted the manor’s south gate hanging wide open, left two guards at the gate, and galloped in pursuit—cutting the convoy off near the west city gate.
“Halt! Drop the masks and open those carriages!”
“Cut ’em down!” Black-Fiend Tiger roared.
Steel clashed in the dark streets. Clashing blades with Black-Fiend Tiger, Lin Jue felt his wrist go numb against brute power—realizing he was outmatched, he yelled while parrying, “Who are you?! Just hit Chen Manor?!”
“Mount Tianmu!” Black-Fiend Tiger knocked Lin Jue clean out of the saddle as his bandits routed the patrol and thundered out the west gate.
“Black-Fiend Tiger!” Scrapping up into his saddle, Lin Jue dashed back to Chen Manor—seeing looted rooms yet everyone still breathing, assuming Zhu Shuzhen lay safe inside too—and galloped straight for Youlin Retreat to wake Prince Yi.
Roused by Butler Li announcing Lin Jue outside—and Qiao Mengrong snapping wide awake at the words “Chen Manor,” eager to hear Zhu Shuzhen lay dead—Shen Jianchen leaped out of bed the instant Lin Jue shouted Black-Fiend Tiger had raided Chen Manor and knocked everyone out.
Dressing in seconds, Shen Jianchen promised a deceptively sweet Mengrong—“Tell me first thing how Shuzhen is!”—and rode hard to Chen Manor with Lin Jue.
Fetching an apothecary who recognized knockout smoke and supplied counter-incense, Shen Jianchen had servants woken first to rouse the masters, sitting tense in the main hall beside silent Lin Jue—two men worrying over the same woman.
Soon Chen Jianglin, his brothers, Chen Haodong, and Chen Haofei hurried in to bow in thanks, relieved the bandits seemed after loot alone. Chen Haodong squirmed with quiet guilt over handing out the map, praying his sister-in-law lay dead so his sacrifice paid off.
“Where’s Second Young Master?” Shen Jianchen asked sharp.
Right then Chen Haoyu burst through the doors wild-eyed. “Father! My wife’s gone! Combed all of Moxiang Courtyard—her daytime clothes are still there, and her quilt’s missing!”
Before Chen Jianglin could speak, Shen Jianchen was already on his feet. “Commander Lin, I’m riding for Mount Tianmu now—fetch Chief Constable Wen and reinforcements right behind me!”
“Wait for me, Your Highness—I’m coming too!” Chen Haoyu sprinted straight after him. By first light they reached the foot of Mount Tianmu, left their horses, and pushed up the trail.
Up at Black Wind Stronghold, flush with enough silver to party a year and thrilled with his gorgeous trophy, Black-Fiend Tiger laid the quilt-wrapped beauty on his couch and waved revival incense under her nose—dismissing his henchman to enjoy her wide awake.
Waking to a rough voice calling “Beauty, wake up,” Qianli Xun blinked open to a dark, burly stranger in an unfamiliar room and shoved his face away in shock. *Not again—did I transmigrate twice?!* “Who am I?!”
“Asking me who you are?” he laughed. “I’m Black-Fiend Tiger—you’re up on Mount Tianmu! Snatched you straight out of Chen Manor. Be my bandit queen and I’ll treat you right!”
*Thank goodness—just kidnapped, not transmigrated!* Checking her modest sleepwear and her own quilt, she realized he’d knocked her out with smoke. “Black-whatever Tiger, never met you—why snatch me?”
“Black-Fiend Tiger! Someone paid me to kill you, only you’re too gorgeous to waste, so I brought you up here instead!”
Mengrong again—a cold ache brushed her heart before Black-Fiend Tiger reached for her and ate a solid fist right to the cheekbone, tumbling clean off the couch!
“Touch me and lose your teeth!”
Spitting blood and grinning wickedly, he scrambled up. “Got fire too—never broken a wildcat yet!”
Fists flew as Qianli Xun vaulted off the couch, weaving around his wild swings and battering him back bloodied until a swarm of bandits piled in. Fighting barefoot through the night till dawn, she dropped bandit after bandit—until one sneaky thug tossed a jagged flint under her descending foot.
Piercing agony shot through her sole, blood gushing; in that split second of distraction, thugs piled on and lashed her to a wooden chair.
Gritting through the throbbing cut, she snapped, “Let me go before the authorities skin you alive!”
Wiping blood off his lip, Black-Fiend Tiger laughed. “Already whipped the city patrol on our way out—the yamen’s a joke!”
“Who paid you to kill me?”
Keeping Chun Jinmo’s name locked tight, he sneered, “Who cares? Paid to kill you, gave you extra hours for your pretty face, and since you’re begging to die—hand me my saber!”
Catching a heavy blade tossed by a henchman, Black-Fiend Tiger stepped straight toward her.
#CH 305
#TITLE: Carrying His Wife Down the Mountain
Cold steel hovered above her; Qianli Xun shut her eyes, bracing for the end.
Even at the last split second, Black-Fiend Tiger hesitated a heartbeat—shame to waste such beauty.
Zip! A flying pebble cracked across his knuckles like lightning. His fingers spasmed, the saber clattering to the floor.
Qianli Xun’s eyes snapped open as Shen Jianchen blurred past everyone, decked Black-Fiend Tiger flat with a single punch, and planted himself straight in front of her. “Don’t be afraid. I’m here.”
No thanks needed between them—she let herself breathe behind his broad back.
Furious, Black-Fiend Tiger scrambled up yelling, “Kill him!” Bandits swarmed in with sabers, only for Shen Jianchen to weave through like wind, knocking blades spinning and driving men reeling back through the doorway before booting Black-Fiend Tiger clean out into the yard after ten bouts.
“Quit showing off and untie me first!” Qianli Xun called out.
Hurrying back to cut her ropes, Shen Jianchen spotted blood pooling under her sole. “Hurt bad?”
“Nothing.” Spotting a spare pair of women’s shoes in the room, she gritted past the sting and slipped them on, easing the cut off the grit. Shen Jianchen shrugged off his outer robe and handed it over; draping it round her shoulders, she grinned fierce. “Time to paint Mount Tianmu red!”
“Nearly got chopped up and still talking big?”
“Less talk, more fists!”
Stepping out into the yard, bandits ringed them tight. Bloodied yet stubborn, Black-Fiend Tiger bluffed, “Outnumbered—surrender and live!”
“My soldiers already surrounded your whole stronghold!” Shen Jianchen shot back cold. “Drop your blades or we raze this place flat!”
“Let your guys wait outside,” Qianli Xun murmured at his shoulder. “My fists are itching—let me fight my fill!”
“With you all the way.” Raising his voice, Shen Jianchen called out, “Boys outside—hold the perimeter till Youqi Jushi and I finish warming up!”
Half rattled, Black-Fiend Tiger rallied his mob. “Bluffing—hear how quiet it is? Swarm them in waves till they drop, then hack ’em to pieces!”
Standing back to back, Qianli Xun and Shen Jianchen tore through wave after wave bare-handed, dodging bright steel and sending bandits crashing groaning to the dirt—six full waves dropped before Shen Jianchen asked, “Tired?”
“Just getting warmed up!”
Peeking from outside the gate, Chen Haoyu couldn’t stomach watching Prince Yi and his wife fight in effortless sync another second. Kicking the timber gate open, he charged in yelling, “Wife! I’m here to save you!”
“What are you doing in here?! Get back!” Qianli Xun yelled—too late, as Black-Fiend Tiger snatched Chen Haoyu in a chokehold right beside him.
“Die right now, beauty, or I snap his neck!”
“Neither of us is dying today!”
