“Charge in—now!” Shen Jianchen barked.
On cue, Lin Jue and Wen Xin burst through the stockade with Lin’an’s soldiers. Distracted for half a second, Black-Fiend Tiger ate Qianli Xun’s flying fist, stumbled back, snatched a blade to slash at her—Chen Haoyu leaping straight in front shouting “Watch out, Wife!”—before Shen Jianchen tackled the bandit chief down and knocked him cold.
Exhausted from six rounds with the pair, the remaining bandits folded in minutes. Soldiers bound all three hundred fifty brigands, freed ten captive women, and tallied Chen Manor’s stolen loot as Wen Xin hauled Black-Fiend Tiger down to death row.
Passing Zhu Shuzhen on his way out, Lin Jue couldn’t resist a snide dig. “Quit stirring up trouble outside—nearly got killed and dragged Chen Manor down with you!”
Chen Haoyu jumped right in his face. “My wife—not your place to lecture! Without her, you’d never have climbed this hill!”
“Look at you groveling—where’d your old swagger go?”
Shoving Lin Jue toward the gate, Chen Haoyu barked, “Scram down the mountain!” Catching Qianli Xun’s sharp glare, Lin Jue scoffed and walked out.
Shen Jianchen asked softly, “Can you walk down?”
“Scratch like this? Easy.”
Chen Haoyu bowed polite yet firm. “Thank you for saving my wife, Your Highness. We’ll escort you down.”
Reminded he had no place left at her side, Shen Jianchen stepped out to the gate. “Lead on, then.”
Yet one step forward split the deep gash in her sole wide open, blood soaking through the shoe as she winced sideways. Chen Haoyu caught her at once. “Foot’s torn up—climb on my back, I’ll carry you down!”
Glancing back in worry, Shen Jianchen saw Qianli Xun already resting atop Chen Haoyu’s back—the intimate sight piercing him with quiet envy. Looking away as he led the trail down, he asked, “Won’t carrying her down a mountain wear you out, Young Master Chen?”
“Carrying my own wife? Light as a feather!”
“Wait up here—I’ll send soldiers up with a stretcher.”
“No need! No need!”
Walking ahead without looking back, Shen Jianchen still caught every warm murmur behind him—“Put me down if you’re tired.” “Never—need to get that bleeding foot doctored fast!”—until Qianli Xun looped her arms snug around Chen Haoyu’s neck and drifted straight off to sleep in pure relief.
Halfway down, Chen Haoyu’s boot slipped on loose gravel; Shen Jianchen whipped back and caught him steady before either fell. “Watch your footing—trail’s slick.” Catching sight of Qianli Xun’s peaceful sleeping face, Shen Jianchen lingered a fraction too long until Chen Haoyu coughed pointedly—“Folks below are waiting, Your Highness”—and Shen Jianchen turned red-faced back down the trail, ears tuned to every step behind him.
At the trailhead under a high sun, Lin Jue waited with horses and a carriage. “I’ll ride,” Shen Jianchen said, mounting up. “Give them the carriage.”
Walking over, Lin Jue jeered as Chen Haoyu murmured gently trying not to wake his wife, “Call yourself a man? Watch a pro!” Leaning right by her ear, Lin Jue roared, “Zhu Shuzhen! Wake up or I start swinging!”
Half asleep, reflex fired—Qianli Xun swung a groggy fist muttering, “Dare hit me? I’ll beat you flat!” Lin Jue blocked her half-strength swing grinning, and she blinked awake, sliding down on her good foot before Chen Haoyu scooped her clean into his arms and carried her aboard while Lin Jue sneered “Pathetic!” and Shen Jianchen told himself silently, *Long as she’s happy.*
Inside the rolling carriage, Chen Haoyu rested her injured foot on his knee, slipped the shoe off, bound the cut tenderly with a clean handkerchief, and eased the shoe back on.
Pulling her foot back shyly, she asked, “Everyone home safe?”
“Prince Yi got there in time—nobody hurt, only looted. Black-Fiend Tiger came for silver and snatched you for your looks.”
Spared her family knowing Mengrong’s hand in it, Qianli Xun sighed. “Guess being pretty isn’t always a blessing.”
“Best blessing there is!”
“Typical lecher—only sweet on me while I’m young and pretty! Wait till I’m old and wrinkled, you’ll ditch me.”
“When you’re old and wrinkled, I’ll be old and wrinkled right beside you—perfect match!”
“I’m older—I’ll wrinkle first.”
“Three tiny years!”
Deep down, Qianli Xun realized a brand-new truth: she was actually starting to worry about Chen Haoyu ever looking at someone else.
#CH 306
#TITLE: Ye Anya’s Cryptic Words
Back at Chen Manor, right after Chen Haoyu doctored his wife’s sole, Old Madam Xia and Yao Qingyan led the women of the house over to Moxiang Courtyard.
Insisting on rising despite her cut foot, Qianli Xun was gently guided back onto the bed by Yao Qingyan while Chen Haoyu lifted her legs up and Kuqiao arranged chairs.
“Foot still hurting?” Old Madam Xia asked with concern.
“Much better with salve on.”
Turning to her grandson, Old Madam Xia ordered, “Skip the silk shop a few days and stay right here tending Shuzhen—I’ll square it with your father.”
“Gladly, Grandmother.”
Old Madam Xia dabbed away tears with a silk handkerchief. “Thank goodness not a single soul in our house was lost—and Prince Yi wiping out Mount Tianmu counts as good karma for the Chens! Father-in-law already decided to donate every copper of the recovered loot for blessings and peace. Silver comes and goes; people are everything.”
Gloom hung over the room, sharper in Qianli Xun’s own chest—knowing she had drawn Mengrong’s blade straight onto Chen Manor.
Breaking the heaviness, Chen Haoyu piped up, “Coming through untouched means good fortune’s waiting—why mope?”
Madam Bao groused, “Untouched?! They swiped my favorite jade bangle and half my jewels!”
Yao Qingyan smoothed it over—“Second Brother will buy you better ones”—before Old Madam Xia clapped her hands briskly. “We came to cheer Shuzhen, not pull long faces! Smiles, everybody!”
Then Madam Cui noticed the garment draped over Shuzhen’s shoulders. “Why are you wearing a man’s outer robe, Shuzhen?”
Still wearing Prince Yi’s robe from the scramble home! Qianli Xun didn’t miss a beat. “Got snatched without a coat, so Chen Haoyu gave me his—taking it off now.”
As Kuqiao slipped it off and eyebrows rose seeing Haoyu already wearing an outer coat, Chen Haoyu backed her smooth: “Swapped into a fresh one right when we got back!”
Satisfied, everyone urged her to rest and filed out—all except Ye Anya.
“Mind giving Shuzhen and me a private minute, Second Brother?”
“Sure—I’ll go whisk tea outside.”
Once Chen Haoyu stepped out, Ye Anya sat on the bed’s edge and clasped Shuzhen’s hand. “I envy you so much.”
“Lying here bandaged and you envy me?”
“Haoyu’s heart belongs to you alone—who wouldn’t envy that?”
Remembering Chen Haodong’s sleazy tryst, Qianli Xun felt an ache for her without knowing what to say.
“Know about the big Chen family event coming up?” Ye Anya asked.
Shaking her head—“What event?”
“The business heir selection—moved up from next year to less than a month away.”
“Big deal for the family, nothing to me.”
“Don’t you want Haoyu chosen?”
“Never crossed my mind—besides, he’s not cut out for it. Everyone expects Eldest Cousin.”
“If you ask me, Chen Haodong doesn’t deserve it.”
Qianli Xun blinked. “You don’t want your own husband to inherit?”
After a beat of silence, Ye Anya’s eyes turned steel. “All I want is out—only getting free of him is nearly impossible. Now I’ve got a plan that keeps him out of the inheritance and forces him to set me free.”
A chill prickled Qianli Xun’s spine; she gripped Ye Anya’s hand tight. “Don’t do anything reckless, Eldest Sister-in-law!”
“Relax—nothing reckless. Something good for me, good for you, good for Haoyu, and good for the whole Chen family.” Rising with a calm, radiant smile that wiped away her usual shadow, Ye Anya glided out with Xiaocui.
Having overheard every word, Kuqiao whispered worriedly while fluffing her miss’s pillows, “What did Eldest Young Madam mean?”
“Forget you ever heard it—breathe not a word to a soul.”
Just as Qianli Xun shut her eyes, Matron Wei stepped in, sitting by the bed to fret over how frantic Young Master and the whole courtyard had been at dawn—right as Chen Haoyu strolled in with two cups of tea painted with distant mountain peaks!
Savoring her cup, Matron Wei marveled at his skill while Kuqiao pouted begging him to teach her foam-painting and Chen Haoyu teased she lacked the finesse—until both empty cups were collected and Haoyu headed back to Boya Study for a nap.
“Look what a gem Young Master is,” Matron Wei praised. “Even maids dare sass him—proves he has zero arrogance!”
“Think he’s good enough to inherit the Chen businesses too?” Qianli Xun tested.
Matron Wei caught the slip in a second. “‘Too’? Who else floated that?”
Brushing it off awkward, Qianli Xun listened as Matron Wei weighed it realistic: “If he’d straightened out years ago and mastered trade, sure—now? Odds are slim unless some earth-shattering twist flips the board, and don’t hold your breath for miracles.”
*An earth-shattering twist—could fragile Eldest Sister-in-law pull that off?*
As Matron Wei rose to leave, Qianli Xun caught her hand. “Stay a minute, Matron—something’s choking me and I need advice.” Taking a deep breath, she spilled the truth: “Princess Consort Yi wants me dead. She hired Black-Fiend Tiger—I got lucky today, yet she’ll come again. I don’t fear dying, only dragging Chen Manor down with me! Should I leave Chen Manor?”
Shocked yet steady, Matron Wei frowned. “Chen Manor is your home now—no leaving! You did nothing wrong; Princess Consort Yi’s narrow heart is to blame. Trust me—I’ll go to Youlin Retreat today and talk to her.”
“Doubt it’ll do any good, Matron...”
“Worth a shot—and drop any thought of leaving! Tell me what message to carry.”
“Tell her to aim straight at me and leave the Chen family out of it.”
Over at Youlin Retreat, Qiao Mengrong had lain awake since midnight expecting news of Zhu Shuzhen’s corpse—only to hear at noon via Prince Yi’s messenger that Mount Tianmu had been wiped out, Black-Fiend Tiger sat in irons, and Zhu Shuzhen walked away alive!
Seething in disbelief, she heard a servant announce Matron Wei at the gate—and ordered Xilan to fetch Matron Zou to handle her instead.
#CH 307
#TITLE: Matron Wei’s Fruitless Plea
Guided into Princess Consort Yi’s courtyard, Matron Wei found no royal waiting—only a matron near her own age seated coolly below the head chair.
“Youqi Jushi’s matron?” Matron Zou didn’t rise. “Her Highness doesn’t see just anyone. State your business to me.”
Learning from a maid this was Prince Yi’s wet nurse, Matron Wei curtsied properly, took a seat, and cut straight to the bone. “Two things. First, tell Princess Consort Yi my miss returned safe from Mount Tianmu—cut foot, nothing serious.”
Seeing Matron Zou’s eyes dart toward the decorative screen confirmed Her Highness sat right behind it. Matron Wei pressed on bold: “Surviving a mess like that means King Yama isn’t ready for her—why waste effort on futile plots?”
“Don’t know what you’re babbling about,” Matron Zou feigned. “What’s her luck got to do with Her Highness?”
“Drop the act, Matron Zou—you wouldn’t be receiving me if you didn’t know everything. My miss only told me today Her Highness wants her dead, and out of love for her I came here to plead.”
Dropping the pretense, Matron Zou said, “Point one noted. What’s point two?”
“I’ve watched my miss every single day: she never crossed a line with Prince Yi, and since marrying she’s focused solely on her husband. Prince Yi isn’t fickle either—now wed, his heart belongs to Her Highness. Holding onto the sting of that wedding raid and targeting my miss only traps everyone in the past. Let bygones go and look forward!”
Matron Zou agreed inside, yet spoke up to test the screen behind her. “Easy for you to say—Prince Yi still drops everything whenever her name comes up! Look at today!”
“He’s Military Commissioner and Judicial Investigator—he’d ride out for any family in Lin’an! If anyone keeps handing Prince Yi chances to play hero for my miss, blame the ones cooking up attacks, not my miss!”
Behind the screen, a cold jolt hit Qiao Mengrong: the pamphlets, Chen Haodong’s stunts, Black-Fiend Tiger—every single trap she sprung had only handed Brother another excuse to rush to Zhu Shuzhen’s side! How stupid had she been—yet letting her live still stuck in her craw.
Matron Zou tried one last jab. “Gossip calls her a born flirt whose eyes steal men’s souls—I’d be mad too.”
“Street slander! Meet her and you’d know better.”
“I have met her—still can’t tell.”
“Because you never lived beside her.”
Unable to hold back, Qiao Mengrong stepped clean out from behind the screen. “I lived beside her—best friends once—and still only saw her true face too late!”
Matron Wei dropped to her knees to bow, rising at Her Highness’s cold wave. “Parts of what you said make sense, and I’ll keep them in mind,” Qiao Mengrong said icy-flat. “Still, don’t dream I’m letting Zhu Shuzhen go. Tell her luck runs out eventually.”
Both matrons’ hearts sank. Having said her piece, Matron Wei delivered her miss’s final word: “Target her alone—spare innocent people.”
“I’d love to spare others, yet she married into Chen Manor—collateral damage happens. Tell her I’ll try.”
Chilled by how unrecognizable kind Princess Jiahe had become, Matron Wei decided to tell a white lie back at Chen Manor rather than watch her miss abandon a thriving marriage out of fear.
Finding Chen Haoyu chatting warmly beside Shuzhen’s bed back in Moxiang Courtyard—and watching him politely excuse himself over “Zhu family business”—Matron Wei sat close and reassured her miss: “Better than expected! Her Highness swore she’ll live her life and let you live yours so long as you stay clear of Prince Yi—just avoid salons and banquets where they show up.”
Qianli Xun’s doubts melted away in relief, right before Chen Wenxin arrived to check on her for the frantic Zhu household.
Meanwhile, Zhu Xiyan marched straight to the Judicial Office and kowtowed three resonant knocks before Prince Yi—forgetting all his past irritation over losing graft from Lazybones Village. “Your Highness saved my daughter and my house—from this day on, I and every official in Lin’an serve at your command!”
“Even if I cut off your side income?” Shen Jianchen teased.
“Silver’s dust—doing real good for the people under Your Highness matters most!”
With Lin’an’s whole officialdom squarely in his pocket, Shen Jianchen set to work with full force.
Over the next twenty days—as Moxiang Courtyard’s lotuses bloomed and silk-tree buds swelled—the Judicial Office stacked five capital charges on Black-Fiend Tiger: arson, murder, robbery, pillage, and rape. Shen Jianchen sentenced him to autumn beheading, sorting his three hundred followers by guilt: death row for the worst, life behind bars for mid-tier, and parole for minor hands split between Lin’an’s garrison and Master Jiang’s river patrols.
Out of old brotherhood, Master Jiang got Prince Yi’s permission to bring wine and four dishes into death row for a farewell drink. Dragging heavy shackles to the wooden table, Black-Fiend Tiger had zero appetite—burning with envy at how Master Jiang rose to Ferry Superintendent while he waited for the axe.
Pouring drinks, Master Jiang said, “One last toast, Brother Black—be an honest ghost in the next life.”
Bitterness spilled out as Black-Fiend Tiger reminisced: ten years back they swore brotherhood, got rejected from Bianliang’s army for their looks, enlisted in Lin’an only to brawl their way out of a corrupt camp—then climbed fast on the dark side where honest paths stayed blocked.
“Still remember my real name?”
“Li Hu—nickname Little Tiger.”
“You killed men too—how’d you end up a shiny official visiting me on death row?”
“Good deeds balanced the bad, never crossed the yamen, and whenever folks paid for hits, I checked first—only whacked scum who deserved heaven’s wrath!”
“If someone paid you to kill me, would I make your list?”
Master Jiang blinked, then answered honest: “By my code, yes—yet we’re sworn brothers, so no silver buys your head.”
Touched, Black-Fiend Tiger clinked bowls and drained his cup.
Master Jiang sighed. “Robbing the Chens was one thing—why snatch Chen Haoyu’s wife?! Prefect’s daughter, Jiangnan’s poetess, and Prince Yi would die for her—she’s the very one who got me my official post! One quick word to me first and your whole stronghold would still be standing!”
#CH 308
#TITLE: After Scarface’s Release
Moved by his brother’s loyalty—and unwilling to leave him blind as a dying man—Black-Fiend Tiger asked, “Happy with your wife, Brother Jiang?”
“Course! First woman to steal my heart—pried her off Chen Haoyu, planning on kids and building a respectable name for her.”
“Something I can’t keep from you: your wife climbed Mount Tianmu herself and begged me to kill the poetess. That’s the only reason I hit Chen Manor.”
Fury flashed in Master Jiang’s eyes. Grabbing Black-Fiend Tiger’s collar, he snarled, “Did you touch her?!”
Black-Fiend Tiger gave one quiet nod.
Crack! Master Jiang floored him with a thunderous punch, stopping at the cell door as jailers stepped up. “Treated you like a brother and you stab me like this—our brotherhood ends today!”
As the iron padlock slammed shut, Black-Fiend Tiger climbed back to the table muttering, “Only told you *because* you’re my brother.”
Over at the Ferry Superintendent’s new riverfront manor, Chun Jinmo sat daydreaming by the window about Youqi Jushi’s death—ignoring Master Jiang’s storm-cloud face when he strode inside.
Sitting across from her, voice shaking, he asked, “Done anything behind my back lately, Wife?”
“What’re you talking about?” she asked flatly without looking away from the window.
“Get out and shut the door!” he roared at the maid, then slammed the shutters tight and cracked his palm straight across Chun Jinmo’s face!
Clutching her stinging cheek, eyes red, she shrieked, “Why hit me?!”
“I gave you my whole heart and you sneak out to sleep with another man?!”
“Baseless slander—prove it!”
“Black-Fiend Tiger told me himself you climbed Mount Tianmu to hire him against Chen Haoyu’s wife!”
“Lies! Never set foot on Mount Tianmu!”
Snatching her collar, he roared, “He’s headed for the chopping block—dying men don’t lie!” Shoving her to the floor, he glared down.
“Chopping block?!” Confined indoors since Qiao Mengrong’s warning, she knew nothing of the raid’s collapse.
“Because of you! Botched the hit, his whole stronghold got wiped out, and he’s rotting on death row!”
“Botched?! Youqi Jushi’s still alive?!”
Her blurted shock proved every word. Heart shattered, Master Jiang dropped into a crouch clutching his head and wept aloud.
Climbing up rubbing her cheek, Chun Jinmo sneered cold, “My heart belongs to Young Master Chen—insisting on marrying me brought this curse on yourself!”
Rising with tear-streaked cheeks, Master Jiang pointed a trembling finger. “Don’t step half an inch outside this manor! Pull one more filthy stunt and I’ll kill you myself!”
Rattled yet defiant, she snapped, “Go yell at Princess Consort Yi—she’s the one who wanted her dead! Promised to bring me into noble society!”
Crack! A second slap spun her head sideways. “Breathe one more word like that and I butcher you right now! Know what happens if Prince Yi hears you sicced bandits on the woman in his heart?! Your neck goes, and my new post goes up in smoke!”
Sinking to the floorboards weeping—not over her furious husband, only over losing her last shot at Young Master Chen—Chun Jinmo heard Master Jiang lay down his ultimatum: “One month to think! Decide to be my wife and I forgive everything; refuse, and I set you loose—live or die, none of my business!”
Stepping out, he had men padlock her door and nail wooden boards across every window.
Meanwhile, Scarface’s thirty-day sentence ended. Blinking in bright sunlight after a month in the dark, he marched straight toward Yongtai Teahouse by West Lake to collect his hundred taels.
Upstairs in his private booth, Chen Haodong sipped tea riding high—heir selection hit tomorrow, and Princess Consort Yi still backed him since his map had worked fine!
When a waiter announced Scarface, Chen Haodong wasn’t surprised—high time to clear this debt before tomorrow’s triumph.
Shutting the booth door, Scarface sat right down. “Kept my mouth shut and did my time—about that silver?”
“Keep your voice down,” Chen Haodong hissed, sliding a crisp bank note across the table. “Added fifty extra—one hundred fifty taels total. Take it and clear out fast.”
Eyes shining at the one-hundred-fifty-tael note, Scarface tucked it inside his shirt—“Worth every lash and every day in jail!”—and hurried out.
Just clearing West Lake, Scarface ran dead into Chief Constable Wen.
“Whoa—served my full month clean, Chief Constable! Why stop me?”
“Curious how much silver you just collected.”
Bolting backward, Scarface bumped into two constables sealing his retreat. “No idea what you mean—penniless straight out of jail!”
“Search him!”
Seconds later the crisp one-hundred-fifty-tael bank note landed in Wen Xin’s palm. “Heh—one hundred fifty taels! Suspect you robbed somebody fresh out of lockup. Back to the Judicial Office!”
Realizing his jackpot was toast and refusing to eat a fresh prison stretch for nothing, Scarface folded on the walk over. Dropping to his knees before Prince Yi, he wailed out the entire truth from night one to the payoff.
“Willing to testify if I arrest Chen Haodong?” Shen Jianchen asked.
“Not built for big money and done taking falls—spare me and I’ll testify to every word!”
Ordering constables to lodge Scarface comfortably inside the compound under strict house arrest, Shen Jianchen fielded Wen Xin’s puzzled question: “Why wait instead of grabbing Chen Haodong right now at Yongtai Teahouse?”
“Tomorrow’s the Chen family heir selection—Prefect Zhu and officials are all attending,” Shen Jianchen explained. “Dragging Chen Haodong away today slaps the whole Chen clan across the face in front of Lin’an. Let their big ceremony finish, then scoop him up quietly.”
“What if they pick Chen Haodong tomorrow—won’t they waste the whole vote?”
“Who they pick is Chen business; arresting him two days later is ours.”
#CH 309
#TITLE: Selecting the Family Heir
The big day arrived—nobody beaming brighter than Chen Haodong and his parents.
Xinglong Pavilion’s grand reception hall stood freshly arrayed: two seats of honor up front—Prefect Zhu Xiyan on the left, Old Madam Xia on the right. Below them on the left sat invited officials in the front row with Granduncle Chen Lu and a dozen clan elders behind; on the right sat the three Chen brothers and three candidates in front, wives and womenfolk behind. On a center table sat three wooden ballot boxes labeled Chen Haodong, Chen Haoyu, and Chen Haofei.
Seated among the wives, Qianli Xun felt an uneasy flutter—not over whether Haoyu won, only wondering what wild bomb Ye Anya planned to drop in this solemn hall.
Chen Jianglin rose to greet the Prefect, officials, Old Madam Xia, and elders, introducing Second Brother Chen Jiangshu to read the clan’s strict qualifications—when Matron Zou’s voice echoed from outside: “Princess Consort Yi arrives!”
Delight flashed across Chen Haodong’s face—she kept her word! Everyone stood baffled why royalty graced a merchant vote, Qianli Xun’s stomach tightening as Qiao Mengrong swept in flanked by Matron Zou and Xilan, shooting a fleeting sneer straight at Zhu Shuzhen.
“At ease,” Qiao Mengrong declared. “Came uninvited simply to back one standout Chen junior whose virtue and skill won wide praise—making sure he doesn’t get overlooked.”
Only Zhu Xiyan dared ask, “Which junior does Your Highness honor?”
“Eldest Young Master Chen Haodong!”
Satisfied murmurs rippled through the elders—locking in even the few fence-sitters—while Chen Haodong’s parents beamed triumphant. *What backroom deal ties Chen Haodong to Mengrong?* Qianli Xun wondered.
Old Madam Xia yielded her head chair to Her Highness, Chen Haoyu smoothly gave up his front-row seat to sit beside his wife—drawing an approving glance from Chen Jianglin as Old Madam Xia took the vacated front chair.
“Sharp move giving up your seat for Grandmother,” Qianli Xun whispered.
“Outcome’s already locked—why warm a front chair?” he whispered back, earning her smile.
Unrolling a scroll, Chen Jiangshu read the six pillars aloud: filial virtue; diligence and courage; mastery of trade; a wife of spotless family; a virtuous wife of fair repute; and clan unity.
Two whole rules about wives! Rule five alone torpedoed Chen Haoyu—karma for his own old gossip! Qianli Xun shot him a playful glare, softening at once seeing his carefree grin.
Next came the three candidates’ speeches. Chen Haodong boasted of training since age eight, running deals by twelve, and using royal backing to conquer Bianliang’s market—drawing warm applause. Chen Haoyu kept it breezy: “Still a rookie—if I had a vote I’d cast it for Brother Dong! Wherever he points, I’ll follow.” (Making Chen Haodong sneer inside while Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan nodded in quiet pride.) Chen Haofei kept it shorter still, focused purely on his books yet pledging full brotherhood.
Voting opened. Led by Chen Lu, every single clan elder dropped their ballots straight into Chen Haodong’s box. After brief thought, Old Madam Xia, Chen Jianglin, and Chen Jiangmu followed suit; certain of victory, Chen Jiangshu played modest and dropped his lone token ballot into Chen Haoyu’s box.
Two officials tallied the count: “Twenty ballots cast—Chen Haodong nineteen, Chen Haoyu one, Chen Haofei zero!”
Glancing at Zhu Shuzhen and seeing zero disappointment soured Qiao Mengrong’s glee.
Zhu Xiyan rose to seal it. “Clearly Eldest Young Master Chen Haodong is chosen—any objections?”
Heart pounding in the back row, Ye Anya seized her one opening and stood straight up. “I object!”
Shock froze the hall. Madam Bao hissed, “Sit down at once!” while Chen Haodong boiled inside swearing to skin her alive afterward. Qianli Xun held her breath—*here comes the twist!*
Steeling her voice, Ye Anya declared, “I object to Chen Haodong inheriting because I—his wife—am of corrupt conduct, unfit for ‘virtuous repute,’ and about to be notorious across Lin’an!”
Horrified, Old Madam Xia cried, “Anya, stop slandering yourself!”
“Not slander, Grandmother—truth! I’m pregnant! Call a physician right now to check my pulse!”
Madam Bao brightened. “Pregnant?! Wonderful—Haodong has an heir, double joy today!”
Sweat broke across Chen Haodong’s brow.
Pulling a folded sheet from her sleeve, Ye Anya held it high. “Don’t celebrate too fast, Mother-in-law! This child isn’t Chen Haodong’s—he’s sterile and can’t father children, and this medical prescription proves it!”
Chen Haodong sprang up roaring, “Waving some fake paper raving like a lunatic! Get back to your room!”
“Drag her out!” Chen Jiangshu barked.
Two male servants seized her arms, yet she fought and shouted across the hall: “Chen Haodong sleeps with brothel courtesans, can’t sire an heir, and plots against Haoyu nonstop out of jealousy—pick him and Chen Manor will live to regret it!”
Qianli Xun shot a desperate look at Old Madam Xia, who thundered right on cue: “Halt!”
Breaking free, Ye Anya knelt before Old Madam Xia and handed up the prescription. “His name is right on it, Grandmother—call the physician who wrote it and verify every word!”
Seeing her eldest grandson’s name plain on the paper, Old Madam Xia tucked it away and bowed with poised grace to the guests. “An unforeseen household crisis—forgive us for troubling Your Highness and Lord Prefect. Once sorted, Jianglin will thank you in person.”
Disappointed she hadn’t even rattled Zhu Shuzhen, Qiao Mengrong swept straight out without a word; Zhu Xiyan—secretly tickled his son-in-law might get another shot—withdrew politely with his officials.
Clan elders stayed seated as Old Madam Xia reclaimed the chair. “Sit, Anya. Everyone sit.”
Chen Haoyu leaned close to whisper, “Did Eldest Sister-in-law lose her mind?”
“People only snap when backed off a cliff,” Qianli Xun murmured back.
“Don’t believe her lies, Grandmother!” Chen Haodong pleaded.
“Sit down, Haodong,” Old Madam Xia commanded. “Clean this up in the open, or whispers will dog you forever.”
Gritting his teeth, Chen Haodong made up his mind: write a divorce letter right here on the spot and cut her loose before she wrecked his crown.
#CH 310
#TITLE: One Wave Down, Another Rises
Ye Anya’s bombshell—most of all claiming Haodong plotted against Haoyu—made Chen Jianglin remember Haoyu’s jail stay and wonder if moving the selection up had been a blunder. Yao Qingyan stayed silent beside him, shocked that gentle Anya had been beaten past her breaking point.
Old Madam Xia spoke with stern pity. “Speak straight, Anya—what are you after today?”
“Two things, Grandmother: first, the clan’s blessing to leave Chen Haodong; second, keeping him out of the inheritance.”
Old Madam Xia rounded on her eldest grandson. “How many times did I warn you to treat your wife right?! Swinging fists day in and day out—see what it bought you?!”
Unrepentant, Chen Haodong snapped, “She cheated on me and treated me like ice—beating her was mercy! Now she wrecks our clan ceremony! Wants out? Fine—I’ll write a divorce letter right here so she’s no longer my wife and can’t touch today’s vote!”
Seeing him dump his wife to protect his crown without an ounce of remorse, Old Madam Xia sighed heavy. “Publicly admitting infidelity—she’s forcing your hand. Go ahead, let her go; staying here only makes her miserable, hard as life is for a divorced woman. I’m washing my hands of you two.”
Chen Jiangshu hurried brush and paper to the center table. Striding over, Chen Haodong dashed off a divorce letter, scanned it, and flung it onto the floor at her feet. “Your divorce letter—want it, pick it up!”
Heart soaring at freedom despite his sneer, Ye Anya walked calmly up, scooped it off the tiles, checked every line, folded it neat, and tucked it into her waist pouch.
“No longer a Chen—get out,” Chen Haodong snapped.
Ignoring him, she faced Old Madam Xia. “Goal two isn’t done yet—”
“She’s divorced—no right to speak! Throw this shrew out!” Madam Bao and Chen Jiangshu shouted together until Old Madam Xia roared them silent.
“Go, Anya,” Old Madam Xia said firm. “Chen business was never your place, even less now.”
Unwilling to leave quiet, Ye Anya cried out one parting blast: “Fine, I’ll go—yet hear my warning! Chen Haodong is a hypocrite unfit to lead! Not only does he sleep with Chunxiang Brothel courtesans, I bet he was behind that courtesan storming Chen Manor too!”
Crack! Chen Haodong lashed across her face. “Whore carrying another man’s bastard—dare smear me?! Move before I kill you!”
Clutching her cheek, Ye Anya laughed bitter. “See that, everyone?! Dressed like a gentleman, pure trash inside—only good for beating his wife! You couldn’t get me pregnant while Mother-in-law blamed me—now my belly proves I’m fine!”
Stomping frantic, Chen Jiangshu shouted her out; spitting straight at Chen Haodong, Ye Anya walked free with Xiaocui.
A chill ran down Old Madam Xia at her parting words—putting together the courtesan trial and Eldest Son suddenly rushing the heir vote the very day Haoyu got freed! Yet if not Haodong, who else? Neither Haoyu nor Haofei fit—better wrap this up fast before more chaos hit.
Just as she opened her mouth, Chen Lu coughed sharp. “The clan elders just conferred—whether an heir can continue the bloodline affects the whole Chen clan’s future! We must pause and verify.”
“Don’t swallow that woman’s lies!” Chen Haodong protested.
“I know medicine,” another elder volunteered. “Hand over the prescription and let me take Haodong’s pulse right here.”
Cornered, Chen Haodong chose partial confession over humiliation. “Fine—my health has a minor issue, and a physician did write that prescription, only he promised one more year of medicine cures it completely! Besides, fertility isn’t even one of the six written rules!”
Airtight—stumping the elder. “True,” Chen Lu conceded, “yet rules evolve—let’s add continuing the bloodline starting this year!”
Breaking silence at last, Chen Jianglin stepped in. “I’ll add that clause afterward; today’s vote stands under existing rules. Anya is divorced and out of the picture, and I’ll arrange a proper new bride for Haodong myself. With the result clear, I’ll draft the appointment scroll right now for all twenty voting elders to stamp their thumbprints.”
Relief flooded Chen Haodong and his parents as Chen Jianglin spread a special scroll and began writing.
Watching quietly, Qianli Xun admired Ye Anya’s fierce nerve—even if she couldn’t topple the vote, walking away free regardless of scandal took guts she herself lacked. *Which way is right—compromising like me, or burning every bridge like her?*
Chen Haoyu leaned in. “You’re the best, Wife.”
“Where’d that come from?”
“For not putting a green hat on my head.”
She burst out laughing. “Funny—feels like I’m the one wearing a green hat!”
Pantomiming lifting a hat off her head, he grinned. “Took it off for good—never letting you wear one again.”
Smiling silent, she realized neither path was right or wrong—so long as you found peace. She felt happy right now, and Ye Anya would be too.
Finishing the scroll, Chen Jianglin pressed the first red thumbprint, followed eagerly by Chen Jiangshu, Chen Jiangmu, Old Madam Xia, and a line of elders. Seventeen prints stamped—only three elders left! Chen Haodong’s heart nearly burst with triumph—
When Han Gu’s voice boomed from the doorway: “The Judicial Investigator arrives!”
The final three elders froze mid-reach. *What’s Prince Yi doing here?!*
After leaving Chen Manor, Qiao Mengrong had stopped by the Judicial Office to chat with her brother and gleefully dished the whole explosive Chen scandal—prompting Shen Jianchen to realize zero Chen face remained to protect and march straight over before they handed their clan to a rapist! Relieved his arrest only touched Chun Jinlan and not Black-Fiend Tiger, Qiao Mengrong had ridden back to Youlin Retreat sighing over losing her errand boy, eyeing high-powered Imperial Attendant Liang Shicheng in Bianliang as a dream weapon—and suddenly realizing Bai Feng had vanished for weeks, sparking fresh dark paranoia about Zhu Shuzhen.
Now Shen Jianchen strode into Xinglong Pavilion flanked by Han Gu, Wen Xin, and armed constables.
Scrambling up to greet him, Chen Jianglin bowed. “Welcome, Your Highness!”
“Heir chosen yet?” Shen Jianchen asked coolly.
“Just three thumbprints away from finalized.”
“Looks like I made it just in time.” Walking straight up to Chen Haodong, Shen Jianchen said clear, “Eldest Young Master Chen, please accompany me to the Judicial Office.”
Stunned murmurs swept the hall as cold sweat drenched Chen Haodong—*Scarface ratted me out!* “Why summon me, Your Highness?”
“Since you ask, I’ll announce it before everyone.” Sweeping the room—catching Qianli Xun’s puzzled look and shifting his eyes right away—Shen Jianchen proclaimed loud and clear: “Remember Chun Jinlan? On the night of the third day of the third month last year, maid Aye saw a man shrouded head to face in a black cloak walk out of that room. That man was *you*—Chen Haodong, you are the rapist who violated Chun Jinlan!”

## 译法建议
柳岸别馆 / 墨香苑 / 兴隆阁 / 永泰茶楼 / 聚香楼 / 花云楼 / 幽林别居 / 素心草堂 / 悦宁屋 / 博雅斋 | Liu’an Villa / Moxiang Courtyard / Xinglong Pavilion / Yongtai Teahouse / Juxiang Tower / Huayun Pavilion / Youlin Retreat / Suxin Thatched Hall / Yuening Room / Boya Study | 沿用全书前文定法（音译加通名），替代术语表零散意译建议，保持跨章一致与宋代异域感。
姑爷 | Young Master | 丫鬟（苦荞、红豆等）与韦嬷嬷称呼陈浩宇时，直译 Son-in-law 不合英文人际称谓习惯，译作 Young Master 更自然贴合语境。
绿野浮芊 | Lush Floating Greens | 第296章叶梦得诗中提炼的新菜名，兼顾诗意画面与菜肴质感。
忘忧草 / 青青色 / 江鲜绿波 | Forget-Sorrow Herb / Verdant Green / River-Fresh Green Waves | 第296章诗会拟菜名，紧扣所引唐诗与《诗经》意象。
茶百戏 | chabaixi / tea-foam painting | 宋代特有文化专有项，首现释义并保留拼音。
睡圣散 | Sleep-Sage Powder | 宋代常见迷药名，保留直译异域感。
监渡官 | Ferry Superintendent | 宋代地方渡口官职，功能对等且无西化色彩。
居养院、安济坊、漏泽园 | Juyang Yuan, Anji Fang, and Louze Yuan | 宋代标志性官办慈善机构，保留拼音音译符合异域保留原则。
捶丸 | chuiwan | 宋代球类运动专有名词，保留拼音不西化作 golf。
审校结论 | 全39章（Ch 202 及 Ch 273–310）逐场景全量重译终稿一次性交付完毕，每章均保持 0.55–0.85 英文段数比与完整对白细节；权威诗词已按核验名家译本标明 [TN:]（许渊冲、理雅各、Tony Barnstone & Chou Ping、Ronald Egan 等），自译均标 [TN: Translator’s rendering]；内部静默修正多处原文笔误（202章“淑贞”→Shuzhen、282–283章“春金兰/春金禅”→Chun Jinchan、284章“他”→her、294章“强调”→tone、295章“二婶”→Third Aunt、298章“他”→she / “才稀罕”→couldn’t care less、299章“韦嬷嬷”→Matron Zou、304–305章“坎来”→砍来、309章“她的妻室”→his wife 等），零 VERB+-ly 弱副词、零代码围栏、零段落编号、零重复章头。
